The literature's discontinuous peridynamic operator assuming fracture from tensile strain only is not mathematically well-posed, motivating the study of a regularized version. Fully including the practical one-point quadrature in the convergence analysis is important for the scheme used in practice.

Method: Convergence analysis of a mesh-free discretization of a regularized dynamic non-linear bond-based peridynamic model with irreversible damage via a bond-breakage criterion, explicitly incorporating the one-point quadrature. The continuous solution's spatial regularity is then analyzed, deriving local Lipschitz bounds for displacement for locally L-Lipschitz input data, regardless of damage, for fixed horizon δ>0.

Result: The scheme converges with respect to the natural norm of C^1([0,T],L^∞(Ω)^d), hence L^∞(Ω)^d in space. The required spatial regularity is heavily constrained; for locally L-Lipschitz data, local Lipschitz bounds on displacement are derived independent of damage.

Conclusion: The result is consistent with interpreting the peridynamic model as an approximation to a truly discontinuous fracture model with length-scale given by the horizon δ>0. The limit as δ→0 is not treated.

Abstract: We show the convergence of a commonly used mesh-free discretization of a dynamic non-linear bond-based peridynamic model with irreversible damage implemented via a bond-breakage criterion for a fixed horizon $δ>0$. The model chosen here is a mathematically well-posed regularization of a discontinuous peridynamic operator often found in the literature, which assumes that fracture results from strain in tension only. We explicitly include the one-point quadrature used in this scheme in the analysis. The convergence is with respect to the natural norm of the solution space given by $C^1([0,T],L^\infty(Ω)^d)$, and therefore $L^\infty(Ω)^d$ in the spatial coordinates. This result heavily constrains the spatial regularity of the continuous solution which we prove afterwards. More specifically, for locally $L$-Lipschitz continuous input data, local Lipschitz bounds are derived for the displacement, regardless of damage. This is consistent with interpreting the peridynamic model used here as an approximation to a truly discontinuous fracture model with a length-scale parameter given by the horizon $δ>0$. We note that we do not deal with the limit of the solutions as $δ\searrow 0$.

</details>


### [28] [Global Optimization of Beam Profiles for Suppressing the Bump-On-Tail Instability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13391)
*Sandro Mignelli,Lukas Einkemmer*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: Beam modulation can suppress the bump-on-tail instability, and population-based optimization algorithms find ON/OFF beam profiles that cut the instability by over 80%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: A constant-intensity particle beam propagating into a plasma excites a bump-on-tail instability. Modulating the beam can reduce this instability, but finding optimal beam profiles is a hard, high-dimensional optimization problem with a rugged landscape, and simple ON/OFF profiles—though experimentally convenient—make it even more challenging.

Method: The authors evaluate several optimization algorithms on this problem and identify population-based methods, especially CMA-ES and Differential Evolution, as the best performers. They specifically optimize binary ON/OFF beam profiles, which are higher-dimensional and harder to optimize but easier to realize experimentally.

Result: The population-based algorithms yield ON/OFF beam profiles that reduce the instability by more than 80%, substantially outperforming results reported previously in the literature.

Conclusion: Population-based optimization, particularly CMA-ES and Differential Evolution, is effective for designing experimentally feasible ON/OFF beam profiles that strongly suppress the bump-on-tail instability.

Abstract: A particle beam with constant intensity that propagates into a plasma triggers a bump-on-tail instability. It has been demonstrated that beam modulation can be used to significantly reduce this instability. However, finding the optimal beam profiles that accomplish this is a complex optimization problem that suffers from an extremely rugged optimization landscape and a relatively high-dimensional parameter space. In this paper, we evaluate a number of optimization algorithms and find that population based methods (in particular, CMA-ES and Differential Evolution) perform best. In particular, we investigate beam profiles where the beam is either completely turned ON or OFF. This configuration is easier to accomplish experimentally, but leads to a higher-dimensional and thus more challenging optimization problem. We obtain ON/OFF beam profiles that reduce the instability by more than 80% and thus perform significantly better than what has been reported in the literature.

</details>


### [29] [Regularisation and Parameter Choice Rules for Composite Linear Inverse Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13483)
*Christina Brandt,Patrick Horn*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: A novel regularization strategy for structured linear inverse problems of the form Ax = F∘G x = z, combining simpler regularizations for F and G separately, with a-priori and a-posteriori parameter choice rules.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical parameter choice rules fail when no source conditions are known because intermediate error bounds may be unavailable. Also, using operator A directly or a complex scheme involving both F and G simultaneously is computationally difficult.

Method: Instead of using operator A or a complex scheme involving F and G simultaneously, the paper combines already existing and simpler regularizations for F and G separately, enabling use of established and optimized programs for high-speed computations. Both a-priori and a-posteriori parameter choice rules are provided with a convergence speed analysis given source conditions.

Result: The method is tested on the pre-clinical medical imaging technique of magnetic particle imaging with different choices of regularization, and on a convolution problem for an analysis of the convergence speed.

Conclusion: The proposed strategy enables efficient high-speed computation by leveraging simple regularizations for F and G, and provides valid parameter choice rules even without known source conditions, demonstrated on magnetic particle imaging and a convolution problem.

Abstract: In this paper we propose a novel regularisation strategy for structured linear inverse problems of the form $Ax=F \circ G x = z$. Instead of using the operator $A$ or a complex scheme involving both $F$ and $G$ simultaneously, combining already existing and simpler regularisations for $F$ and $G$ allows the use of established and optimised programs for high-speed computations. As classical parameter choice rules fail in the scenario when no source conditions are known because intermediate error bounds may be unavailable, we provide both a-priori and a-posteriori rules with a convergence speed analysis given source conditions. We test the proposed method on the pre-clinical medical imaging technique of magnetic particle imaging with different choices of regularisation and on a convolution problem for an analysis of the convergence speed.

</details>


### [30] [On a maximum principle in discretization with spherical harmonics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13540)
*Arnold Reusken*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: Sharp estimates are derived for the largest radius $r_N^{max}$ within which the discrete spherical-harmonic solution $u_N$ of the Laplace equation on the $d$-dimensional unit sphere still satisfies the maximum principle, with explicit bounds for $d=3$ and general $d$, plus sharpness analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Spherical harmonics are a classical tool for solving the Laplace equation on the unit sphere, and the true solution obeys the fundamental maximum principle. However, when boundary data are approximated in the finite-dimensional space of spherical harmonics of degree at most $N$, the resulting discrete solution $u_N$ generally loses this maximum principle. The paper aims to quantify how far from the boundary the maximum principle remains valid for $u_N$.

Method: The authors analyze the discrete solution $u_N$ obtained by projecting boundary data onto the space spanned by all spherical harmonics of degree at most $N$, for general dimension $d \geq 2$. They derive sharp estimates on the radius $r_N^{max}$ such that the maximum principle holds for $u_N(x)$ for all $\|x\| \leq r_N^{max}$.

Result: For $d=3$ they prove $r_N^{max} \gtrsim 1 - 3N^{-1}\ln(2^{1/3}N)$, with analogous estimates for $d \neq 3$. The sharpness of these estimates is also investigated.

Conclusion: The maximum principle for the truncated spherical-harmonic solution $u_N$ is shown to hold up to a radius approaching 1 as $N$ grows, with the deviation from 1 decaying like $N^{-1}\ln N$ in dimension three; these bounds are shown to be essentially sharp.

Abstract: Spherical harmonics have been studied extensively and are used in many fields of application. We consider the probably earliest application of spherical harmonics, namely for a representation of the solution of the Laplace equation on the $d$-dimensional unit sphere where the boundary data are represented in a spherical harmonics basis. We consider general $d \geq 2$. For the solution $u$ of the Laplace equation the fundamental maximum principle holds. If one approximates the boundary data in the finite dimensional space spanned by all spherical harmonics of degree at most $N$, the resulting discrete solution, denoted by $u_N$, does in general not satisfy the maximum principle. In this paper we derive sharp estimates for $r_N^{\rm max}$ such that the maximum principle holds for $u_N(x)$ for all $x \in \Bbb{R}^d$ with $\|x\| \leq r_N^{\rm max}$. We show that for $d=3$ one has $r_N^{\rm max} \gtrsim 1- 3 N^{-1} \ln\big( 2^\frac13 N \big) $. Similar estimates are derived for $d \neq 3$. We also study the sharpness of these estimates.

</details>


### [31] [Preconditioning proximal Galerkin: mesh and degree robust solvers for variational inequalities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13591)
*Ioannis P. A. Papadopoulos,Pablo D. Brubeck*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: A robust, scalable block preconditioner enabling iterative solvers for the proximal Galerkin algorithm applied to variational inequalities in large 3D simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Proximal Galerkin is structure-preserving with discretization-independent nonlinear iteration counts, but in large 3D simulations the arising Newton saddle point systems cannot be solved by direct methods. An iterative linear solver with an effective preconditioner is needed.

Method: A block preconditioner is proposed that targets the primal Schur complement (rather than the usual dual Schur complement on the latent variable), requiring two approximate inverses of the latent block and one of the primal Schur complement. The latent block (algebraic equation) becomes cheaply invertible after a small perturbation. Two approximations for the primal Schur complement are introduced: PDE-operator-based and algebraic, each efficiently inverted by geometric multigrid. Standard relaxation methods are used in the block preconditioner route, with specialized relaxation and prolongation for Signorini problems.

Result: Robust iteration counts with respect to mesh size and discretization degree. Effective for nearly-incompressible elastic materials (Signorini). Linear solves are parameter-robust in 2D with mild growth in 3D. Enables solving a 3D contact problem with Poisson's ratio 0.499 and over ten million degrees of freedom.

Conclusion: The primal-Schur-complement-based block preconditioner, combined with multigrid-inverted approximations and specialized relaxation, delivers a scalable and robust solver for proximal Galerkin applied to obstacle, Signorini, and strain-constrained elasticity problems, scaling to large 3D problems.

Abstract: The proximal Galerkin algorithm is a structure-preserving solver for variational inequalities that results in discretization-independent nonlinear iteration counts. However, in large three-dimensional simulations, the arising Newton saddle point systems cannot be feasibly solved by direct methods. To leverage an iterative linear solver, we propose a robust and scalable block preconditioner. Instead of using the usual dual Schur complement that acts on the latent variable, we design a preconditioner for the primal Schur complement. This leads to a block preconditioner that requires two approximate inverses of the latent block and only one of the primal Schur complement. The latent block represents an algebraic equation which, after a small perturbation, can be cheaply inverted. We then introduce two choices of approximations for the primal Schur complement: a PDE-operator-based and an algebraic approach. Either approximation can be efficiently inverted by geometric multigrid.
  We investigate the effectiveness of these strategies when proximal Galerkin is applied to obstacle, Signorini, and strain-constrained elasticity problems and also compare with a monolithic multigrid approach. Choices of standard relaxation methods, in the block preconditioner route, lead to robust iteration counts with respect to the mesh size and discretization degree. Moreover, in the context of the Signorini, specialized relaxation and prolongation also result in effective solvers for nearly-incompressible elastic materials. The linear solves are parameter-robust in 2D, with only mild growth in 3D. This allows for the solution of a three-dimensional contact problem, with a Poisson's ratio of 0.499, discretized with over ten million degrees of freedom.

</details>


### [32] [Energy conserving compatible space--time FEM for deterministic and stochastic rotating shallow water](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13624)
*Maneesh Kumar Singh,Colin J. Cotter*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper introduces a fully discrete, arbitrary-order energy-conserving discretization of the rotating shallow water equations, using compatible space-time finite elements, projected auxiliary variables for exact energy conservation, and a monolithic patch-smoother iterative solver, extended to Stochastic Forcing by Lie Transport.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Exact energy conservation in numerical discretizations of the rotating shallow water equations is challenging, especially when achieving arbitrary order accuracy in space and time and extending to stochastic formulations.

Method: A space-time finite element discretization combining compatible (mixed) finite element methods in space and continuous Petrov-Galerkin schemes in time. Projected auxiliary variables are used for Hamiltonian derivatives to ensure exact energy conservation. An iterative solver based on monolithic patch smoothers converges a Newton method sufficiently for very small solver-induced energy errors. Extended to the energy-conserving Stochastic Forcing by Lie Transport (SFLT) formulation.

Result: Numerical tests demonstrate convergence, conservation, and robustness of the proposed scheme.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides a robust, arbitrary-order, fully discrete energy-conserving discretization for the rotating shallow water equations, extendable to stochastic variants.

Abstract: We present a fully discrete energy conserving discretisation of the rotating shallow water equations with arbitrary order in space and time. A space time finite element discretisation is used, built from compatible finite element methods in space and continuous Petrov-Galerkin schemes in time. Projected auxiliary variables for Hamiltonian derivatives are necessary for exact energy conservation. We provide an iterative solver approach based on monolithic patch smoothers, which allows us to efficiently converge a Newton method sufficiently that energy errors due to the solver are very small. We extend this construction to the energy conserving Stochastic Forcing by Lie Transport formulation. Then we demonstrate the convergence, conservation and robustness in numerical tests.

</details>


### [33] [Analysis of an inexact Schwarz domain decomposition method in 3D](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13793)
*Nibedita Ghosh*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: An inexact 3D Schwarz domain decomposition method for a Laplace problem on two overlapping spheres, where subproblems are solved by projecting boundary data onto spherical harmonics of degree ≤ N, analyzed via a new maximum principle variant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The problem arises from the ddCOSMO solver used for implicit solvation in computational chemistry, requiring an efficient and analyzable domain decomposition approach for Laplace problems on overlapping spherical domains.

Method: An inexact Schwarz domain decomposition method in 3D for a Laplace problem on the union of two overlapping spheres. Each subproblem is solved approximately by L²-orthogonally projecting its boundary data onto the space of spherical harmonics of degree at most N. The analysis establishes a new variant of the maximum principle.

Result: A new variant of the maximum principle is established, enabling the analysis of the Schwarz-spherical domain decomposition method.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides a rigorous convergence analysis for the inexact Schwarz domain decomposition method relevant to the ddCOSMO solver.

Abstract: We consider an inexact version of the Schwarz domain decomposition method in $3$D to solve a Laplace problem in a domain that is the union of two overlapping spheres. We solve each subproblem approximately by projecting its boundary data $L^2$-orthogonally onto the space of spherical harmonics of degree at most $N$. The model problem arises from the ddCOSMO solver used for implicit solvation in computational chemistry. We analyze the Schwarz-spherical domain decomposition method by establishing a new variant of the maximum principle.

</details>


### [34] [Fast and Stable Arbitrary Precision Approximate Matrix Multiplication](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13905)
*Ioana Dumitriu,Olga Holtz,Abraham Hsu,Nicholas Liskij,Yoav Moran,Ryan Schneider,Oded Schwartz,Shuncheng Yuan*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper advances the theory and practice of Arbitrary Precision Approximate (APA) matrix multiplication, introducing a search algorithm for generating APA routines, reducing addition counts via alternative bases, and proving numerical stability for a Bini algorithm.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: APA algorithms can have lower asymptotic cost than many fast exact matrix multiplication schemes, yet despite existing for nearly half a century, their numerical properties remain poorly understood and understudied.

Method: A new search algorithm automatically generates APA encoding matrices with functional entries; combined with alternative bases to minimize additions and improve the leading coefficient; stability analysis of Bini's modified APA scheme for exact products.

Result: The search algorithm matches or beats best known APA algorithms for several sizes, improving the upper bound on the border rank of ⟨2,4,5⟩ matrix multiplication; addition counts are reduced, improving the leading coefficient; the same stability as fast exact matrix multiplication is proven for Bini's algorithm.

Conclusion: The work illuminates APA algorithms by providing practical construction tools, cost improvements, and stability guarantees, closing gaps in understanding of their numerical properties.

Abstract: We present several new and surprising results on the theory and practice of Arbitrary Precision Approximate (APA) matrix multiplication, which refers to algorithms that output merely approximations to matrix multiplication even in exact arithmetic. While APA algorithms have been around for nearly half a century, and despite the fact that they have potentially lower (asymptotic) cost than many fast exact matrix multiplication schemes, they have not been studied as closely, to the extent that relatively little is known about their numerical properties. In this paper, we shine a spotlight on these algorithms by presenting three important results. First, we provide a new search algorithm for APA routines that is easy to use, automatically generates encoding matrices with functional entries, and can match or beat the best known APA algorithms for several sizes. In particular, we improve the upper bound on the border rank of $\langle 2,4,5 \rangle$ matrix multiplication. Second, we combine this insight with alternative bases to minimize the number of additions in an APA algorithm, thereby improving its leading coefficient. Finally, we prove the same kind of stability that characterizes fast exact matrix multiplication for an algorithm of Bini [Calcolo 1980] that computes an exact product of two matrices using a modified APA scheme (with or without alternative bases).

</details>

Abstract: We give a reduction from a smooth-domain theorem excluding interior critical points of first positive Neumann eigenfunctions to a non-strict hot-spots conclusion on arbitrary bounded simply connected planar domains with compact Neumann resolvent and simple first positive eigenvalue. The main analytic step is spectral convergence under exhaustion by conformal images of concentric disks. A reflection extension, conformal invariance of Dirichlet energy, and compactness of the Sobolev embedding give a direct proof. A truncation argument justifies square integrability of the transferred extensions without any boundary regularity. The smooth-domain theorem proved by OpenAI is an external input; the approximation argument is proved here in full. The paper was generated by ChatGPT with prompts from the listed author.

### [36] [A Framework for Arbitrary-Order Electromagnetic Field Approximation Based on Analytical Solutions of Maxwell's Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12682)
*David Wei Ge*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper introduces a Time-Space Theorem linking higher-order temporal derivatives to higher-order spatial curl operators, enabling an arbitrary-order FDTD framework for electromagnetic computation, with errors decreasing monotonically as order increases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The standard Yee FDTD algorithm is limited to replacing first-order temporal derivatives with spatial curls, restricting it to low-order accuracy. A general framework for arbitrary-order electromagnetic approximations was needed to achieve high-accuracy computation.

Method: A Time-Space Theorem is established connecting higher-order temporal derivatives to successively higher-order spatial curl operators. This yields an arbitrary-order framework allowing different spatial discretizations and curl-estimation techniques. One implementation is an arbitrary-order FDTD using staggered time-space stepping with recursive curl estimation.

Result: Simulations of an initial-value problem with fixed step sizes for temporal orders from second to tenth show errors decreasing rapidly and monotonically with increasing approximation order, matching analytical solutions.

Conclusion: The framework shows practical potential for high-accuracy electromagnetic computation and provides a basis for further development of arbitrary-order numerical methods.

Abstract: The standard Yee finite-difference time-domain (FDTD) algorithm uses Maxwell's equations to replace first order temporal derivatives with spatial curls. In this work, the relation is extended to arbitrary orders: a Time-Space Theorem establishes the correspondence between higher-order temporal derivatives and successively higher-order spatial curl operators. Based on this relation, an arbitrary-order framework for electromagnetic field approximations is developed. The framework constructs higher temporal orders through higher curl orders and permits different spatial discretizations and curl-estimation techniques, thereby providing a general basis for developing numerical algorithms of arbitrary orders. As one implementation, an arbitrary-order FDTD algorithm is constructed using staggered time and space stepping together with recursive curl estimations. Numerical simulations of an initial-value problem are performed for temporal approximation orders from second to tenth order while the spatial and temporal step sizes are kept fixed. Comparisons with the analytical solution show that the errors decrease rapidly and monotonically with increasing approximation order. These results demonstrate the practical potential of the framework for high-accuracy electromagnetic computation and provide a basis for further development of arbitrary-order numerical methods.

</details>


### [37] [An exact Delaunay flux identity for compact equilibria in the liquid drop model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12689)
*Averkios Averkiou,Manuel del Pino,Monica Musso*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper resolves the solvability coefficient in a Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction for compact liquid drop equilibria built from Delaunay unduloids, proving its conjectured positivity and extending the construction to larger masses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work (Arch. Ration. Mech. Anal. 249 (2025)) constructed compact liquid drop equilibria from Delaunay unduloids, but left a coefficient in the final solvability condition of the Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction uncomputed. Its positivity was conjectured but unproven, blocking a complete understanding of the construction's validity.

Method: The authors evaluate the coefficient exactly, identifying it as the product of the axial period and the normalized Delaunay flux. They use this identity together with the monotonicity of the period to prove the coefficient's positivity, and further analyze the volume and surface area per period via an exact relation and neck-radius monotonicity.

Result: The conjectured positivity of the coefficient is established, and its product with the period strictly increases with the neck radius. An exact volume-surface area relation for one period is derived, with both quantities strictly decreasing in the neck radius. The construction is extended across the entire embedded unduloid family, producing compact equilibria for every sufficiently large mass, with reference neck radii converging to any prescribed interior value as mass tends to infinity.

Conclusion: The work closes the gap in the earlier unduloid-based construction, confirming the conjectured positivity and broadening the existence result to all sufficiently large masses with controlled neck-radius asymptotics.

Abstract: We revisit the construction of compact liquid drop equilibria from Delaunay unduloids in [Arch. Ration. Mech. Anal. 249 (2025), Paper No. 74]. We evaluate exactly the coefficient arising in the final solvability condition of the Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction, identifying it as the product of the axial period and the normalised Delaunay flux. Using this identity and the monotonicity of the period, we establish the conjectured positivity of the coefficient and prove that its product with the period strictly increases with the neck radius. We also derive an exact relation between the volume and the surface area of one period and prove that both strictly decrease with the neck radius. These results extend the construction throughout the embedded unduloid family and yield compact equilibria of every sufficiently large mass, with reference neck radii converging to any prescribed interior value as the mass tends to infinity.

</details>


### [38] [Stability threshold of the Couette flow in 2D Navier-Stokes Boussinesq equations on $\mathbb{T} \times [-1, 1]$ and $\mathbb{R} \times [-1, 1]$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12810)
*Nangao Zhang,Gengwei Zhong,Ruizhao Zi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the transition threshold problem for the 2D Navier-Stokes Boussinesq equations around Couette flow with constant background temperature in T×[-1,1] and R×[-1,1], proving global stability for small perturbations and deriving dissipation and damping estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The transition threshold problem for the 2D Navier-Stokes Boussinesq equations around Couette flow has not been fully understood, particularly with the added coupling between velocity and temperature fields. This coupling introduces analytical difficulties that motivate the study.

Method: The authors use a quasi-linear method combined with energy estimates to handle the coupled system and overcome the difficulties posed by the coupling in the equations.

Result: It is proved that if the initial perturbations of vorticity and temperature in Sobolev spaces have sizes ν^(1/3) and ν^(5/6) respectively, the system admits a globally stable solution. Enhanced dissipation estimates for vorticity and temperature, and inviscid damping estimate for velocity, are also derived.

Conclusion: The transition threshold for the 2D Navier-Stokes Boussinesq equations around Couette flow is established, with the coupling challenges overcome via quasi-linear methods and energy estimates, yielding stability and decay estimates in two domains.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the transition threshold problem for the 2D Navier-Stokes Boussinesq equations around the Couette flow with a constant background temperature in two domains: $\mathbb{T} \times [-1, 1]$ and $\mathbb{R} \times [-1, 1]$.
  It was proved that if the sizes of initial perturbations of the vorticity and the temperature in Sobolev spaces are $ν^{\fr13}$ and $ν^{\fr56}$, respectively, then this system admits a globally stable solution.
  Besides, we derive the enhanced dissipation estimate for the vorticity and temperature, as well as the inviscid damping estimate for the velocity. The coupling in the equations poses some difficulties to the analysis. We overcome these challenges and establish the results via the quasi-linear method and energy estimates.

</details>


### [39] [Precise propagation rates of the fractional Fisher-KPP equation with free boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12838)
*Huyuan Chen,Yihong Du,Wenjie Ni*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper determines the exact propagation rates of the fractional Fisher-KPP equation with free boundaries, showing the rate depends on the fractional exponent s: polynomial for s<1/2, t·ln t for s=1/2, and linear for s>1/2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: A previous work (\cite{CDN26}) modeled species spreading with the fractional Fisher-KPP equation with free boundaries, proving well-posedness and a spreading-vanishing dichotomy. However, the precise spreading speeds (propagation rates) were left undetermined, motivating this paper to quantify them.

Method: The authors use delicate upper and lower solutions. For s in (0,1/2], they construct these from a smooth profile function; for s in (1/2,1), they use the semi-wave profile. The asymptotic rates of the free boundaries h(t) and g(t) are then derived via comparison.

Result: When spreading occurs, the free boundaries and level sets of u satisfy: for s in (0,1/2), h(t)/t^{1/(2s)} and -g(t)/t^{1/(2s)} converge to (2^{1-2s}μ/(1-2s))^{1/(2s)}; for s=1/2, they converge to μ at rate t·ln t; for s in (1/2,1), they converge to c_0(s) at a linear rate, where c_0(s) in (0,∞) is uniquely determined by the associated semi-wave problem.

Conclusion: The propagation rate of the fractional Fisher-KPP model depends qualitatively on the fractional exponent: sublinear-polynomial, logarithmic-type, and linear regimes for s<1/2, s=1/2, and s>1/2 respectively. The level sets of the density u propagate at the same rates as the free boundaries.

Abstract: In this paper, we determine the propagation rates of the Fisher-KPP equation with free boundaries and fractional diffusion $(-Δ)^s$ $(0<s<1)$, which was considered in \cite{CDN26} as a model for species spreading, where the density function $u(t,x)$ satisfies the fractional Fisher-KPP equation $u_t+(-Δ)^su=f(u)$ over a one-dimensional spatial domain $(g(t), h(t))$, representing the expanding population range, with $x=g(t)$ and $x=h(t)$ the free boundaries. It was shown in \cite{CDN26} that the model is well-posed, and its long-time dynamics exhibits a spreading-vanishing dichotomy. We show in this paper that, when spreading occurs,
  \[ \begin{cases} \lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{h(t)}{t^{1/(2s)}} =\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{-g(t)}{t^{1/(2s)}} =\left(\frac{2^{1-2s}μ}{1-2s}\right)^{1/(2s)} & \mbox{ if $s\in(0,1/2)$,}\\[2mm] \lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{h(t)}{t\ln t} =\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{-g(t)}{t\ln t} =μ& \mbox{ if $s=1/2$,}\\[2mm] \lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{h(t)}{t} =\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{-g(t)}{t} =c_0(s) & \mbox{ if $s\in(1/2,1)$}, \end{cases} \] where $c_0(s)\in (0,\infty)$ is uniquely determined by the associated semi-wave problem. The level sets of $u(t,x)$ propagate with the same rates as the free boundaries. The proof uses delicate upper and lower solutions constructed from a smooth profile function in the case $s\in (0, 1/2]$, and from the semi-wave profile when $s\in (1/2, 1)$.

</details>


### [40] [Global Well-Posedness and Energy Decay for an Abstract Viscoelastic Wave Equation with Nonlocal or Friction Damping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12846)
*Jun Zhou,Teng Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies an abstract viscoelastic wave equation whose damping term depends on a power of the velocity norm, unifying friction (exponent zero) and nonlocal (nonzero exponent) damping alongside viscoelastic integral damping, and establishes well-posedness and explicit energy decay estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To analyze the combined effects of viscoelastic damping and nonlocal damping in an abstract Hilbert-space framework, including the previously untreated case of nonlocal damping with negative exponents.

Method: Monotone operator theory combined with the Faedo--Galerkin method for well-posedness; uniqueness via monotonicity (handling the singular exponent range \(-1\) to \(0\) without Lipschitz continuity); long-time behavior via integral inequalities, multiplier techniques, and Nakao's method.

Result: Global well-posedness for initial data in \(\mathcal{H}_1\); uniqueness for all exponents greater than \(-1\); explicit decay estimates showing exponential energy decay in the friction case and polynomial decay in the nonlocal case.

Conclusion: The work is the first to consider combined viscoelastic and nonlocal damping in such an abstract framework and the first to analyze nonlocal damping with negative exponents.

Abstract: This paper investigates an abstract viscoelastic wave equation in a separable Hilbert space, in which the damping term depends on a power of the velocity norm. Depending on whether the power exponent is zero or nonzero, this damping corresponds to friction damping or nonlocal damping, respectively; the equation also contains a viscoelastic damping represented by an integral term with a relaxation function. We establish global well-posedness for initial data in \(\mathcal{H}_1\) by combining monotone operator theory with the Faedo--Galerkin method, and prove uniqueness for all exponents greater than \(-1\), with the singular range between \(-1\) and \(0\) handled through the monotonicity rather than the Lipschitz continuity of the nonlocal damping operator. We then study the long-time behavior of the energy via integral inequalities, multiplier techniques, and Nakao's method, and obtain explicit decay estimates showing that the energy decays exponentially in the friction case and polynomially in the nonlocal case. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first work to consider the combined effects of viscoelastic damping and nonlocal damping in such an abstract framework, and it is also the first to analyze nonlocal damping with negative exponents.

</details>


### [41] [Localized Generative Design of Ship Hulls with Diffusion Models and High-Fidelity Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12870)
*Hadi Keramati,Mohammed A. Hannan,Patrick Kirchen,Sina Tousi,Joonas Riekkinen,Jim Pyra,Rajeev K. Jaiman*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper introduces a reward-directed diffusion framework for localized hull optimization, evaluated via high-fidelity CFD, revealing limits of the surrogate objective and representation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Practical ship design needs localized geometric refinement and reliable performance evaluation, which generic generative models and low-fidelity screening cannot fully provide.

Method: Parametric masking restricts changes to selected variables (bulbous bow, forebody) preserving hull continuity; surrogate-assisted sequential Monte Carlo sampling minimizes predicted calm-water resistance, with stability kept via training data and displacement matched during screening. An automated viscous free-surface CFD pipeline (validated on two container ships) evaluates a generated variant and GenHull E.

Result: At matched KCS displacement, the generated variant has comparable total resistance at Fn=0.195 but 45-50% higher at Fn=0.250 and 0.260, missed by empirical screening. GenHull E reaches 6-8% lower resistance up to Fn=0.240 but higher above Fn=0.250, as friction and pressure trends reverse.

Conclusion: Aggregating resistance hides friction/pressure tradeoffs. The assessment exposes an inability to represent a faired bulbous bow and a reward lacking viscous pressure and stability terms, showing how high-fidelity assessment informs better representations and objectives for physically realizable generative designs.

Abstract: Practical ship design requires localized refinement and performance evaluation. This paper presents a reward-directed diffusion framework for localized hull optimization. Parametric masking during dataset generation restricts geometric changes to selected variables, including the bulbous bow and forebody, while preserving full-hull continuity. Surrogate-assisted sequential Monte Carlo sampling targets reduced predicted calm-water resistance, with stability retained through training data and displacement matched during screening. An automated viscous free-surface CFD pipeline, validated against measurements for two benchmark container ships, evaluates a generated variant and GenHull E, a hull refined by a naval architect within the same representation. At matched KRISO Container Ship (KCS) displacement, the generated variant has comparable total resistance at $F_n=0.195$ but 45-50% higher resistance at $F_n=0.250$ and $0.260$, missed by empirical screening. GenHull E achieves 6-8% lower total resistance up to $F_n=0.240$ but higher resistance above $F_n=0.250$ as opposing friction and pressure trends reverse the gains. Aggregating resistance across this crossover can conceal tradeoffs that emerge when friction and pressure components are evaluated separately. The assessment reveals an inability to represent a faired bulbous bow and a reward lacking viscous pressure and stability terms. These findings show how high-fidelity assessment informs improved representations and objectives for physically realizable generative designs.

</details>


### [42] [Fourier--Laplace Injectivity for Oblique and Polygonal Cylinders in Passive Inverse Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12983)
*Yan Jiang,Hongyu Liu,Tianhao Ni,Kai Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies Fourier–Laplace transforms on null (characteristic) phases for passive inverse problems and uses them to recover initial data for wave and heat equations from boundary data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Passive inverse problems seek to determine unknown interior quantities from boundary observations; the work aims to establish injectivity and stability (Lipschitz/Hölder) of such recovery using null-phase transforms, including the case of limited-time weighted boundary data.

Method: Fourier–Laplace transforms on null phases are analyzed. For codimension-one densities in oblique cylinders, integration along the distinguished direction yields an entire transform on a real-analytic maximally totally real submanifold, giving injectivity via holomorphic uniqueness. For polygonal cylinders with densities polynomial in transverse variables, a weighted Brion–Barvinok expansion separates vertex and piecewise contributions. These are applied to acoustic and parabolic models; continuous curves/densities are approximated by polygonal and piecewise-polynomial versions.

Result: Equality of Laplace-domain boundary data determines (initial data)/(speed^2) in the acoustic model and (initial data)/(thermal conductivity) in the parabolic model. With weighted generalized boundary data on [0,T], the harmonic moments of the quotient are determined up to a weighted terminal residual, yielding a Hölder estimate in oblique cylinders and a Lipschitz estimate in polygonal cylinders. The reconstruction estimate separates measurement noise, density approximation error, geometric error, and sampling-matrix condition number.

Conclusion: Null-phase Fourier–Laplace transforms provide injectivity and quantitative stability for passive wave and heat inverse problems, with explicit error decomposition for practical finite-sampling reconstructions.

Abstract: We study Fourier--Laplace transforms on null phases arising in passive inverse problems. Densities with codimension-one dependence in oblique cylinders are first considered. Integration in the distinguished direction reduces the data to an entire transform on a real-analytic maximally totally real submanifold. Holomorphic uniqueness then gives injectivity. A fixed transverse polytope swept along a polygonal curve, forming a polygonal cylinder, is next considered. The density is assumed to be polynomial in the material transverse variables on each swept piece. A weighted Brion--Barvinok expansion separates the vertex terms and the successive pieces. The same representation gives a finite-dimensional Lipschitz estimate when the polynomial degree is fixed.
  We apply these transform results to passive wave and heat equations in arbitrary dimension. The initial state is zero outside the observation domain. Equality of the Laplace-domain boundary data determines the quantity of the initial data divided by the speed squared in the acoustic model and the quantity of the initial data divided by the coefficient of thermal conductivity in the parabolic model. The question of what remains true when only weighted generalized boundary data on a finite interval \([0,T]\) are available is then studied. These data determine the harmonic moments of the quotient up to the weighted terminal residual. This identity gives a Hölder estimate in an oblique cylinder and a Lipschitz estimate in a polygonal cylinder. Finally, a continuous generating curve is approximated by a polygonal curve and a continuous material density by piecewise transverse polynomials. The resulting reconstruction estimate separates measurement noise, density approximation error, geometric error, and the condition number of the finite sampling matrix.

</details>


### [43] [Sharp Sobolev inequalities on minimal immersions via optimal transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13052)
*Zhe-Feng Xu,Zhuo-Nan Zhu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The optimal L^p-Sobolev constant on any complete n-dimensional Euclidean minimal immersion (without boundary) equals the Euclidean Aubin-Talenti constant for 2 ≤ p < n.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establishes rigidity/optimality of the Euclidean Sobolev constant in the setting of minimal immersions, extending the classical Aubin-Talenti result.

Method: Combines optimal transport to a suitably chosen target manifold with a two-kernel comparison that relates the tangential transport moment to deficits in the normal-fibre and Jacobian estimates.

Result: For 2 ≤ p < n, the optimal L^p-Sobolev constant on every complete n-dimensional Euclidean minimal immersion without boundary is the Euclidean Aubin-Talenti constant.

Conclusion: The Euclidean Aubin-Talenti constant is optimal on minimal immersions, with the proof hinging on an optimal transport plus two-kernel comparison argument.

Abstract: We prove that, for $2\leq p<n$, the optimal $L^p$-Sobolev constant on every complete $n$-dimensional Euclidean minimal immersion without boundary is the Euclidean Aubin--Talenti constant. The proof combines optimal transport to a suitably chosen target with a two-kernel comparison linking the tangential transport moment to deficits in the normal-fibre and Jacobian estimates.

</details>


### [44] [Qualitative-to-quantitative regularity for degenerate equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13137)
*Aelson Sobral,Eduardo V. Teixeira,José Miguel Urbano*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Qualitative regularity implies uniform quantitative interior estimates via compactness and improvement-of-flatness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bridges the gap between qualitative and quantitative regularity, showing optimal qualitative and quantitative gradient Hölder exponents coincide for degenerate equations.

Method: Compactness for shifted equations combined with an improvement-of-flatness iteration at variable scales.

Result: For p-Laplace (p>2), local C^{1,γ} regularity implies uniform C^{1,β} estimates for all β<γ; planar case gives control via L^p norm below the Iwaniec-Manfredi threshold; yields uniform C^{1,β} for planar infinity-harmonic functions (β<1/3); for nonlocal superconductivity |Du|(-Δ)^s u=0, pointwise differentiability implies uniform C^{1,α} estimates.

Conclusion: A general principle converts qualitative regularity into uniform interior estimates for degenerate equations, with applications to p-Laplace and nonlocal superconductivity equations.

Abstract: We establish a principle that converts qualitative regularity into uniform interior estimates for degenerate equations. For the $p$-Laplace equation, $p>2$, we prove that local $C^{1,γ}$ regularity of every solution, without any uniform bound on the corresponding seminorms, implies uniform $C^{1,β}$ estimates for every $0<β<γ$. Thus, the optimal qualitative and quantitative gradient Hölder exponents coincide as suprema in every dimension. In the plane, this yields estimates controlled by the $L^p$ norm of the solution for every exponent below the sharp Iwaniec--Manfredi threshold. Applied to Xu's recent qualitative $C^{1,1/3}$ regularity for planar infinity-harmonic functions, we obtain uniform interior $C^{1,β}$ estimates for every $0<β<1/3$. For the nonlocal superconductivity equation $|Du|(-Δ)^s u=0$, with $1/2<s<1$, we prove that pointwise differentiability of all solutions with integrable tails implies uniform interior $C^{1,α}$ estimates for bounded solutions, for some $α>0$. The proofs combine compactness for shifted equations with an improvement-of-flatness iteration at variable scales.

</details>


### [45] [Strict Dirichlet Pólya Inequality for Simply Connected Planar Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13139)
*Quanyu Tang,Zuoqin Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves the strict Dirichlet Pólya inequality for bounded simply connected planar domains using a unitary boundary transport formulation and Dirichlet-to-Neumann map techniques.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Dirichlet Pólya inequality is a fundamental spectral conjecture linking eigenvalue counting to domain area; establishing a strict version in full generality remains an important open problem.

Method: A unitary boundary transport formulation of the Helmholtz equation relates Dirichlet resonances to unitary eigenphase crossings via the Dirichlet-to-Neumann map. An exact area identity gives the sharp Weyl coefficient, and a Ky Fan estimate leaves a strictly positive remainder in the counting estimate. The general case follows by conformal exhaustion.

Result: The strict Dirichlet Pólya inequality is established on every bounded simply connected planar domain, with the sharp Weyl coefficient and a strictly positive remainder in the counting estimate.

Conclusion: The strict Dirichlet Pólya inequality holds for all bounded simply connected planar domains, proven through boundary transport and conformal exhaustion.

Abstract: We establish the strict Dirichlet Pólya inequality on every bounded simply connected planar domain. The proof uses a unitary boundary transport formulation of the Helmholtz equation and relates Dirichlet resonances to unitary eigenphase crossings through the Dirichlet-to-Neumann map. An exact area identity yields the sharp Weyl coefficient, while a Ky Fan estimate leaves a strictly positive remainder in the counting estimate. The general case follows by conformal exhaustion.

</details>


### [46] [Remarks on temperature-dependent Cahn--Hilliard equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13140)
*Mi-Ho Giga,Yoshikazu Giga,Naoto Kajiwara,Kazuyuki Tsuda*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper reviews derivations of temperature-dependent Cahn-Hilliard equations (mixed-order systems) and establishes local-in-time well-posedness for a subclass via L^p-maximal regularity, plus equilibrium stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Temperature-dependent Cahn-Hilliard models arise in phase separation where thermal effects matter; existing work mostly treats isothermal cases. The mixed-order nature of these systems makes standard well-posedness theory insufficient, motivating a systematic treatment.

Method: Recalls several derivations of temperature-dependent Cahn-Hilliard equations, identifies a suitable subclass of the resulting mixed-order systems, and applies L^p-maximal regularity theory to prove local-in-time well-posedness; analyzes stability of equilibria.

Result: Local-in-time well-posedness is obtained for a suitable subclass of the mixed-order Cahn-Hilliard systems. Stability of equilibria is also characterized.

Conclusion: The L^p-maximal regularity framework effectively handles the mixed-order structure of temperature-dependent Cahn-Hilliard equations, providing well-posedness and stability results.

Abstract: We recall several derivations of temperature-dependent Cahn-Hilliard equations. The resulting equations are systems of mixed order. For a suitable subclass of equations we discuss its local-in-time well-posedness based on $L^p$-maximal regularity theory. We also discuss stability of equilibria.

</details>


### [47] [Stability of composite waves consisting of a non-classical Liu shock and a rarefaction wave for a non-convex viscoelastic system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13151)
*Feimin Huang,Yi Wang,Jian Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves time-asymptotic stability of composite waves (a non-classical Liu shock plus a rarefaction wave from a different characteristic family) for a 1D non-convex viscoelastic system with cubic stress-strain relation, via the a-contraction method.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Composite-wave stability for non-convex physical systems is largely open. This work targets the intricate case where a non-classical Liu shock interacts with a rarefaction wave from a different characteristic family.

Method: Uses delicate time-dependent shift functions for the viscous shock and tailored relative-entropy weight functions, applying the a-contraction method. Handles both degenerate and non-degenerate Liu shocks; shift/weight functions differ between nearly degenerate and fully non-degenerate cases.

Result: Establishes time-asymptotic stability of the composite of a viscous Liu shock and a rarefaction wave from two different characteristic families for the 1D viscoelastic system.

Conclusion: Provides the first time-asymptotic stability result for composite waves consisting of both a non-classical Liu shock and a rarefaction wave from two different families in a non-convex physical system.

Abstract: We investigate the time-asymptotic stability of composite waves consisting of a non-classical Liu shock and a rarefaction wave for non-convex viscoelastic system. By choosing delicate time-dependent shift functions to the viscous shock and suitable weight functions for the relative entropy, we succeed in proving the time-asymptotic stability of the composite of a viscous Liu shock and a rarefaction wave from two different characteristic families for the one-dimensional viscoelastic system with a cubic non-convex stress-strain relation by $a$-contraction method. Here the non-classical Liu shock can be either degenerate or non-degenerate, which can be seen as the system version of the scalar Oleinik shock. Note that both the shift and the weight functions are chosen quite differently in the two cases of nearly degenerate and fully non-degenerate. The paper represents the first time-asymptotic stability result for the composite waves consisting of both non-classical Liu shock and rarefaction wave from two different families for the non-convex physical system.

</details>


### [48] [Minimizing tangent maps into complex projective space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13214)
*Matilde Gianocca,André Guerra,David Ziener*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper classifies nonconstant smooth harmonic maps from S² and S³ into CPⁿ whose 0-homogeneous extensions are minimizing: they are isometric embeddings (S² case) or holomorphic isometric embeddings composed with a Hopf map (S³ case).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Identifying which harmonic maps are minimizing is a central problem in geometric analysis, and the 0-homogeneous extension provides a natural setting to study minimization beyond the domain sphere itself.

Method: A classification argument analyzing smooth harmonic maps S²→CPⁿ and S³→CPⁿ subject to the condition that their 0-homogeneous extensions are minimizing.

Result: Such maps are exactly: isometric embeddings for S²→CPⁿ, and holomorphic isometric embeddings composed with a Hopf map for S³→CPⁿ.

Conclusion: A complete classification of nonconstant smooth harmonic maps with minimizing 0-homogeneous extensions from S² and S³ into CPⁿ is obtained.

Abstract: We classify all nonconstant smooth harmonic maps $\mathbb{S}^2\to \mathbb{CP}^n$ and $\mathbb{S}^3\to \mathbb{CP}^n$ whose 0-homogeneous extensions are minimizing: they are, respectively, isometric embeddings or holomorphic isometric embeddings composed with a Hopf map.

</details>


### [49] [Gradient rearrangement estimates for singular and degenerate anisotropic $p$-Laplace equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13226)
*Lu Chen,Daniel Hauer,Yabo Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global pointwise rearrangement estimates for SOLAs of anisotropic p-Laplace Poisson equations with L1 data, extending Cianchi-Maz'ya theory to the singular range and covering all 1<p<n.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing rearrangement theory (Cianchi-Maz'ya) covers the degenerate range 2≤p<n but not the singular range 1<p<2, where nonlinear interpolation methods fail. This work aims to fill that gap.

Method: Proves global pointwise estimates on decreasing rearrangements u* and [H(∇u)]* for SOLAs under Dirichlet/Neumann conditions; decomposes the data for the singular range to obtain new gradient estimates; derives weak-type gradient estimates and stability estimates for gradient differences when p_*(n)<p<n.

Result: Obtains anisotropic extension and full-interval completion for 2≤p<n, new rearrangement gradient estimates for 1<p<2, endpoint weak-type gradient estimates for all 1<p<n, and stability estimates for gradient differences for p_*(n):=(3n-2)/(2n-1) < p < n.

Conclusion: The results extend known rearrangement and stability estimates to the singular range p_*(n)<p<2, completing the anisotropic rearrangement theory across the full interval 1<p<n.

Abstract: In this paper, we prove global pointwise estimates on the decreasing rearrangement \(u^{\ast}\) and \([H(\nabla u)]^{\ast}\) of SOLAs \(u\) of an elliptic Poisson equation driven by the anisotropic \(p\)-Laplace operator \(Δ_p^H\) for given \(L^1\)-data and equipped with either homogeneous Dirichlet or Neumann boundary conditions. Here, we assume that the dimension \(n\ge 2\), \(1<p<n\) and \(H\) is norm on \(\R^n\). In the degenerate range \(2 \leq p <n\), this gives an anisotropic extension and a full-interval completion of the rearrangement theory of Cianchi-Maz'ya [JEMS, 2014]. In particular, we obtain a new rearrangement estimate for gradients by decomposing the data for singular range \(1<p<2\), where the nonlinear interpolation method aren't available. Moreover, the endpoint weak-type gradient estimate is obtained for all \(1<p<n\). In the range \(p_*(n)<p<n\) with \(p_*(n):=\frac{3n-2}{2n-1}\), we prove a stability estimate for differences of gradients. These results extends the known ones in the literature to the singular range \(p_*(n)<p<2\).

</details>


### [50] [Vector and matrix monotonicity characterisation of the "load forces" in nonlinear elasticity: compressible versus incompressible response](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13235)
*Jendrik Voss,Ionel-Dumitrel Ghiba,Robert J. Martin,Patrizio Neff*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies relations between notions of monotonicity for isotropic hyperelastic compressible and incompressible materials, defining monotonicity on the manifold of positive definite symmetric tensors with unit determinant and its spectral manifold, and characterizing monotonicity for hyperelastic materials, including the Biot stress tensor.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Different notions of monotonicity exist for isotropic hyperelastic materials, but their relations—especially on the constrained manifold of incompressible materials—require clarification.

Method: The paper defines the monotonicity concept on Sym^{++}(n) ∩ SL(n) (positive definite symmetric tensors with unit determinant) and on the corresponding spectral manifold with unit product constraint, then gives a precise characterization of monotonicity for hyperelastic materials.

Result: Monotonicity is characterized for incompressible and compressible hyperelastic materials, with specific consideration of the Biot stress tensor (engineering stress/load tensor).

Conclusion: The paper clarifies relationships among monotonicity notions and provides a precise characterization relevant to the Biot stress tensor for isotropic hyperelastic materials.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the relation between different notions of monotonicity for isotropic hyperelastic compressible and incompressible materials. We study how to define the monotonicity concept on the manifold $\rm{Sym}^{++}(n)\cap \rm{SL}(n)$ of positive definite symmetric tensors with unit determinant and the corresponding spectral manifold $\mathcal S=\left\{(λ_1,\dotsc,λ_n)\in \mathbb{R}^n_+\,\middle|\, λ_1λ_2\cdotsλ_n=1\right\}$, and give a precise characterisation of monotonicity in the case of hyperelastic materials. In particular, we consider the monotonicity properties of the Biot stress tensor $T_{\rm Biot}(U)$, the engineering stress, or the load tensor.

</details>


### [51] [Weighted derivative estimates for Kolmogorov equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13241)
*Artur Sidorenko,Vasilii Kolokoltsov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Explicit pointwise bounds are derived for spatial derivatives of the classical solution to a Cauchy problem for diffusions with smooth, at most linearly growing coefficients and bounded higher-order derivatives, with polynomially growing initial data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to establish explicit pointwise bounds on spatial derivatives of the Cauchy problem solution and on the differentiability of the SDE with respect to initial data, for diffusions with smooth coefficients under growth constraints.

Method: The proofs combine Lyapunov functions and Markov semigroups on weighted spaces together with Kunita's theory of differentiable dependence of SDE solutions on the initial data.

Result: Explicit pointwise bounds on spatial derivatives of the classical solution are obtained, and explicit growth estimates for the SDE derivatives with respect to the initial data are established.

Conclusion: The paper provides explicit derivative bounds for diffusion Cauchy problems and SDE initial-data dependence under smoothness, linear growth, and bounded higher-derivative assumptions.

Abstract: For diffusions whose coefficients are smooth, with at most linear growth and bounded derivatives of higher order, we establish explicit pointwise bounds on the spatial derivatives of the classical solution to the Cauchy problem with polynomially growing initial data. We also obtain explicit growth estimates for the derivatives of the SDE with respect to the initial data. The proofs combine Lyapunov functions and Markov semigroups on weighted spaces with Kunita's theory of the differentiable dependence of SDE solutions on the initial data.

</details>


### [52] [Global Regular Solutions for One-Dimensional Degenerate Compressible Non-Resistive MHD Equations with Large Data and Far-Field Vacuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13248)
*Zhaoyang Shang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves two global large-data existence theorems for a 1D transverse-field isentropic non-resistive MHD system with density-dependent viscosity μ(ρ)=αρ^δ (0<δ≤1) and vacuum at infinity, for the sublinear range 0<δ<1 and the linear case δ=1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work by Cao, Li, and Zhu (2022) established large-data global results for the Navier-Stokes equations; this paper aims to extend those results to the more complex MHD equations, where the magnetic field and a viscosity that vanishes where density vanishes (vacuum at spatial infinity) introduce significant new difficulties.

Method: The analysis uses a nontrivial invariant class of magnetic fields described by the frozen-in flux h=b/ρ and its mass derivative k=ρ^{-1}h_x. A key identity, [∂_t+u∂_x, ρ^{-1}∂_x]=0, transports both h and k, removes negative powers of density from the Lorentz force, and propagates magnetic regularity. A modified effective-velocity entropy with an additional coercive magnetic term (error controlled by k) is introduced, and magnetic pressure strengthens the density upper bound.

Result: Global large-data theorems are established for 0<δ<1 with γ≥δ+1/2, and for δ=1 with γ>3/2. The combined estimates close the large-data continuation argument in these parameter ranges.

Conclusion: The paper extends the work of Cao, Li, and Zhu from the Navier-Stokes equations to the MHD equations, providing global large-data well-posedness results for the considered transverse-field MHD system with degenerate viscosity.

Abstract: We consider the Cauchy problem on the real line for a one-dimensional transverse-field isentropic non-resistive magnetohydrodynamic system with viscosity $μ(ρ)=αρ^δ$, $0<δ\leq1$, and vacuum at spatial infinity. The viscosity therefore vanishes precisely where the density vanishes. We establish two global large-data theorems, one for the sublinear range $0<δ<1$ and one for the linear case $δ=1$, in a nontrivial invariant class of magnetic fields. The class is described by the frozen-in flux $h=b/ρ$ and its mass derivative $k=ρ^{-1}h_x$. The key identity $[\partial_t+u\partial_x,ρ^{-1}\partial_x]=0$ transports both $h$ and $k$, removes all negative powers of the density from the Lorentz force, and propagates the magnetic regularity. A modified effective-velocity entropy contains an additional coercive magnetic term, while its only error is controlled by $k$. Magnetic pressure also strengthens the density upper bound. These estimates close the large-data continuation argument in the ranges $0<δ<1$, $γ\geqδ+\tfrac12$, and $δ=1$, $γ>\tfrac32$. As a result, we extend the work of Cao, Li, and Zhu (SIAM J. Math. Anal., 2022) from the Navier-Stokes equations to the MHD equations.

</details>


### [53] [Loss of derivatives for hyperbolic operators with coefficients oscillating in time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13343)
*Wenhui Chen,Michael Reissig*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves energy estimates for second-order strictly hyperbolic operators with Lipschitz-continuous coefficients that may oscillate in time, using Tarama's energy combined with frequency-dependent weights.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extends recent work by Colombini, Del Santo, and Fanelli (2025) on spatially Lipschitz coefficients by allowing time-derivative bounds with an extra logarithmic factor, addressing oscillatory coefficients near the initial time.

Method: Combines Tarama's energy with frequency-dependent weights to derive energy estimates, controlling coefficients whose first two time derivatives are bounded by powers of (1/t)(log(1/t))^α with 0 ≤ α < 1, and whose first time derivative satisfies the spatial Lipschitz bound.

Result: Obtains an energy estimate without loss for α = 0, and with a stretched logarithmic upper bound for the loss when 0 < α < 1, implying arbitrarily small loss in the Sobolev scale.

Conclusion: The estimates generalize prior spatially-Lipschitz analyses to time-derivative bounds with an additional logarithmic factor, yielding near-lossless energy estimates in the Sobolev scale.

Abstract: We consider second-order strictly hyperbolic operators whose coefficients are Lipschitz continuous in space and may oscillate near the initial time. Their first two time derivatives are bounded by the corresponding powers of $\frac{1}{t}(\log\frac{1}{t})^α$ with $0\leqslantα<1$, and the first time derivative satisfies the corresponding spatial Lipschitz bound. By combining Tarama's energy with frequency-dependent weights, we prove an energy estimate without loss for $α=0$ and with a stretched logarithmic upper bound for the loss when $0<α<1$, which implies an arbitrarily small loss in the Sobolev scale. These estimates extend the analysis of Colombini, Del Santo and Fanelli [\emph{Ann. Scuola Norm. Sup. Pisa Cl. Sci. (5)} \textbf{26} (2025)] for spatially Lipschitz coefficients to time-derivative bounds with an additional logarithmic factor.

</details>


### [54] [Complex Monge-Amp{è}re equations with boundary values: a variational approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13478)
*Papa Badiane,Chinh H. Lu,Souhaibou Sambou,Ahmed Zeriahi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper introduces a class E¹_Φ(Ω) of plurisubharmonic functions with boundary values Φ (Cegrell sense) and finite Φ-energy, characterizing when a Borel measure is a Monge-Ampère measure via a variational approach.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the theory of Monge-Ampère measures on hyperconvex domains to allow maximal plurisubharmonic boundary data Φ (possibly unbounded), generalizing earlier results of Cegrell and others.

Method: A variational approach combined with a logarithmic transformation is used to study Monge-Ampère measures in the new class E¹_Φ(Ω).

Result: A Borel measure μ is the Monge-Ampère measure of some u ∈ E¹_Φ(Ω) iff it satisfies a certain integrability condition; a quantitative version of this condition is also established, and analogous results hold for real Monge-Ampère equations with unbounded boundary data.

Conclusion: The work characterizes solvability of the complex (and via logarithmic transformation, real) Monge-Ampère equation with general boundary data Φ, extending previous results.

Abstract: Let $Ω$ ___ C n be a bounded hyperconvex domain and $Φ$\,: $Ω$ $\rightarrow$ R a maximal plurisubharmonic function in $Ω$ (possibly unbounded). We introduce a new class E 1 $Φ$ ($Ω$) of plurisubharmonic functions with boundary values equal to $Φ$ in the sense of Cegrell and finite $Φ$-energy on $Ω$. Then given a Borel measure $μ$ on $Ω$, we apply a variational approach to prove that $μ$ is the Monge-Amp{è}re measure of a function u $\in$ E 1 $Φ$ ($Ω$) if and only if it satisfies the integrability condition  We also establish a quantitative version of this condition extending previous results of Cegrell and others. Using a logarithmic transformation, we obtain analogous results for the real Monge-Amp{è}re equations with (unbounded) boundary data.

</details>


### [55] [An inverse spectral problem for the wave equation with a varying Robin parameter at the boundary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13629)
*Antti Hannukainen,Petr Kulikov,Matti Lassas,Lauri Oksanen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A two-step method recovers wave speed c(x) in a 1D wave equation from Neumann-Dirichlet eigenvalues and their Robin-parameter derivatives, combining NtD reconstruction with a regularized boundary control method.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve an inverse spectral problem for the (1+1)-dimensional wave equation with variable wave speed, recovering c(x) from spectral data (Neumann-Dirichlet eigenvalues plus their derivatives w.r.t. a Robin boundary parameter).

Method: Two-step recovery: (1) reconstruct the Neumann-to-Dirichlet map Λ from spectral data using arXiv/0406582; (2) use Λ in the regularized boundary control method of arXiv:1509.04478, with an improved regularization using a weighted L²+H¹ penalty and a uniform convergence result for the optimal controls enabling simultaneous tuning of regularization parameters.

Result: Proves the forward spectral map is Lipschitz continuous and the inverse spectral map is Hölder continuous on a subclass of wave speeds. Numerical experiments on smooth and piecewise smooth profiles show high accuracy and robustness.

Conclusion: The proposed two-step, regularized boundary control approach yields an accurate and robust reconstruction of the wave speed, supported by continuity proofs and numerical validation.

Abstract: An inverse spectral problem for a $(1+1)$-dimensional wave equation with wave speed $c(x)$ is considered. The spectral data consist of the eigenvalues of the Neumann--Dirichlet problem for the operator $-c^2(x)\partial_x^2$ together with their derivatives with respect to a Robin parameter at the boundary. We propose a two-step recovery procedure. In the first step, the Neumann-to-Dirichlet map $Λ$ is reconstructed from the spectral data using the results of arXiv/0406582. In the second step, the reconstructed $Λ$ is used within the regularization strategy for the boundary control method presented in arXiv:1509.04478. As an improvement, the regularization of the control problems employs a weighted combination of $L^2$ and $H^1$ penalty terms, and we prove a uniform convergence result for the family of optimal controls, thereby allowing the regularization parameters to be tuned simultaneously.
  We also prove that the forward spectral map, which assigns to a wave speed its spectral data, is Lipschitz continuous, and that the inverse spectral map underlying the reconstruction procedure is Hölder continuous on a subclass of wave speeds.
  The method is implemented numerically and validated on both smooth and piecewise smooth wave speed profiles. Numerical experiments demonstrate that the recovery of the wave speed exhibits high accuracy and robustness.

</details>


### [56] [Weighted Liouville Theorems and A Priori Bounds for Indefinite Superlinear Equations with Pucci's Maximal Operator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13486)
*Karan Rathore,Mohan Mallick,Ram Baran Verma*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes a uniform bound on positive solutions of a fully nonlinear elliptic problem driven by the Pucci extremal operator, with a sign-changing weight whose zero set may have non-empty interior, via a suitable Liouville-type theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Uniform bounds for positive solutions are needed for this Pucci-operator problem where the weight a(x) may change sign and its zero set can have non-empty interior, a setting not covered by standard results.

Method: Prove a Liouville-type theorem for the equation involving the Pucci operator with a strictly increasing one-dimensional weight h(x1) and a positive nondecreasing f, then apply it to derive the uniform bound.

Result: A uniform bound on the set of positive solutions of the problem is established, supported by the new Liouville-type theorem.

Conclusion: The Liouville-type theorem provides the key tool enabling uniform boundedness of positive solutions even when the weight changes sign and degenerates on a set with non-empty interior.

Abstract: Consider \begin{equation*}\ \left\{ \begin{aligned} -\mathcal_{λ,Λ}^{+}(D^{2}u)&=μu+a(x)u^{p}\quad\text{in}~Ω,\\ u&=0\quad\text{on}~~\partialΩ, \end{aligned} \right. \end{equation*} where $ \ Omega$ is a smooth bounded domain, $a$ is a continuous function which is allowed to change sign. Moreover, the set of zeros of $a$ may have a non-empty interior. The main objective of this article is to establish a uniform bound on the set of positive solutions of the above problem. This approach requires a suitable Liouville-type theorem. We first prove the Liouville-type theorem for $-\mathcal_{λ,Λ}^{+}(D^{2}v)=h(x_{1})f(v),$ where \(h\) is a strictly increasing one-dimensional weight and \(f\) is positive and nondecreasing.

</details>


### [57] [Diffraction of waves by finite thin coatings: asymptotic expansion and effective models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13680)
*Cédric Baudet*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the Helmholtz equation in a Dirichlet angular sector covered on one side by a thin homogeneous layer. It derives and justifies an asymptotic expansion of the solution as the layer thickness tends to zero, validates it numerically up to order 5, and builds effective models of order 3 and 5 using Robin/Ventcel conditions away from the corner and a Dirichlet-to-Neumann condition near it.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Thin layers covering part of a domain arise in many physical settings, and treating them directly is costly. When the domain has corners, the fields exhibit complex singular behaviors that make standard asymptotic matching delicate. The goal is to understand the limit behavior as the layer thickness vanishes and to replace the thin layer by an efficient equivalent boundary condition.

Method: A matched asymptotic expansion is used, introducing separate expansions in three subdomains: the corner vicinity, the thin layer, and the rest of the domain, linked by matching conditions. A novel algebraic system of tools (formal series and operators on them) handles the delicate matching caused by corner singularities. The expansion is justified with error estimates. Numerical validation up to order 5 requires computing the behavior at infinity of corner profiles, done via a Galerkin-type method enriched by complements at infinity with unknown coefficients. Effective models of order 3 (Robin) and 5 (Ventcel) replace the layer far from the corner, while near the corner an artificial boundary with a DtN-type condition based on a modal decomposition of the truncated expansion is imposed.

Result: An asymptotic expansion of the solution at any order and for any angle is proposed and justified with error estimates, with the expansion form detailed as a function of the angle. The expansion is numerically validated up to order 5. Effective models of order 3 and 5 are proposed; their well-posedness and error estimates are proven, and numerical results are provided.

Conclusion: The thin layer can be efficiently replaced by effective Robin (order 3) or Ventcel (order 5) boundary conditions away from the corner, combined with a DtN-type artificial boundary condition near the corner that captures the singular corner profiles and algebraic matching coefficients. These effective models are well-posed and accurate, providing a practical reduced description of the original problem.

Abstract: We consider the Helmholtz equation in a Dirichlet angular sector (which may be locally perturbed) covered on one side by a thin homogeneous layer. First, we propose an asymptotic expansion of the solution at any order and for any angle when the thickness of the layer tends to 0, using a matched asymptotic expansion. This consists in introducing different asymptotic expansions in three subdomains -- the vicinity of the corner, the layer and the rest of the domain -- that are linked through matching conditions. The presence of the corner makes these matching conditions delicate to derive because the fields have complex singular behaviors. We overcome this difficulty using a novel system of algebraic tools, including formal series and operators on these series. Moreover, we justify the asymptotic expansion with error estimates and provide details on the form of the expansion depending on the angle.
  We numerically validate this expansion up to order 5. This requires computing the behavior at infinity of some corner profiles. To do this, we introduce a Galerkin-type method enriched by complements at infinity. These complements are known functions that appear in the behavior at infinity of the corner profiles with coefficients that become unknowns of the system.
  Finally, we propose effective models of order 3, resp. 5 where, far from the corner, the layer is replaced with an effective Robin, resp. Ventcel, boundary condition and, near the corner, an artificial boundary is introduced and a Dirichlet-to-Neumann (DtN) type condition is imposed. The DtN map relies on a modal decomposition of the truncated asymptotic expansion at order 3, resp. 5. The modes take into account the singular behavior of the expansion terms, the corner profiles and some algebraic matching coefficients. We prove the well-posedness of our effective models, as well as error estimates, and provide numerical results.

</details>


### [58] [Blocking of bistable front propagation: Application to Wolbachia population replacement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13497)
*Saoussen Latrach,Nicolas Vauchelet,Hatem Zaag*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A reaction-diffusion model for spreading Wolbachia through mosquito populations is reduced to a single closed equation, then used to study how spatial variations in environmental carrying capacity can block the propagation of population replacement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Mosquito-borne diseases like dengue lack a vaccine, so strategies directly targeting mosquito populations are needed. Introducing the bacterium Wolbachia blocks pathogen transmission, and prior work showed spatial propagation of replacement in homogeneous environments. This paper addresses the gap of understanding what happens in non-homogeneous environments.

Method: Reduce the original 2x2 reaction-diffusion system to a single closed equation to simplify analysis of Wolbachia propagation dynamics, then analyze the blocking of propagation focusing on spatial variations in environmental carrying capacity across regions. Numerical simulations validate the theoretical results.

Result: The propagation of Wolbachia-induced population replacement can be blocked when the environmental carrying capacity varies significantly across different regions of the spatial domain. Theoretical results are confirmed by numerical simulations.
