Conclusion: Spatial heterogeneity in carrying capacity can halt the spatial spread of Wolbachia replacement, providing insight for disease-control strategies in non-homogeneous environments.

Abstract: In order to control epidemics of mosquito-borne diseases such as dengue, for which there is still no vaccine available, several strategies aim to directly target the mosquito population. One technique involves introducing a symbiotic bacterium called Wolbachia into the population, which blocks the transmission of pathogens. A mathematical model of the introduction of this bacterium and the replacement of the mosquito population with a population carrying this bacterium has been developed, proving that in a homogeneous environment, there is spatial propagation of the replacement. The objective of this paper is to study the blocking of this propagation in cases where the environment is not homogeneous. In particular, we are considering scenarios where the environmental carrying capacity varies significantly across different regions of the spatial domain. To achieve this objective, we first reduce the original 2x2 reaction-diffusion system to a single closed equation, providing a simplified framework for studying the dynamics of Wolbachia propagation. We then investigate the blocking of the propagation in the population replacement process, focusing on the effects of spatial variations in the environmental carrying capacity. Numerical simulations have been performed to validate the theoretical results.

</details>


### [59] [Short scale asymptotics in nonlocal spectral problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13513)
*Volodymyr Rybalko,Antonina Rybalko*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Asymptotic expansion of the first eigenvalues of a nonlocal convolution-type operator L_ε as ε→0, with distinct behavior depending on whether the minimum of a(x) is interior or on the boundary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize the spectral asymptotics of nonlocal convolution operators L_ε in the small-scaling (semiclassical-type) limit ε→0, where the leading behavior is governed by the minimum of the potential a(x) and the interplay with the domain boundary.

Method: Blow-up analysis at the minimum point x_0 (assumed non-degenerate), yielding a harmonic oscillator in the interior case; for boundary minima, a mixed scaling combining a scaled Airy function in the normal direction and harmonic-oscillator eigenfunctions in tangential variables.

Result: Interior x_0: λ_ε^(k) = a(x_0) − 1 + εμ^(k) + o(ε), eigenfunctions concentrated near x_0 on scale ε^(1/2). Boundary x_0: λ_ε^(k) = a(x_0) − 1 + ε^(2/3)Λ_0 + ε(μ^(k) + αp_0) + o(ε), with eigenfunctions as products of a normal-direction Airy-type factor on scale ε^(2/3) and tangential harmonic-oscillator modes on scale ε^(1/2); the constants Λ_0, α, p_0 depend on J and a.

Conclusion: The spectrum of L_ε exhibits a two-scale structure whose asymptotics are governed by the location (interior vs. boundary) of the non-degenerate minimum of a(x), with interior minima giving ε-corrections and boundary minima giving ε^(2/3) corrections tied to Airy behavior.

Abstract: We study the asymptotic behavior of eigenvalues of nonlocal convolution-type operators $$L_\varepsilonρ=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\int_ΩJ\left({\frac{|x-y|^2}{\varepsilon^2}}\right)ρ(y)dy+a(x)ρ(x),$$ (with a continuous sufficiently fast decaying at infinity function $J\geq 0$) when the scaling parameter $\varepsilon>0$ tends to zero. Assuming that the minimum of the function $a(x)$ is attained at a point $x_0\in \overlineΩ\subset \mathbb{R}^d$ and is non-degenerate, we establish several terms in the asymptotic expansion of the first eigenvalues $λ_\varepsilon^{(k)}$ of $L_\varepsilon$. If $x_0$ is an interior point of the domain $Ω$, we show that $λ_\varepsilon^{(k)}=a(x_0)-1+\varepsilonμ^{(k)}+\overline{o}(\varepsilon)$, where $μ^{(k)}$ are eigenvalues of a harmonic oscillator (obtained by the blow up analysis), the corresponding eigenfunctions of $\mathcal{L}_\varepsilon$ are concentrated near $x_0$ on the scale $\varepsilon^{1/2}$. If $x_0\in\partial Ω$ then the asymptotic expansion of the eigenvalues of $L_\varepsilon$ has the form $λ_\varepsilon^{(k)}=a(x_0)-1+\varepsilon^{2/3}Λ_0+\varepsilon(μ^{(k)}+αp_0)+\overline{o}(\varepsilon)$. The corresponding eigenfunctions are represented as the product of a function depending on the distance to $\partialΩ$ and concentrated on the scale $\varepsilon^{2/3}$, and functions localized on the scale $\varepsilon^{1/2}$ and asymptotically independent of the distance to $\partialΩ$. The latter functions converge (after rescaling) to eigenfunctions of a harmonic oscillator (in tangential variables), $μ^{(k)}$ being their eigenvalues. The former function is given, to the leading term, by the scaled Airy function, and $Λ_0$, $α$ and $p_0$ depend on $J(|x|^2)$ and $a(x)$.

</details>


### [60] [Hölder regularity of solutions to parabolic equations associated with fractional $p$-Laplacian type operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13539)
*Takashi Kumagai,Jian Wang,Meng-ge Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes Hölder regularity for local weak supersolutions of parabolic equations governed by fractional p-Laplacian type operators, extending prior work from p=2 to the nonlinear regime p≠2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Kassmann and Weidner established fundamental Hölder regularity results for the linear case (p=2) of nonlocal parabolic equations. This work aims to extend those results to the nonlinear case p≠2, which requires addressing new technical challenges.

Method: The proof uses the method of expansion of positivity as the key ingredient in a reduction-of-oscillation argument. The authors derive an explicit nonlocal parabolic tail condition expressed via an L^{1+ε}-norm in the time variable.

Result: Hölder regularity is proven to hold for local weak supersolutions under the proposed explicit nonlocal parabolic tail condition, valid for all p in (1,∞), with the kernel J comparable to the fractional p-Laplacian kernel |x−y|^{-d−sp}.

Conclusion: The work generalizes the Hölder regularity theory of Kassmann and Weidner from the linear case to nonlinear fractional p-Laplacian type parabolic equations, providing a unified tail condition valid for all p∈(1,∞).

Abstract: In this paper, we study Hölder regularity of local weak super-solutions to the parabolic equation associated with fractional $p$-Laplacian type operators $$ \partial_t u(t,x)-\text{p.v.}\int_{\R^d}|u(t,y)-u(t,x)|^{p-2}(u(t,y)-u(t,x))J(t;x,y)\,dy=0,\quad (t,x)\in \R\times \R^d, $$ where $\text{p.v.}$ denotes the principal value, $p\in(1,\infty)$ and the kernel $J(t;x,y)$ is comparable (uniformly with respect to
  $(t;x,y)\in\R\times\R^d\times\R^d$) to the fractional $p$-Laplacian kernel $|x-y|^{-d-sp}$ with $s\in(0,1)$. Our contribution extends the fundamental work of Kassmann and Weidner to the nonlinear case $p\ne 2$. In particular, we provide an explicit nonlocal parabolic tail condition in terms of an $L^{1+\varepsilon}$-norm in the time variable (for a suitable $\varepsilon>0$) for all $p\in(1,\infty)$, under which Hölder regularity holds. Our proof is based on the method of expansion of positivity, which is the key ingredient in the reduction-of-oscillation argument.

</details>


### [61] [Identifying secondary critical mass levels in two- and higher-dimensional Keller-Segel systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13559)
*Yulan Wang,Michael Winkler*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper identifies a critical mass $m_\star(n,R)$ for the parabolic-elliptic Keller-Segel system in a ball, below which the constant equilibrium is locally asymptotically stable, and above which radial solutions with certain mass distribution blow up in finite time. The threshold is explicitly related to Bessel function zeros, with $m_\star(2,R)\approx 14.68197\pi$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Known mass-dependent blow-up dichotomies for Keller-Segel systems typically involve primary critical masses. This work seeks to reveal a genuinely secondary critical mass phenomenon, especially in the two-dimensional case where no such precedent was known.

Method: The parabolic-elliptic Keller-Segel system is studied under homogeneous Neumann boundary conditions in a ball $B_R(0)\subset\mathbb{R}^n$ for $n\ge 2$. Stability analysis and finite-time blow-up criteria are established via the identification of a critical mass $m_\star(n,R)$ tied to principal zeros of Bessel functions of the first kind.

Result: If $0<m<m_\star(n,R)$, the constant equilibrium $u\equiv v\equiv m/|B_R|$ is locally asymptotically stable in $L^\infty(B_R)$. If $m>m_\star(n,R)$, any solution with nonnegative, nonconstant, continuous radial initial data of mass $m$ and satisfying the radial mass-averaging condition blows up in finite time. The threshold is essentially explicit via Bessel zeros; notably $m_\star(2,R)\approx 14.68197\pi$ for all $R>0$.

Conclusion: The findings establish a secondary critical mass phenomenon for the Keller-Segel system, complementing known mass-dependent blow-up dichotomies and providing a novel result particularly in the two-dimensional setting.

Abstract: The parabolic-elliptic Keller-Segel system \[
  \left\{ \begin{array}{l}
  u_t = Δu - \nabla \cdot (u\nabla v), \\[1mm]
  0 = Δv - μ+ u,
  \qquad μ:=\frac{1}{|Ω|} \int_Ωu,
  \end{array} \right.
  \qquad \qquad (\star) \] is considered under homogeneous Neumann boundary conditions in the ball $Ω=B_R(0)\subset {\mathbb R}^n$, where $n\ge 2$ and $R>0$.
  The main results identify a mass level $m_\star(n,R)>0$ as critical in the sense that
  $\bullet$ whenever $0<m<m_\star(n,R)$, the constant equilibrium of ($\star$) given by $u\equiv v\equiv \frac{m}{|B_R|}$ is locally asymptotically stable with respect to the norm in $L^\infty(B_R)$,
  but that
  $\bullet$ if $m>m_\star(n,R)$, then any solution of ($\star$) emanating from nonnegative and nonconstant continuous radial initial data $u_0$ with $\int_{B_R} u_0=m$ and $\frac{1}{|B_r|} \int_{B_r} u_0 \ge \frac{m}{|B_R|}$ for all $r\in (0,R)$ blows up in finite time.
  The value of $m_\star(n,R)$ can be determined in an essentially explicit manner in dependence on principal zeroes of appropriate Bessel functions of the first kind; in particular, $m_\star(2,R)\approx 14.68197 \cdot π$ for all $R>0$. Accordingly, as a supplement to known mass-dependent blow-up dichotomies these findings reveal a genuinely secondary critical mass phenomenon for ($\star$) especially also in its two-dimensional version in which no precedent of this form seems known.

</details>


### [62] [Boundary stabilization of multi-dimensional first-order quasilinear hyperbolic systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13577)
*Haitian Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Global existence and exponential H^s decay are proved for multi-dimensional first-order symmetrizable quasilinear hyperbolic systems with nonlinear feedback boundary conditions, assuming linearized Lyapunov inequalities and non-characteristic boundaries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Long-time behaviour of quasilinear hyperbolic systems with nonlinear feedback boundary conditions is not fully understood, motivating a general framework using strict interior and boundary Lyapunov inequalities.

Method: Assume strict interior and boundary Lyapunov inequalities for the linearized system and non-characteristic boundaries; prove global existence for small data and exponential H^s decay (s>d/2+1 integer) on bounded smooth domains and flat periodic cylinders.

Result: Global existence and exponential H^s decay are established for small initial data, supported by physical examples and numerical simulations.

Conclusion: The Lyapunov-inequality framework yields general stability results for quasilinear hyperbolic systems with nonlinear feedback boundary conditions.

Abstract: We study the long-time behaviour of multi-dimensional first-order symmetrizable quasilinear hyperbolic systems with nonlinear feedback boundary conditions. Assuming strict interior and boundary Lyapunov inequalities for the linearized system and non-characteristic boundaries, we prove global existence for small initial data and exponential H^s decay, with s>d/2+1 an integer. We consider both bounded smooth domains and flat periodic cylinders, and illustrate the results with physical examples and numerical simulations.

</details>


### [63] [The conformal Poisson embedding method for the anisotropic Calderón problem with a potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13592)
*Cătălin I. Cârstea,Tony Liimatainen,Yi-Hsuan Lin,William Trad*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The local Dirichlet-to-Neumann map of Δ_g + q determines a compact connected real-analytic Riemannian manifold (dim ≥ 3) and real-analytic potential up to boundary-fixing diffeomorphism and conformal gauge; new even for constant q (Helmholtz).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establishes a strong uniqueness/rigidity result for Calderón-type inverse problems with partial boundary measurements on real-analytic manifolds, extending known results and covering the fixed-frequency Helmholtz case.

Method: Develops a conformal Poisson embedding method and uses coordinates given by quotients of solutions, with measurements restricted to an arbitrary nonempty open boundary set Γ.

Result: The local Dirichlet-to-Neumann map determines the manifold, metric conformal class, and potential up to diffeomorphism fixing the observed boundary and conformal gauge, for dimension ≥ 3.

Conclusion: A conformal-gauge rigidity theorem for the partial-data Dirichlet-to-Neumann map, giving new results even in the constant-potential (Helmholtz) setting.

Abstract: We prove that the local Dirichlet-to-Neumann map of $Δ_g+q$ determines a compact connected real-analytic Riemannian manifold of dimension at least three and a real-analytic real-valued potential, up to a diffeomorphism fixing the observed boundary and the conformal gauge of the equation. The result is new even when \(q\) is constant, i.e. for the fixed-frequency (Helmholtz) equation \(Δ_g u+λu=0\). The measurements are restricted to an arbitrary nonempty open boundary set $Γ$. The proof develops conformal Poisson embeddings method and uses coordinates given by quotients of solutions.

</details>


### [64] [Global Existence and Partial Regularity for the Three-Dimensional Chemotaxis--Navier--Stokes System in a Half-Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13613)
*Yubo Chen,Jianbo Yu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Endpoint partial regularity for 3D chemotaxis--Navier--Stokes in a half-space: singular set has vanishing 1D parabolic Hausdorff measure, via a CKN-type criterion at r^{-1} scaling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend partial regularity and large-time asymptotics for chemotaxis--Navier--Stokes to the physically relevant half-space with no-slip/no-flux boundary conditions, at the endpoint dissipation scaling and without extra pressure smallness.

Method: Weighted mass control combined with the localized entropy--energy inequality to close the iteration without an n log n quantity, handling the rescaling-generated mass term; boundary-compatible pressure and caloric decompositions; a lower-order criterion requiring smallness only of velocity, signal gradient, and pressure oscillation.

Result: The full space--time singular set (including boundary) has vanishing one-dimensional parabolic Hausdorff measure. A lower-order criterion needs smallness only of velocity, signal gradient, and pressure oscillation. Global suitable weak solutions exist under natural entropy/energy assumptions without smallness; with Hölder-continuous potential gradient, a solution becomes bounded and classical after finite waiting time, with decay and Neumann Gaussian asymptotics for both scalars, no initial moment needed.

Conclusion: The theory yields endpoint partial regularity and long-time behavior for chemotaxis--Navier--Stokes in a half-space; profile coefficients are the conserved bacterial mass and the limiting signal mass, the latter strictly positive for nonzero initial signal.

Abstract: We establish endpoint partial regularity for suitable weak solutions to the three-dimensional chemotaxis--Navier--Stokes system with constant sensitivity and linear consumption in a half-space under no-slip and no-flux boundary conditions. The full space--time singular set, including its boundary part, has vanishing one-dimensional parabolic Hausdorff measure. This follows from a Caffarelli--Kohn--Nirenberg type criterion at the natural $r^{-1}$ dissipation scaling, valid at interior and boundary points without additional pressure smallness.
  The key estimate combines weighted mass control with the localized entropy--energy inequality to close the iteration without a separate $n\log n$ quantity and accounts for the mass term generated by rescaling. Boundary-compatible pressure and caloric decompositions yield local Hölder regularity. We also prove a lower-order criterion requiring smallness only of the velocity, signal gradient, and pressure oscillation, with a threshold depending on an arbitrary finite density mass bound.
  We construct global suitable weak solutions under natural entropy and energy assumptions without smallness restrictions on the initial data. The potential is time-independent with bounded gradient; boundedness of the potential itself is assumed only for the large-time results. If its gradient is also Hölder continuous, a solution can be selected to become bounded and classical throughout the closed half-space after a finite waiting time. For the same solution, we obtain decay and Neumann Gaussian asymptotics for both scalar components without initial spatial moment assumptions. The profile coefficients are the conserved bacterial mass and the limiting signal mass, respectively, and the latter is strictly positive whenever the initial signal is nonzero.

</details>


### [65] [Global Existence of Strong and Weak Solutions to the 2D Compressible Navier-Stokes-Poisson System in Bounded Domains with Large Data and Vacuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13618)
*Yingzhi Du,Huijiang Zhao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global existence of unique strong and global weak solutions to the 2D compressible Navier-Stokes-Poisson system on a general simply connected bounded domain with Navier-slip boundary conditions, arbitrary large data, and vacuum, requiring shear viscosity constant and bulk viscosity ∝ ρ^γ with γ>4/3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing results are limited by boundary geometry and Poisson coupling. This work addresses general simply connected domains with non-flat doping profiles, where the interaction between the Poisson force and boundary geometry creates the main difficulties.

Method: Introduces a modified material derivative; derives quantitative L^p density estimates via a Neumann duality argument with a harmonic correction from the Navier-slip condition; obtains a pointwise representation of the effective viscous flux by pulling back the Neumann Green function on the unit disk through a conformal mapping; uses commutator estimates plus elliptic estimates for Poisson; constructs the global weak solution via charge-preserving approximation and compactness.

Result: Establishes global existence of a unique strong solution and a global weak solution for arbitrarily large initial data allowing vacuum, under the stated viscosity assumptions.

Conclusion: The uniform-in-time upper bound for the density enables closing higher-order a priori estimates, yielding global well-posedness and weak solutions for the compressible Navier-Stokes-Poisson system in general domains.

Abstract: We study the two-dimensional compressible Navier-Stokes-Poisson system in a general simply connected bounded domain with Navier-slip boundary conditions and a positive, possibly non-flat, doping profile. We establish the global existence of a unique strong solution and of a global weak solution for arbitrarily large initial data allowing vacuum when the shear viscosity is a positive constant and the bulk one proportional to a power of the density with the power bigger than 4/3.
  The main difficulties arise from the interaction between the Poisson force and the general boundary geometry. To overcome them, we introduce a modified material derivative and derive quantitative $L^p$ estimates for the density by means of a Neumann duality argument together with a harmonic correction generated by the Navier-slip boundary condition. We further obtain a pointwise representation of the effective viscous flux by pulling back the Neumann Green function on the unit disk through a conformal mapping. The resulting commutator estimates, combined with elliptic estimates for the Poisson equation, yield a uniform-in-time upper bound for the density and allow us to close the higher-order a priori estimates. Finally, the global weak solution is constructed through a charge-preserving approximation and compactness argument.

</details>


### [66] [Travel time tomography for elliptically anisotropic elastic planar media](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13747)
*Joonas Ilmavirta,Teemu Saksala,Hjørdis Schlüter*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies travel time tomography for elliptically anisotropic planar media with slowness surfaces combining an inner ellipse and an outer circle. It shows uniqueness (up to diffeomorphism) of the anisotropic stiffness tensor when at least one isotropic point exists, and exactly two solutions otherwise.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Travel time tomography for anisotropic media is challenging due to non-uniqueness. Restricting to elliptical anisotropy in planar media, where slowness surfaces are an inner ellipse and outer circle, offers a tractable setting to investigate the gauge (non-uniqueness) of the inverse problem.

Method: The authors model wave propagation via two Riemannian metrics, the shear-wave one being conformally Euclidean. Assuming lens rigidity, they combine algebra and geometry, characterizing elliptical anisotropy through several polynomial conditions.

Result: Up to boundary-fixing diffeomorphisms, each elliptically anisotropic stiffness tensor is uniquely determined by lens data whenever it has at least one isotropic point; if no such point exists, there are exactly two such tensors with identical lens data. Additionally, the set of well-behaved boundary-fixing diffeomorphisms is never a subgroup.

Conclusion: The lens data determines elliptical anisotropy almost uniquely, with a two-fold ambiguity only in the absence of isotropic points, and the structure-preserving diffeomorphisms do not form a subgroup.

Abstract: We study the gauge of the travel time tomography problem for elliptically anisotropic planar media. Under this assumption the respective slowness surfaces consist of an inner ellipse and an outer circle. Hence, the singularities of seismic waves propagate on geodesics given by two Riemannian metrics of which the one related to shear waves is conformally Euclidean. We show that if both of these metrics are lens rigid then, up to boundary fixing diffeomorphisms, each elliptically anisotropic stiffness tensor is uniquely determined by the lens data whenever it has at least one isotropic point. If none of those points exists, then there are exactly two elliptically anisotropic stiffness tensors with the same lens data. Clearly, not all boundary fixing diffeomorphisms preserve the imposed structure of the slowness surface. We show that the set of well-behaved diffeomorphisms is never a subgroup of all boundary fixing diffeomorphisms. Our proofs are based on an interplay of algebra and geometry, and the key of the proofs is a characterization of the elliptic anisotropy via several polynomial conditions.

</details>


### [67] [Asymmetric minimal mass blow-up for the quintic NLS on a star graph](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13752)
*Mattia Buratti,François Genoud,Vladimir Georgiev*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper constructs a non-radial minimal mass finite-time blow-up solution for the focusing quintic NLS on a two-edge star graph with a delta interaction at the vertex, with blow-up at a prescribed interior point.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding minimal mass blow-up for NLS on graphs is a natural step beyond the Euclidean line, especially for non-radial solutions and the role of the vertex interaction.

Method: Cut-off of the standard pseudoconformal blow-up solution of the focusing quintic NLS on the line, inspired by Ogawa-Tsutsumi and Burq-Gérard-Tzvetkov, with persistence of the Hamiltonian domain via Kato's persistence of regularity method.

Result: A non-radial minimal mass finite-time blow-up solution whose singularity forms at an arbitrarily prescribed interior point of one edge; a two-parameter family of distinct blow-up profiles identified.

Conclusion: The construction is generic, extends to star graphs with arbitrary numbers of edges, and can be adapted to other metric graphs; the blow-up profiles should aid classification of non-radial minimal mass blow-up solutions on star graphs.

Abstract: This work is concerned with the focusing quintic nonlinear Schrödinger equation on a star graph with two edges, with a delta interaction of arbitrary strength at the vertex. We construct a non-radial, minimal mass, finite time blow-up solution, whose singularity forms at an arbitrarily prescribed interior point of one edge. Our approach is based on a suitable cut-off of the standard pseudoconformal blow-up solution of the focusing quintic NLS on the line, inspired by Ogawa-Tsutsumi and Burq-Gérard-Tzvetkov. A noteworthy feature of our construction is the persistence of the domain of the Hamiltonian under the flow, obtained by the persistence of regularity method of Kato. We also identify a two-parameter family of distinct blow-up profiles, which should play a central role in the classification of non-radial minimal mass blow-up solutions on the star graph. Our construction is generic and can be extended to a star graph with an arbitrary number of edges. It can certainly also be adapted to other types of metric graphs.

</details>


### [68] [Scattering for the KP-II equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13753)
*Xi Chen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves $L^2(\mathbb{R}^2)$ scattering for real-valued solutions of the KP-II equation with localized initial data, including all Schwartz functions, without smallness or zero-mean conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Scattering for the KP-II equation has typically required restrictive assumptions such as small amplitude or zero horizontal mean. This work aims to remove these constraints and establish scattering for a broad class of localized data.

Method: The proof employs the scalar $\bar\partial$ equation associated with the heat operator. A uniform inverse estimate and an exact $L^2$ identity enable an inverse construction for large amplitudes. The nonlinear remainder is estimated on the propagation cones of free solutions, and the limiting spectral mass is identified via the heat equation.

Result: Scattering in $L^2(\mathbb{R}^2)$ is established for all Schwartz functions, data with a half spatial moment in $B^2_{1,1}$, and certain weighted tensor Besov spaces permitting low regularity in either coordinate.

Conclusion: This is the first result proving scattering for the KP-II equation for every real-valued Schwartz initial datum, without smallness or zero horizontal mean conditions.

Abstract: We prove scattering in $L^2(\mathbb{R}^2)$ for real-valued solutions of the KP-II equation with localized initial data. The hypotheses cover all Schwartz functions, data with a half spatial moment in $B^2_{1,1}$, and a class of weighted tensor Besov spaces allowing low regularity in either coordinate. The proof uses the scalar $\bar\partial$ equation associated with the heat operator. A uniform inverse estimate and an exact $L^2$ identity give an inverse construction for large amplitudes. We then estimate the nonlinear remainder on the propagation cones of free solutions and identify the limiting spectral mass through the heat equation. No smallness or zero horizontal mean condition is required. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first result establishing scattering for the KP-II equation for every real-valued Schwartz initial datum.

</details>


### [69] [Existence and uniqueness of bound states with prescribed number of nodes for nonlinear dispersive equations on spherically symmetric manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13813)
*Valentino Badalucco*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves existence and uniqueness of radial bound states with a prescribed number of nodal surfaces for the nonlinear elliptic ODE Δu+f(u)=0, on a broad class of spherically symmetric manifolds and nonlinearities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standing waves e^{iωt}u(x) of nonlinear dispersive equations (e.g., nonlinear Schrödinger and Klein–Gordon) reduce to a nonlinear elliptic ODE Δu+f(u)=0. Understanding existence and uniqueness of such bound states—particularly radial ones with prescribed nodal structure—is a fundamental problem, but results covering general geometries and singular manifolds are lacking.

Method: The authors study the elliptic ODE Δu+f(u)=0 for radial solutions. They establish existence of radial bound states with a prescribed number of nodal surfaces on a large class of spherically symmetric manifolds and nonlinearities f. For the model nonlinearity f(u)=|u|^{p-1}u−u, they prove uniqueness under mild assumptions. The analysis also handles manifolds with a singularity at the origin.

Result: Existence of radial bound states with a prescribed number of nodal surfaces is proven for a large class of spherically symmetric manifolds and nonlinearities f. Uniqueness of such bound states is established for the model nonlinearity f(u)=|u|^{p-1}u−u.

Conclusion: The results yield both existence and uniqueness of nodal radial bound states, applying to a wide class of geometries including some manifolds with a singularity at the origin.

Abstract: Standing waves $e^{iωt}u(x)$ of nonlinear dispersive equations, such as the nonlinear Schrödinger and Klein--Gordon equations, have a profile $u$ that solves a nonlinear elliptic ODE of the form $Δu+f(u)=0$. We prove the existence of radial bound states with a prescribed number of nodal surfaces for this equation, on a large class of spherically symmetric manifolds and nonlinearities $f$. Under mild assumptions, we prove uniqueness of such bound states for the model nonlinearity $f(u)=|u|^{p-1}u-u$. Both existence and uniqueness results apply to some manifolds with a singularity at the origin.

</details>


### [70] [Existence and large-field asymptotics of Hopfions in a continuum model of frustrated magnets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13883)
*Glal Bacho,Christof Melcher*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies a continuum model of frustrated magnetism whose maps from R^3 to S^2 possess nontrivial Hopf index, proving existence of minimizers in infinitely many homotopy classes and analyzing the large-field limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Frustrated magnetism supports particle-like field configurations (Hopfions) with nontrivial Hopf index, and understanding their existence via a rigorous variational framework is the central goal.

Method: A variational problem for maps from three-dimensional space into the two-sphere, with an energy functional combining competing second- and first-order exchange interactions stabilized by an external magnetic field.

Result: Existence of energy minimizers in infinitely many nontrivial homotopy classes is proven; a unit-charge Hopfion exists in the strong external field regime; the large-field limit is analyzed, the natural concentration scale identified, and strong convergence of rescaled minimizers to a limiting variational problem is established.

Conclusion: The model admits stable Hopfion solutions across many topological classes, and its large-field asymptotics are rigorously characterized by a limiting variational problem.

Abstract: We study a continuum model of frustrated magnetism that supports particle-like field configurations with nontrivial Hopf index. The energy functional combines competing second and first order exchange interactions with a stabilizing external magnetic field and gives rise to a variational problem for maps from three-dimensional space into the two-sphere. We prove the existence of energy minimizers in infinitely many nontrivial homotopy classes. We further establish the existence of a unit-charge Hopfion in the regime of strong external fields. Finally, we analyze the corresponding large-field limit, identify the natural concentration scale, and prove strong convergence of suitably rescaled minimizers to a minimizer of a limiting variational problem.

</details>


### [71] [Ground states and sharp logarithmic Sobolev inequalities for logarithmic Schrödinger equations with partial confinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13887)
*Baihong Li,Jarosław Mederski,Xiangjian Zeng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the logarithmic Schrödinger equation with a positive semidefinite quadratic potential and a periodic potential in the unconfined directions, establishing existence and classification of ground states via a new variational principle.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: A key variational difficulty arises: turning a constrained minimizer into a critical point is hard when the energy is extended-valued and not C^1 on the natural Hilbert space, especially without homogeneity of the convex part.

Method: The authors prove an abstract principle for convex lower semicontinuous energies with a smooth perturbation, allowing a scaling law for the full energy without homogeneity of the convex part. This is combined with a profile decomposition under integer translations.

Result: The principle yields a positive ground state for every rank of the quadratic form. When the periodic potential vanishes, a sharp anisotropic logarithmic Sobolev inequality is proved, with optimal constant, all equality cases, and explicit anisotropic Gausson ground states unique up to translations along the kernel. When the periodic component is constant, normalized ground states exist and are classified at every prescribed positive mass, with explicit minimum energy and Lagrange multiplier.

Conclusion: The paper provides a general variational framework and sharp results, classifying ground states of the logarithmic Schrödinger equation with quadratic and periodic potentials.

Abstract: We study the logarithmic Schrödinger equation with an arbitrary positive semidefinite quadratic potential and a periodic potential in the unconfined directions. The main variational difficulty is to turn a constrained minimizer into a critical point when the energy is extended-valued and not of class $C^1$ on the natural Hilbert space. We establish an abstract principle for convex lower semicontinuous energies with a smooth perturbation, allowing a scaling law for the full energy without requiring homogeneity of its convex part. Combined with a profile decomposition under integer translations, this principle yields a positive ground state for every rank of the quadratic form. When the periodic potential vanishes, we prove a sharp anisotropic logarithmic Sobolev inequality, determine its optimal constant and all equality cases, and classify positive ground states as explicit anisotropic Gaussons, unique up to translations along the kernel of the quadratic form. When the periodic component is constant, we also establish existence and classify all normalized ground states at every prescribed positive mass, with explicit formulas for the minimum energy and the associated Lagrange multiplier.

</details>


### [72] [The Inviscid Limit in Uniformly Local Yudovich Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13898)
*Zachary Radke*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For 2D Navier-Stokes and Euler equations with bounded velocity and vorticity in a uniformly local Yudovich space, the inviscid limit (ν→0) holds in L∞([0,T];L²_ul) if θ grows slowly enough, from identical initial data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical inviscid limit results for 2D fluids often assume vorticity in L∞ or specific Yudovich classes. This work extends the limit to the uniformly local Yudovich space Y^θ_ul, addressing unbounded (non-decaying) velocity/vorticity settings and identifying how growth of θ controls the convergence.

Method: Function-space/energy approach: study solutions in L∞ velocity and Y^θ_ul vorticity, then prove convergence of Navier-Stokes to Euler in the uniformly local L² topology over [0,T]. The key condition is a sufficiently slow growth of θ governing the uniformly local Yudovich space.

Result: Provided θ grows sufficiently slowly, for every T>0 the Navier-Stokes solutions converge to Euler solutions in L∞([0,T];L²_ul(R²)) as ν→0+, when generated from the same initial data.

Conclusion: The inviscid limit of 2D Navier-Stokes to Euler is established in the uniformly local Yudovich setting, with convergence rate/validity controlled by the growth of θ.

Abstract: We consider solutions to the two-dimensional Navier-Stokes and Euler equations with the velocity in $L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^2)$ and the vorticity in the uniformly local Yudovich space $Y^θ_{ul}(\mathbb{R}^2)$. We show that if $θ$ grows sufficiently slowly, then for every $T>0$, solutions of the Navier-Stokes equation converge to solutions of the Euler equation in $L^{\infty}([0,T];L^2_{ul}(\mathbb{R}^2))$ as $ν\to 0^+$, provided they are generated from the same initial data.

</details>


### [73] [An Aleksandrov estimate for kinetic equations with rough coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13921)
*Amĺie Loher,Connor Mooney,Clément Mouhot,Lukas Niebel,Luis Silvestre,Yuzhe Zhu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: An expository account of the Astra proof of an Aleksandrov estimate for kinetic equations with rough coefficients, also explained in simpler elliptic and parabolic settings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Aleksandrov estimates are important tools in the regularity theory of PDEs; extending them to kinetic equations with rough coefficients is challenging, and a new proof by Astra motivates a clear exposition.

Method: Expository presentation of Astra's proof, illustrated first in the simpler elliptic and parabolic settings before the kinetic case.

Result: A self-contained explanation of the Astra proof for the Aleksandrov estimate in the kinetic rough-coefficient setting, with elliptic and parabolic analogues.

Conclusion: The exposition clarifies Astra's technique and situates it within the broader context of elliptic, parabolic, and kinetic regularity theory.

Abstract: We give an exposition of a proof, discovered by Astra, of an Aleksandrov estimate for kinetic equations with rough coefficients. We explain this method in the simpler elliptic and parabolic settings as well.

</details>


### [74] [A Riesz-Map Preconditioned Extragradient Method for Price Formation in Mean Field Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13934)
*Yeva Gevorgyan,Diogo Gomes*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A projected extragradient method preconditioned by a Riesz map solves a 1D first-order price-formation mean field game, with mesh-independent convergence on fixed grids and first-order price/value errors under refinement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The MFG couples HJB and transport equations via a global market-clearing constraint, and the localized admissible set lacks cocoercivity in every equivalent Hilbert metric, ruling out simpler fixed-point schemes. The Riesz-map preconditioning avoids adding higher-order regularization to the equations.

Method: Riesz-map preconditioned projected extragradient method. The Riesz map uses L2 norms for density/price and a space-time H1 norm for value-function directions. For H=p^2/2, a monotone upwind discretization with adjoint-consistent transport and state-constraint boundary closure is used, plus exact metric projection onto bounded convex admissible sets.

Result: Mesh-independent Lipschitz bound on bounded convex admissible sets; convergence to the discrete variational inequality solution on each fixed grid with a step-size threshold independent of mesh sizes. On a quadratic benchmark, all runs converge with one fixed step size across a 16x mesh reduction. Price and value errors (away from low-density regions) decay ~first order; density errors decay more slowly.

Conclusion: The method is provably convergent per fixed grid with mesh-independent step size, but convergence under mesh refinement is not proved. Numerics suggest near-first-order accuracy for price and value, with weaker density convergence.

Abstract: We develop and analyze a projected extragradient method, preconditioned by a Riesz map, for a first-order price-formation mean field game in one space dimension. The model couples Hamilton--Jacobi--Bellman and transport equations through a global market-clearing constraint. Finite propagation justifies truncating the first-order transport equation to a bounded interval. A monotone weak formulation incorporates the mixed initial--terminal data. Its Riesz map uses $L^2$ norms for density and price directions and a space-time $H^1$ norm for value-function directions, preconditioning the residual without adding higher-order regularization terms to the MFG equations. A density-only argument rules out cocoercivity on the localized admissible set in every equivalent Hilbert metric, motivating extragradient updates. For $H(x,p)=p^2/2$, we use a monotone upwind discretization with adjoint-consistent transport and a state-constraint boundary closure. We establish a mesh-independent Lipschitz bound on bounded convex admissible sets with uniform density, slope, and price bounds. With exact metric projection onto these sets, the iteration converges to a solution of the discrete variational inequality on each fixed grid, with an admissible step-size threshold independent of both mesh sizes. Convergence under mesh refinement is not proved. On a quadratic benchmark, all reported runs reach the stopping tolerance with one fixed step size across a factor-of-16 reduction in both mesh sizes. Price errors and value-function errors measured away from low-density regions decay approximately at first order under refinement, while density errors decrease more slowly.

</details>


### [75] [Removing Boundary Smoothness from the Hot-Spots Conclusion in the Simple-Eigenvalue Case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12622)
*Krzysztof Burdzy*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Reduces a smooth-domain theorem on first positive Neumann eigenfunctions (no interior critical points) to a non-strict hot-spots result on general simply connected planar domains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extend the smooth-domain hot-spots-type result to arbitrary bounded simply connected planar domains lacking boundary regularity.

Method: Approximation by exhaustion with conformal images of concentric disks; uses a reflection extension, conformal invariance of Dirichlet energy, Sobolev compactness, and a truncation argument for square integrability.

Result: Spectral convergence under the exhaustion is established, yielding a non-strict hot-spots conclusion; the approximation argument is proved in full.

Conclusion: The smooth-domain theorem (an external input) transfers to general planar domains; the paper was generated by ChatGPT with author prompts.

Abstract: We give a reduction from a smooth-domain theorem excluding interior critical points of first positive Neumann eigenfunctions to a non-strict hot-spots conclusion on arbitrary bounded simply connected planar domains with compact Neumann resolvent and simple first positive eigenvalue. The main analytic step is spectral convergence under exhaustion by conformal images of concentric disks. A reflection extension, conformal invariance of Dirichlet energy, and compactness of the Sobolev embedding give a direct proof. A truncation argument justifies square integrability of the transferred extensions without any boundary regularity. The smooth-domain theorem proved by OpenAI is an external input; the approximation argument is proved here in full. The paper was generated by ChatGPT with prompts from the listed author.

</details>


### [76] [A Framework for Arbitrary-Order Electromagnetic Field Approximation Based on Analytical Solutions of Maxwell's Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12682)
*David Wei Ge*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proposes a Time-Space Theorem extending the Yee FDTD correspondence between temporal derivatives and spatial curls to arbitrary orders, yielding an arbitrary-order framework for electromagnetic field approximation, validated by an arbitrary-order FDTD implementation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The standard Yee FDTD algorithm is limited to replacing first-order temporal derivatives with spatial curls, restricting it to low-order accuracy. There is a need for higher-accuracy electromagnetic computation, motivating a generalization of this relation to arbitrary orders.

Method: A Time-Space Theorem establishes the correspondence between higher-order temporal derivatives and successively higher-order spatial curl operators. This yields an arbitrary-order framework that builds higher temporal orders via higher curl orders and supports different spatial discretizations and curl-estimation techniques. An arbitrary-order FDTD implementation is constructed using staggered time and space stepping with recursive curl estimations.

Result: Numerical simulations of an initial-value problem were run for temporal approximation orders from second to tenth order with fixed spatial and temporal step sizes. Comparison with the analytical solution shows errors decrease rapidly and monotonically as approximation order increases.

Conclusion: The framework demonstrates practical potential for high-accuracy electromagnetic computation and provides a basis for further development of arbitrary-order numerical methods.

Abstract: The standard Yee finite-difference time-domain (FDTD) algorithm uses Maxwell's equations to replace first order temporal derivatives with spatial curls. In this work, the relation is extended to arbitrary orders: a Time-Space Theorem establishes the correspondence between higher-order temporal derivatives and successively higher-order spatial curl operators. Based on this relation, an arbitrary-order framework for electromagnetic field approximations is developed. The framework constructs higher temporal orders through higher curl orders and permits different spatial discretizations and curl-estimation techniques, thereby providing a general basis for developing numerical algorithms of arbitrary orders. As one implementation, an arbitrary-order FDTD algorithm is constructed using staggered time and space stepping together with recursive curl estimations. Numerical simulations of an initial-value problem are performed for temporal approximation orders from second to tenth order while the spatial and temporal step sizes are kept fixed. Comparisons with the analytical solution show that the errors decrease rapidly and monotonically with increasing approximation order. These results demonstrate the practical potential of the framework for high-accuracy electromagnetic computation and provide a basis for further development of arbitrary-order numerical methods.

</details>


### [77] [An exact Delaunay flux identity for compact equilibria in the liquid drop model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12689)
*Averkios Averkiou,Manuel del Pino,Monica Musso*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper evaluates a Lyapunov-Schmidt solvability coefficient for compact liquid drop equilibria from Delaunay unduloids, proves its positivity and monotonicity, and extends the equilibria construction to all sufficiently large masses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: A prior construction (Arch. Ration. Mech. Anal. 249 (2025), Paper No. 74) built compact liquid drop equilibria from Delaunay unduloids but left a conjectured positivity of a coefficient in the final solvability condition unresolved, limiting the construction's range.

Method: Revisit the Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction and compute the solvability coefficient exactly, identifying it as the product of the axial period and the normalized Delaunay flux; then use period monotonicity and exact volume–surface-area relations for one period.

Result: The coefficient is proven positive and its product with the period strictly increases with the neck radius; the volume and surface area of one period satisfy an exact relation and both strictly decrease with neck radius.

Conclusion: The construction extends throughout the embedded unduloid family, yielding compact equilibria of every sufficiently large mass, with reference neck radii converging to any prescribed interior value as mass tends to infinity.

Abstract: We revisit the construction of compact liquid drop equilibria from Delaunay unduloids in [Arch. Ration. Mech. Anal. 249 (2025), Paper No. 74]. We evaluate exactly the coefficient arising in the final solvability condition of the Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction, identifying it as the product of the axial period and the normalised Delaunay flux. Using this identity and the monotonicity of the period, we establish the conjectured positivity of the coefficient and prove that its product with the period strictly increases with the neck radius. We also derive an exact relation between the volume and the surface area of one period and prove that both strictly decrease with the neck radius. These results extend the construction throughout the embedded unduloid family and yield compact equilibria of every sufficiently large mass, with reference neck radii converging to any prescribed interior value as the mass tends to infinity.

</details>


### [78] [Stability threshold of the Couette flow in 2D Navier-Stokes Boussinesq equations on $\mathbb{T} \times [-1, 1]$ and $\mathbb{R} \times [-1, 1]$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12810)
*Nangao Zhang,Gengwei Zhong,Ruizhao Zi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the transition threshold problem for the 2D Navier-Stokes Boussinesq equations around Couette flow with constant background temperature on T×[-1,1] and R×[-1,1], establishing global stability and enhanced dissipation/inviscid damping estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The transition threshold problem for the 2D Navier-Stokes Boussinesq equations around Couette flow with constant background temperature—particularly understanding how the coupling between vorticity and temperature affects the stability threshold—motivates this study in two domains.

Method: The authors use a quasi-linear method combined with energy estimates to overcome difficulties arising from the coupling in the equations.

Result: If the initial perturbations of the vorticity and temperature in Sobolev spaces are of sizes ν^(1/3) and ν^(5/6) respectively, the system admits a globally stable solution. Enhanced dissipation estimates for the vorticity and temperature, and inviscid damping estimate for the velocity, are derived.

Conclusion: The 2D Navier-Stokes Boussinesq equations around Couette flow with constant background temperature are globally stable under perturbations of sizes ν^(1/3) (vorticity) and ν^(5/6) (temperature), with enhanced dissipation and inviscid damping established despite coupling-induced challenges.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the transition threshold problem for the 2D Navier-Stokes Boussinesq equations around the Couette flow with a constant background temperature in two domains: $\mathbb{T} \times [-1, 1]$ and $\mathbb{R} \times [-1, 1]$.
  It was proved that if the sizes of initial perturbations of the vorticity and the temperature in Sobolev spaces are $ν^{\fr13}$ and $ν^{\fr56}$, respectively, then this system admits a globally stable solution.
  Besides, we derive the enhanced dissipation estimate for the vorticity and temperature, as well as the inviscid damping estimate for the velocity. The coupling in the equations poses some difficulties to the analysis. We overcome these challenges and establish the results via the quasi-linear method and energy estimates.

</details>


### [79] [Precise propagation rates of the fractional Fisher-KPP equation with free boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12838)
*Huyuan Chen,Yihong Du,Wenjie Ni*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper determines the exact asymptotic propagation rates of the fractional Fisher-KPP equation with free boundaries, showing distinct regimes depending on the fractional exponent s: sublinear/self-similar for s<1/2, t ln t for s=1/2, and linear for s>1/2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Fisher-KPP equation with free boundaries and fractional diffusion was previously shown to be well-posed and to exhibit a spreading-vanishing dichotomy, but the precise propagation speed of the expanding population range remained unknown. This paper aims to fill that gap.

Method: The authors analyze the fractional operator (-Δ)^s (0<s<1) on a growing interval (g(t), h(t)). They construct delicate upper and lower solutions: for s∈(0,1/2] using a smooth profile function, and for s∈(1/2,1) using the semi-wave profile. This yields sharp growth estimates for the free boundaries.

Result: When spreading occurs, the boundaries propagate as: h(t)/t^{1/(2s)} → (2^{1-2s}μ/(1-2s))^{1/(2s)} for s∈(0,1/2); h(t)/(t ln t) → μ for s=1/2; and h(t)/t → c_0(s) for s∈(1/2,1), where c_0(s) is uniquely given by the semi-wave problem. The level sets of u propagate at the same rates.

Conclusion: The propagation rate of the fractional Fisher-KPP free-boundary model depends qualitatively on the exponent s, transitioning from self-similar (s<1/2) to logarithmic (s=1/2) to linear (s>1/2) growth, with the level sets matching the boundary speeds.

Abstract: In this paper, we determine the propagation rates of the Fisher-KPP equation with free boundaries and fractional diffusion $(-Δ)^s$ $(0<s<1)$, which was considered in \cite{CDN26} as a model for species spreading, where the density function $u(t,x)$ satisfies the fractional Fisher-KPP equation $u_t+(-Δ)^su=f(u)$ over a one-dimensional spatial domain $(g(t), h(t))$, representing the expanding population range, with $x=g(t)$ and $x=h(t)$ the free boundaries. It was shown in \cite{CDN26} that the model is well-posed, and its long-time dynamics exhibits a spreading-vanishing dichotomy. We show in this paper that, when spreading occurs,
  \[ \begin{cases} \lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{h(t)}{t^{1/(2s)}} =\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{-g(t)}{t^{1/(2s)}} =\left(\frac{2^{1-2s}μ}{1-2s}\right)^{1/(2s)} & \mbox{ if $s\in(0,1/2)$,}\\[2mm] \lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{h(t)}{t\ln t} =\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{-g(t)}{t\ln t} =μ& \mbox{ if $s=1/2$,}\\[2mm] \lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{h(t)}{t} =\lim_{t\to\infty}\frac{-g(t)}{t} =c_0(s) & \mbox{ if $s\in(1/2,1)$}, \end{cases} \] where $c_0(s)\in (0,\infty)$ is uniquely determined by the associated semi-wave problem. The level sets of $u(t,x)$ propagate with the same rates as the free boundaries. The proof uses delicate upper and lower solutions constructed from a smooth profile function in the case $s\in (0, 1/2]$, and from the semi-wave profile when $s\in (1/2, 1)$.

</details>


### [80] [Global Well-Posedness and Energy Decay for an Abstract Viscoelastic Wave Equation with Nonlocal or Friction Damping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12846)
*Jun Zhou,Teng Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Studies an abstract viscoelastic wave equation with velocity-norm-power damping (friction or nonlocal) plus integral viscoelastic damping; proves well-posedness, uniqueness for exponents > -1, and exponential/polynomial energy decay.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Nonlocal damping with negative exponents and its combination with viscoelastic damping have not been studied in an abstract framework; this work fills that gap.

Method: Combines monotone operator theory with Faedo-Galerkin for well-posedness; handles singular range (-1, 0) via monotonicity instead of Lipschitz continuity; uses integral inequalities, multiplier techniques, and Nakao's method for decay.

Result: Global well-posedness in H1; uniqueness for all exponents > -1; energy decays exponentially in the friction case and polynomially in the nonlocal case, with explicit estimates.

Conclusion: First work to combine viscoelastic and nonlocal damping in an abstract setting, and first to analyze nonlocal damping with negative exponents.

Abstract: This paper investigates an abstract viscoelastic wave equation in a separable Hilbert space, in which the damping term depends on a power of the velocity norm. Depending on whether the power exponent is zero or nonzero, this damping corresponds to friction damping or nonlocal damping, respectively; the equation also contains a viscoelastic damping represented by an integral term with a relaxation function. We establish global well-posedness for initial data in \(\mathcal{H}_1\) by combining monotone operator theory with the Faedo--Galerkin method, and prove uniqueness for all exponents greater than \(-1\), with the singular range between \(-1\) and \(0\) handled through the monotonicity rather than the Lipschitz continuity of the nonlocal damping operator. We then study the long-time behavior of the energy via integral inequalities, multiplier techniques, and Nakao's method, and obtain explicit decay estimates showing that the energy decays exponentially in the friction case and polynomially in the nonlocal case. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first work to consider the combined effects of viscoelastic damping and nonlocal damping in such an abstract framework, and it is also the first to analyze nonlocal damping with negative exponents.

</details>


### [81] [Localized Generative Design of Ship Hulls with Diffusion Models and High-Fidelity Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12870)
*Hadi Keramati,Mohammed A. Hannan,Patrick Kirchen,Sina Tousi,Joonas Riekkinen,Jim Pyra,Rajeev K. Jaiman*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A reward-directed diffusion framework for localized hull optimization is presented and evaluated with viscous free-surface CFD, revealing that empirical screening misses speed-dependent resistance tradeoffs and that the representation/objective cannot capture a faired bulbous bow or viscous pressure and stability effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Practical ship design requires localized refinement and rigorous performance evaluation, but generative hull optimization often relies on empirical screening that may fail to reflect true hydrodynamic performance across operating conditions.

Method: Reward-directed diffusion with parametric masking (limited to bulbous bow and forebody while preserving hull continuity), surrogate-assisted sequential Monte Carlo sampling targeting reduced calm-water resistance, stability via training data and displacement matching during screening, plus an automated viscous free-surface CFD pipeline validated on two benchmark container ships.

Result: At matched KCS displacement, the generated variant has comparable total resistance at Fn=0.195 but 45-50% higher resistance at Fn=0.250 and 0.260, missed by empirical screening. GenHull E achieves 6-8% lower total resistance up to Fn=0.240 but higher resistance above Fn=0.250, where opposing friction and pressure trends reverse gains.

Conclusion: Aggregating resistance can conceal tradeoffs visible only when friction and pressure are separated. The assessment exposes an inability to represent a faired bulbous bow and a reward lacking viscous pressure and stability terms, showing how high-fidelity assessment can guide improved representations and objectives for physically realizable generative designs.

Abstract: Practical ship design requires localized refinement and performance evaluation. This paper presents a reward-directed diffusion framework for localized hull optimization. Parametric masking during dataset generation restricts geometric changes to selected variables, including the bulbous bow and forebody, while preserving full-hull continuity. Surrogate-assisted sequential Monte Carlo sampling targets reduced predicted calm-water resistance, with stability retained through training data and displacement matched during screening. An automated viscous free-surface CFD pipeline, validated against measurements for two benchmark container ships, evaluates a generated variant and GenHull E, a hull refined by a naval architect within the same representation. At matched KRISO Container Ship (KCS) displacement, the generated variant has comparable total resistance at $F_n=0.195$ but 45-50% higher resistance at $F_n=0.250$ and $0.260$, missed by empirical screening. GenHull E achieves 6-8% lower total resistance up to $F_n=0.240$ but higher resistance above $F_n=0.250$ as opposing friction and pressure trends reverse the gains. Aggregating resistance across this crossover can conceal tradeoffs that emerge when friction and pressure components are evaluated separately. The assessment reveals an inability to represent a faired bulbous bow and a reward lacking viscous pressure and stability terms. These findings show how high-fidelity assessment informs improved representations and objectives for physically realizable generative designs.

</details>


### [82] [Fourier--Laplace Injectivity for Oblique and Polygonal Cylinders in Passive Inverse Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12983)
*Yan Jiang,Hongyu Liu,Tianhao Ni,Kai Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies Fourier--Laplace transforms on null phases in passive inverse problems, establishing injectivity and stability estimates on oblique/polygonal cylinders, then applies them to passive wave and heat equations in arbitrary dimension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Passive inverse problems require understanding what boundary data determine about unknown interior quantities (e.g., initial data divided by speed squared or thermal conductivity). The goal is to characterize uniqueness and to quantify reconstruction stability, especially when only weighted generalized boundary data over a finite time interval [0,T] are available.

Method: Fourier--Laplace transforms on null phases are analyzed. For codimension-one density dependence in oblique cylinders, integration in the distinguished direction reduces data to an entire transform on a real-analytic maximally totally real submanifold, yielding injectivity via holomorphic uniqueness. For polygonal cylinders, polynomial density in transverse variables is handled by a weighted Brion--Barvinok expansion separating vertex and piece terms, giving finite-dimensional Lipschitz estimates at fixed polynomial degree. These results are applied to passive wave and heat equations, using weighted generalized boundary data to recover harmonic moments of the relevant quotient up to a weighted terminal residual.

Result: Equality of Laplace-domain boundary data determines the initial data divided by speed squared (acoustic) and by thermal conductivity (parabolic). Weighted generalized boundary data on [0,T] determine harmonic moments of the quotient up to the weighted terminal residual, yielding a Hölder estimate in an oblique cylinder and a Lipschitz estimate in a polygonal cylinder. The reconstruction estimate separates measurement noise, density approximation error, geometric error, and the condition number of the finite sampling matrix.

Conclusion: The transform framework provides injectivity and stability for passive inverse problems. Stability transitions from Hölder (oblique cylinders) to Lipschitz (polygonal cylinders) under polynomial density assumptions, and continuous settings are approximated by polygonal curves and piecewise transverse polynomials with an explicitly decomposed error bound.

Abstract: We study Fourier--Laplace transforms on null phases arising in passive inverse problems. Densities with codimension-one dependence in oblique cylinders are first considered. Integration in the distinguished direction reduces the data to an entire transform on a real-analytic maximally totally real submanifold. Holomorphic uniqueness then gives injectivity. A fixed transverse polytope swept along a polygonal curve, forming a polygonal cylinder, is next considered. The density is assumed to be polynomial in the material transverse variables on each swept piece. A weighted Brion--Barvinok expansion separates the vertex terms and the successive pieces. The same representation gives a finite-dimensional Lipschitz estimate when the polynomial degree is fixed.
  We apply these transform results to passive wave and heat equations in arbitrary dimension. The initial state is zero outside the observation domain. Equality of the Laplace-domain boundary data determines the quantity of the initial data divided by the speed squared in the acoustic model and the quantity of the initial data divided by the coefficient of thermal conductivity in the parabolic model. The question of what remains true when only weighted generalized boundary data on a finite interval \([0,T]\) are available is then studied. These data determine the harmonic moments of the quotient up to the weighted terminal residual. This identity gives a Hölder estimate in an oblique cylinder and a Lipschitz estimate in a polygonal cylinder. Finally, a continuous generating curve is approximated by a polygonal curve and a continuous material density by piecewise transverse polynomials. The resulting reconstruction estimate separates measurement noise, density approximation error, geometric error, and the condition number of the finite sampling matrix.

</details>


### [83] [Sharp Sobolev inequalities on minimal immersions via optimal transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13052)
*Zhe-Feng Xu,Zhuo-Nan Zhu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The optimal $L^p$-Sobolev constant on every complete $n$-dimensional Euclidean minimal immersion without boundary equals the Euclidean Aubin–Talenti constant (for $2\leq p<n$).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establishing sharp Sobolev constants on curved/manifold settings is difficult because curvature and geometry generally degrade constants relative to the Euclidean benchmark. This paper aims to show that minimality is strong enough to preserve the sharp Euclidean (Aubin–Talenti) constant.

Method: The proof uses optimal transport to a suitably chosen target space, combined with a two-kernel comparison argument that relates the tangential transport moment to deficits appearing in the normal-fibre estimates and the Jacobian estimates.

Result: For $2\leq p<n$, the optimal $L^p$-Sobolev constant on every complete $n$-dimensional Euclidean minimal immersion without boundary is exactly the Euclidean Aubin–Talenti constant.

Conclusion: Minimal immersions attain the optimal Euclidean Sobolev constant, confirming that the Aubin–Talenti constant is sharp even in this geometric setting.

Abstract: We prove that, for $2\leq p<n$, the optimal $L^p$-Sobolev constant on every complete $n$-dimensional Euclidean minimal immersion without boundary is the Euclidean Aubin--Talenti constant. The proof combines optimal transport to a suitably chosen target with a two-kernel comparison linking the tangential transport moment to deficits in the normal-fibre and Jacobian estimates.

</details>


### [84] [Qualitative-to-quantitative regularity for degenerate equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13137)
*Aelson Sobral,Eduardo V. Teixeira,José Miguel Urbano*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes a principle converting qualitative regularity into uniform interior estimates for degenerate equations, applying it to the p-Laplace, infinity-harmonic, and nonlocal superconductivity equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Optimal qualitative and quantitative gradient Hölder exponents are believed to coincide, but a general principle linking qualitative regularity (without uniform seminorm bounds) to uniform quantitative estimates for degenerate equations was lacking.

Method: The proofs combine compactness arguments for shifted equations with an improvement-of-flatness iteration at variable scales.

Result: For the p-Laplace equation (p>2), local C^{1,γ} regularity of all solutions implies uniform C^{1,β} estimates for 0<β<γ, so optimal exponents coincide as suprema. In the plane, estimates are controlled by the L^p norm below the sharp Iwaniec–Manfredi threshold; applied to Xu's C^{1,1/3} result, uniform C^{1,β} estimates for 0<β<1/3 follow. For the nonlocal superconductivity equation |Du|(-Δ)^s u=0 (1/2<s<1), pointwise differentiability with integrable tails implies uniform interior C^{1,α} estimates for bounded solutions for some α>0.

Conclusion: Qualitative regularity can be systematically upgraded to uniform quantitative estimates for degenerate equations, unifying optimal exponents and yielding new uniform estimates for planar infinity-harmonic and nonlocal superconductivity equations.

Abstract: We establish a principle that converts qualitative regularity into uniform interior estimates for degenerate equations. For the $p$-Laplace equation, $p>2$, we prove that local $C^{1,γ}$ regularity of every solution, without any uniform bound on the corresponding seminorms, implies uniform $C^{1,β}$ estimates for every $0<β<γ$. Thus, the optimal qualitative and quantitative gradient Hölder exponents coincide as suprema in every dimension. In the plane, this yields estimates controlled by the $L^p$ norm of the solution for every exponent below the sharp Iwaniec--Manfredi threshold. Applied to Xu's recent qualitative $C^{1,1/3}$ regularity for planar infinity-harmonic functions, we obtain uniform interior $C^{1,β}$ estimates for every $0<β<1/3$. For the nonlocal superconductivity equation $|Du|(-Δ)^s u=0$, with $1/2<s<1$, we prove that pointwise differentiability of all solutions with integrable tails implies uniform interior $C^{1,α}$ estimates for bounded solutions, for some $α>0$. The proofs combine compactness for shifted equations with an improvement-of-flatness iteration at variable scales.

</details>


### [85] [Strict Dirichlet Pólya Inequality for Simply Connected Planar Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13139)
*Quanyu Tang,Zuoqin Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves the strict Dirichlet Pólya inequality for bounded simply connected planar domains by connecting Dirichlet resonances to unitary eigenphase crossings via the Dirichlet-to-Neumann map.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Dirichlet Pólya inequality is a long-standing spectral geometry problem; establishing it strictly (not just asymptotically) for general bounded simply connected planar domains was an open objective.

Method: Uses a unitary boundary transport formulation of the Helmholtz equation, relates Dirichlet resonances to unitary eigenphase crossings through the Dirichlet-to-Neumann map; employs an exact area identity for the sharp Weyl coefficient and a Ky Fan estimate, then extends by conformal exhaustion.

Result: The strict Dirichlet Pólya inequality is established on every bounded simply connected planar domain; the sharp Weyl coefficient follows from an exact area identity, and a strictly positive remainder remains in the counting estimate.

Conclusion: The strict Dirichlet Pólya inequality holds for all bounded simply connected planar domains, with sharp asymptotics and a positive remainder in the counting estimate.

Abstract: We establish the strict Dirichlet Pólya inequality on every bounded simply connected planar domain. The proof uses a unitary boundary transport formulation of the Helmholtz equation and relates Dirichlet resonances to unitary eigenphase crossings through the Dirichlet-to-Neumann map. An exact area identity yields the sharp Weyl coefficient, while a Ky Fan estimate leaves a strictly positive remainder in the counting estimate. The general case follows by conformal exhaustion.

</details>


### [86] [Remarks on temperature-dependent Cahn--Hilliard equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13140)
*Mi-Ho Giga,Yoshikazu Giga,Naoto Kajiwara,Kazuyuki Tsuda*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Reviews derivations of temperature-dependent Cahn-Hilliard equations (mixed-order systems) and establishes local-in-time well-posedness via L^p-maximal regularity, plus equilibrium stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Temperature-dependent Cahn-Hilliard equations arise from various modeling derivations, but their mathematical analysis—particularly well-posedness of the resulting mixed-order systems—needs systematic treatment.

Method: Recalls several derivations of temperature-dependent Cahn-Hilliard equations; identifies a suitable subclass of mixed-order systems; applies L^p-maximal regularity theory to prove local-in-time well-posedness; analyzes stability of equilibria.

Result: Local-in-time well-posedness is established for a suitable subclass of the derived equations, and stability of equilibria is discussed.

Conclusion: Temperature-dependent Cahn-Hilliard equations form mixed-order systems whose well-posedness can be handled via L^p-maximal regularity, with equilibrium stability characterized for a subclass.

Abstract: We recall several derivations of temperature-dependent Cahn-Hilliard equations. The resulting equations are systems of mixed order. For a suitable subclass of equations we discuss its local-in-time well-posedness based on $L^p$-maximal regularity theory. We also discuss stability of equilibria.

</details>


### [87] [Stability of composite waves consisting of a non-classical Liu shock and a rarefaction wave for a non-convex viscoelastic system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13151)
*Feimin Huang,Yi Wang,Jian Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves time-asymptotic stability of composite waves (a non-classical Liu shock plus a rarefaction wave from different characteristic families) in a 1D non-convex viscoelastic system with cubic stress-strain relation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Composite waves involving non-classical Liu shocks had not previously been shown to be time-asymptotically stable, especially when the shock and rarefaction wave come from two different characteristic families in a non-convex physical system.

Method: Uses careful time-dependent shift functions for the viscous shock and suitable weight functions for the relative entropy, combined with the a-contraction method. Handles both nearly-degenerate and fully non-degenerate cases with different choices of shift and weight functions.

Result: Establishes time-asymptotic stability of the composite of a viscous Liu shock and a rarefaction wave for the 1D viscoelastic system with cubic non-convex stress-strain relation. The Liu shock may be degenerate or non-degenerate.

Conclusion: This is the first time-asymptotic stability result for composite waves consisting of both a non-classical Liu shock and a rarefaction wave from two different characteristic families in a non-convex physical system.

Abstract: We investigate the time-asymptotic stability of composite waves consisting of a non-classical Liu shock and a rarefaction wave for non-convex viscoelastic system. By choosing delicate time-dependent shift functions to the viscous shock and suitable weight functions for the relative entropy, we succeed in proving the time-asymptotic stability of the composite of a viscous Liu shock and a rarefaction wave from two different characteristic families for the one-dimensional viscoelastic system with a cubic non-convex stress-strain relation by $a$-contraction method. Here the non-classical Liu shock can be either degenerate or non-degenerate, which can be seen as the system version of the scalar Oleinik shock. Note that both the shift and the weight functions are chosen quite differently in the two cases of nearly degenerate and fully non-degenerate. The paper represents the first time-asymptotic stability result for the composite waves consisting of both non-classical Liu shock and rarefaction wave from two different families for the non-convex physical system.

</details>


### [88] [Minimizing tangent maps into complex projective space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13214)
*Matilde Gianocca,André Guerra,David Ziener*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper classifies nonconstant smooth harmonic maps from S² and S³ into CPⁿ whose 0-homogeneous extensions are minimizing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding which harmonic maps are energy-minimizing is a central problem; the 0-homogeneous extension provides a natural setting to study minimization.

Method: Classification via analysis of 0-homogeneous extensions and minimizing properties of harmonic maps into CPⁿ.

Result: Minimizing maps S²→CPⁿ are isometric embeddings; minimizing maps S³→CPⁿ are holomorphic isometric embeddings composed with a Hopf map.

Conclusion: A complete classification is obtained: the minimizing maps are exactly isometric embeddings (S²) or holomorphic isometric embeddings composed with a Hopf map (S³).

Abstract: We classify all nonconstant smooth harmonic maps $\mathbb{S}^2\to \mathbb{CP}^n$ and $\mathbb{S}^3\to \mathbb{CP}^n$ whose 0-homogeneous extensions are minimizing: they are, respectively, isometric embeddings or holomorphic isometric embeddings composed with a Hopf map.

</details>


### [89] [Gradient rearrangement estimates for singular and degenerate anisotropic $p$-Laplace equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13226)
*Lu Chen,Daniel Hauer,Yabo Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes global pointwise estimates on rearrangements of SOLAs and their gradients for anisotropic p-Laplace Poisson equations with L1 data, covering both Dirichlet and Neumann conditions, extending rearrangement theory to the singular range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing rearrangement theory (Cianchi-Maz'ya) covers the degenerate range 2≤p<n but leaves the singular range 1<p<2 unaddressed, where nonlinear interpolation methods are unavailable.

Method: Proves global pointwise estimates on decreasing rearrangements u* and [H(∇u)]* of SOLAs, decomposing the data for the singular range 1<p<2 and employing alternative techniques in place of nonlinear interpolation.

Result: Obtains anisotropic extension and full-interval completion of Cianchi-Maz'ya theory for 2≤p<n; new gradient rearrangement estimates for 1<p<2; endpoint weak-type gradient estimate for all 1<p<n; stability estimate for gradient differences for p*(n)<p<n where p*(n)=(3n-2)/(2n-1).

Conclusion: The results extend known rearrangement and stability estimates to the singular range p*(n)<p<2, completing the theory across the full interval 1<p<n.
