Abstract: In this paper, we prove global pointwise estimates on the decreasing rearrangement \(u^{\ast}\) and \([H(\nabla u)]^{\ast}\) of SOLAs \(u\) of an elliptic Poisson equation driven by the anisotropic \(p\)-Laplace operator \(Δ_p^H\) for given \(L^1\)-data and equipped with either homogeneous Dirichlet or Neumann boundary conditions. Here, we assume that the dimension \(n\ge 2\), \(1<p<n\) and \(H\) is norm on \(\R^n\). In the degenerate range \(2 \leq p <n\), this gives an anisotropic extension and a full-interval completion of the rearrangement theory of Cianchi-Maz'ya [JEMS, 2014]. In particular, we obtain a new rearrangement estimate for gradients by decomposing the data for singular range \(1<p<2\), where the nonlinear interpolation method aren't available. Moreover, the endpoint weak-type gradient estimate is obtained for all \(1<p<n\). In the range \(p_*(n)<p<n\) with \(p_*(n):=\frac{3n-2}{2n-1}\), we prove a stability estimate for differences of gradients. These results extends the known ones in the literature to the singular range \(p_*(n)<p<2\).

</details>


### [90] [Vector and matrix monotonicity characterisation of the "load forces" in nonlinear elasticity: compressible versus incompressible response](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13235)
*Jendrik Voss,Ionel-Dumitrel Ghiba,Robert J. Martin,Patrizio Neff*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper examines relationships between different notions of monotonicity for isotropic hyperelastic compressible and incompressible materials, defining monotonicity on the manifold Sym++(n)∩SL(n) and its spectral manifold, with a precise characterisation for hyperelastic materials.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Different notions of monotonicity exist for isotropic hyperelastic materials, and their relationships—particularly on the appropriate manifold of positive definite symmetric tensors with unit determinant—need clarification and a unified treatment.

Method: The authors formulate the monotonicity concept on the manifold Sym++(n)∩SL(n) of positive definite symmetric tensors with unit determinant, as well as on the corresponding spectral manifold S = {(λ1,...,λn) ∈ R^n_+ | λ1λ2···λn = 1}, and derive a precise characterisation of monotonicity for hyperelastic materials.

Result: A precise characterisation of monotonicity is obtained for hyperelastic materials, and the monotonicity properties of the Biot stress tensor T_Biot(U) (the engineering stress or load tensor) are considered.

Conclusion: The paper clarifies the relation between different monotonicity notions for compressible and incompressible isotropic hyperelastic materials, providing a rigorous framework on the relevant manifold and spectral manifold.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the relation between different notions of monotonicity for isotropic hyperelastic compressible and incompressible materials. We study how to define the monotonicity concept on the manifold $\rm{Sym}^{++}(n)\cap \rm{SL}(n)$ of positive definite symmetric tensors with unit determinant and the corresponding spectral manifold $\mathcal S=\left\{(λ_1,\dotsc,λ_n)\in \mathbb{R}^n_+\,\middle|\, λ_1λ_2\cdotsλ_n=1\right\}$, and give a precise characterisation of monotonicity in the case of hyperelastic materials. In particular, we consider the monotonicity properties of the Biot stress tensor $T_{\rm Biot}(U)$, the engineering stress, or the load tensor.

</details>


### [91] [Weighted derivative estimates for Kolmogorov equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13241)
*Artur Sidorenko,Vasilii Kolokoltsov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Explicit pointwise bounds on spatial derivatives of the classical Cauchy problem solution for diffusions with smooth coefficients and polynomially growing initial data, plus growth estimates for SDE derivatives w.r.t. initial data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establish explicit spatial derivative bounds for parabolic Cauchy problems with polynomially growing initial data, addressing the need for sharp estimates under smooth, linearly growing coefficients.

Method: Combines Lyapunov functions, Markov semigroups on weighted spaces, and Kunita's theory of differentiable dependence of SDE solutions on initial data.

Result: Explicit pointwise bounds on spatial derivatives of the classical solution and explicit growth estimates for derivatives of the SDE w.r.t. initial data.

Conclusion: The approach yields explicit derivative estimates by merging probabilistic (Lyapunov, Markov semigroups, Kunita) tools.

Abstract: For diffusions whose coefficients are smooth, with at most linear growth and bounded derivatives of higher order, we establish explicit pointwise bounds on the spatial derivatives of the classical solution to the Cauchy problem with polynomially growing initial data. We also obtain explicit growth estimates for the derivatives of the SDE with respect to the initial data. The proofs combine Lyapunov functions and Markov semigroups on weighted spaces with Kunita's theory of the differentiable dependence of SDE solutions on the initial data.

</details>


### [92] [Global Regular Solutions for One-Dimensional Degenerate Compressible Non-Resistive MHD Equations with Large Data and Far-Field Vacuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13248)
*Zhaoyang Shang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Global large-data existence theorems for a 1D transverse-field isentropic non-resistive MHD system with density-dependent viscosity μ(ρ)=αρ^δ (0<δ≤1) and vacuum at spatial infinity, using an invariant magnetic class via frozen-in flux.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extend prior Navier-Stokes results (Cao, Li, Zhu, SIAM J. Math. Anal. 2022) to MHD. The viscosity vanishes where density vanishes, and vacuum at spatial infinity makes the problem delicate; a suitable invariant framework is needed to control magnetic and density behavior.

Method: Study the Cauchy problem on the real line. Introduce an invariant class of magnetic fields described by the frozen-in flux h=b/ρ and its mass derivative k=ρ^{-1}h_x. Exploit the key commutation identity [∂_t+u∂_x, ρ^{-1}∂_x]=0 to transport h and k, eliminate negative powers of density from the Lorentz force, and propagate magnetic regularity. Use a modified effective-velocity entropy with an added coercive magnetic term (error controlled by k), plus magnetic pressure to strengthen the density upper bound.

Result: Establishes two global large-data theorems: for the sublinear range 0<δ<1 with γ≥δ+1/2, and the linear case δ=1 with γ>3/2. Estimates close the large-data continuation argument in both regimes.

Conclusion: The work provides global large-data well-posedness for the non-resistive MHD system in the stated parameter ranges, generalizing the Navier-Stokes results of Cao, Li, and Zhu to MHD.

Abstract: We consider the Cauchy problem on the real line for a one-dimensional transverse-field isentropic non-resistive magnetohydrodynamic system with viscosity $μ(ρ)=αρ^δ$, $0<δ\leq1$, and vacuum at spatial infinity. The viscosity therefore vanishes precisely where the density vanishes. We establish two global large-data theorems, one for the sublinear range $0<δ<1$ and one for the linear case $δ=1$, in a nontrivial invariant class of magnetic fields. The class is described by the frozen-in flux $h=b/ρ$ and its mass derivative $k=ρ^{-1}h_x$. The key identity $[\partial_t+u\partial_x,ρ^{-1}\partial_x]=0$ transports both $h$ and $k$, removes all negative powers of the density from the Lorentz force, and propagates the magnetic regularity. A modified effective-velocity entropy contains an additional coercive magnetic term, while its only error is controlled by $k$. Magnetic pressure also strengthens the density upper bound. These estimates close the large-data continuation argument in the ranges $0<δ<1$, $γ\geqδ+\tfrac12$, and $δ=1$, $γ>\tfrac32$. As a result, we extend the work of Cao, Li, and Zhu (SIAM J. Math. Anal., 2022) from the Navier-Stokes equations to the MHD equations.

</details>


### [93] [Loss of derivatives for hyperbolic operators with coefficients oscillating in time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13343)
*Wenhui Chen,Michael Reissig*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves energy estimates for second-order strictly hyperbolic operators with coefficients Lipschitz in space and oscillating near t=0, with logarithmic time-derivative bounds, yielding no loss for α=0 and stretched-logarithmic loss for 0<α<1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extends prior work by Colombini, Del Santo and Fanelli on spatially Lipschitz coefficients to time-derivative bounds that include an additional logarithmic factor, addressing oscillation near the initial time.

Method: Combines Tarama's energy with frequency-dependent weights to derive energy estimates.

Result: Obtains an energy estimate without loss for α=0 and with a stretched logarithmic upper bound for the loss when 0<α<1, implying arbitrarily small loss in the Sobolev scale.

Conclusion: The estimates extend previous analysis to time-derivative bounds with a logarithmic factor, showing arbitrarily small Sobolev-scale loss.

Abstract: We consider second-order strictly hyperbolic operators whose coefficients are Lipschitz continuous in space and may oscillate near the initial time. Their first two time derivatives are bounded by the corresponding powers of $\frac{1}{t}(\log\frac{1}{t})^α$ with $0\leqslantα<1$, and the first time derivative satisfies the corresponding spatial Lipschitz bound. By combining Tarama's energy with frequency-dependent weights, we prove an energy estimate without loss for $α=0$ and with a stretched logarithmic upper bound for the loss when $0<α<1$, which implies an arbitrarily small loss in the Sobolev scale. These estimates extend the analysis of Colombini, Del Santo and Fanelli [\emph{Ann. Scuola Norm. Sup. Pisa Cl. Sci. (5)} \textbf{26} (2025)] for spatially Lipschitz coefficients to time-derivative bounds with an additional logarithmic factor.

</details>


### [94] [Complex Monge-Amp{è}re equations with boundary values: a variational approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13478)
*Papa Badiane,Chinh H. Lu,Souhaibou Sambou,Ahmed Zeriahi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper introduces a new class E^1_Φ(Ω) of plurisubharmonic functions with boundary values equal to a maximal psh function Φ and finite Φ-energy, then characterizes which Borel measures are Monge-Ampère measures of such functions via a variational approach.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the solvability theory of the complex Monge-Ampère equation to unbounded boundary data given by a maximal plurisubharmonic function Φ on a bounded hyperconvex domain.

Method: Introduce the class E^1_Φ(Ω) of plurisubharmonic functions with Cegrell boundary values Φ and finite Φ-energy; use a variational approach to prove μ is a Monge-Ampère measure of u ∈ E^1_Φ(Ω) iff an integrability condition holds; establish a quantitative version; and apply a logarithmic transformation to real Monge-Ampère equations with unbounded boundary data.

Result: A Borel measure μ on Ω is the Monge-Ampère measure of a function u ∈ E^1_Φ(Ω) if and only if μ satisfies the given integrability condition. A quantitative version of this condition extends earlier results of Cegrell and others. Analogous results are obtained for real Monge-Ampère equations with unbounded boundary data.

Conclusion: The variational framework characterizes Monge-Ampère measures in the new Φ-energy class, generalizing prior work and extending to real Monge-Ampère equations with unbounded boundary data.

Abstract: Let $Ω$ ___ C n be a bounded hyperconvex domain and $Φ$\,: $Ω$ $\rightarrow$ R a maximal plurisubharmonic function in $Ω$ (possibly unbounded). We introduce a new class E 1 $Φ$ ($Ω$) of plurisubharmonic functions with boundary values equal to $Φ$ in the sense of Cegrell and finite $Φ$-energy on $Ω$. Then given a Borel measure $μ$ on $Ω$, we apply a variational approach to prove that $μ$ is the Monge-Amp{è}re measure of a function u $\in$ E 1 $Φ$ ($Ω$) if and only if it satisfies the integrability condition  We also establish a quantitative version of this condition extending previous results of Cegrell and others. Using a logarithmic transformation, we obtain analogous results for the real Monge-Amp{è}re equations with (unbounded) boundary data.

</details>


### [95] [An inverse spectral problem for the wave equation with a varying Robin parameter at the boundary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13629)
*Antti Hannukainen,Petr Kulikov,Matti Lassas,Lauri Oksanen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A two-step method recovers wave speed c(x) in a 1+1D wave equation from Neumann-Dirichlet eigenvalues and their Robin-parameter derivatives, using NtD reconstruction plus a regularized boundary control method with mixed L2/H1 penalties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Inverse spectral problems for the wave equation with variable speed are challenging; the goal is to reconstruct c(x) from spectral data (Neumann-Dirichlet eigenvalues and their derivatives w.r.t. a Robin parameter).

Method: Two-step procedure: (1) reconstruct the Neumann-to-Dirichlet map Λ from spectral data via arXiv/0406582; (2) feed Λ into the regularized boundary control method from arXiv:1509.04478. Regularization of control problems uses a weighted combination of L2 and H1 penalties, with a uniform convergence result for the optimal controls enabling simultaneous tuning of regularization parameters.

Result: The forward spectral map is proven Lipschitz continuous; the inverse spectral map is Hölder continuous on a subclass of wave speeds. Numerical experiments on smooth and piecewise smooth profiles show high accuracy and robustness.

Conclusion: The proposed two-step, regularized reconstruction is theoretically justified and numerically validated, offering accurate and robust recovery of wave speed from spectral data.

Abstract: An inverse spectral problem for a $(1+1)$-dimensional wave equation with wave speed $c(x)$ is considered. The spectral data consist of the eigenvalues of the Neumann--Dirichlet problem for the operator $-c^2(x)\partial_x^2$ together with their derivatives with respect to a Robin parameter at the boundary. We propose a two-step recovery procedure. In the first step, the Neumann-to-Dirichlet map $Λ$ is reconstructed from the spectral data using the results of arXiv/0406582. In the second step, the reconstructed $Λ$ is used within the regularization strategy for the boundary control method presented in arXiv:1509.04478. As an improvement, the regularization of the control problems employs a weighted combination of $L^2$ and $H^1$ penalty terms, and we prove a uniform convergence result for the family of optimal controls, thereby allowing the regularization parameters to be tuned simultaneously.
  We also prove that the forward spectral map, which assigns to a wave speed its spectral data, is Lipschitz continuous, and that the inverse spectral map underlying the reconstruction procedure is Hölder continuous on a subclass of wave speeds.
  The method is implemented numerically and validated on both smooth and piecewise smooth wave speed profiles. Numerical experiments demonstrate that the recovery of the wave speed exhibits high accuracy and robustness.

</details>


### [96] [Weighted Liouville Theorems and A Priori Bounds for Indefinite Superlinear Equations with Pucci's Maximal Operator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13486)
*Karan Rathore,Mohan Mallick,Ram Baran Verma*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes a uniform bound on positive solutions of a fully nonlinear equation with sign-changing weight, via a Liouville-type theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study positive solutions of $-\mathcal{λ,Λ}^{+}(D^{2}u)=μu+a(x)u^{p}$ where the weight $a$ may change sign and its zero set may have non-empty interior, requiring new tools.

Method: Prove a suitable Liouville-type theorem for $-\mathcal{λ,Λ}^{+}(D^{2}v)=h(x_{1})f(v)$, with $h$ a strictly increasing one-dimensional weight and $f$ positive and nondecreasing, then apply it to derive uniform bounds.

Result: A Liouville-type theorem is proven for the weighted equation, enabling a uniform bound on the set of positive solutions of the original problem.

Conclusion: The Liouville-type theorem provides a framework for bounding positive solutions of fully nonlinear equations with sign-changing weights.

Abstract: Consider \begin{equation*}\ \left\{ \begin{aligned} -\mathcal_{λ,Λ}^{+}(D^{2}u)&=μu+a(x)u^{p}\quad\text{in}~Ω,\\ u&=0\quad\text{on}~~\partialΩ, \end{aligned} \right. \end{equation*} where $ \ Omega$ is a smooth bounded domain, $a$ is a continuous function which is allowed to change sign. Moreover, the set of zeros of $a$ may have a non-empty interior. The main objective of this article is to establish a uniform bound on the set of positive solutions of the above problem. This approach requires a suitable Liouville-type theorem. We first prove the Liouville-type theorem for $-\mathcal_{λ,Λ}^{+}(D^{2}v)=h(x_{1})f(v),$ where \(h\) is a strictly increasing one-dimensional weight and \(f\) is positive and nondecreasing.

</details>


### [97] [Diffraction of waves by finite thin coatings: asymptotic expansion and effective models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13680)
*Cédric Baudet*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies the Helmholtz equation in a Dirichlet angular sector covered by a thin homogeneous layer, deriving asymptotic expansions of the solution as the layer thickness tends to 0, justified with error estimates, and building effective Robin/Ventcel boundary models with DtN conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Thin layers covering angular sectors appear in many applications, and their accurate modeling is challenging due to the complex singular behavior of fields near the corner. The paper aims to rigorously handle these singularities when the layer thickness vanishes.

Method: Matched asymptotic expansion with different expansions in three subdomains (corner vicinity, layer, outer domain) linked by matching conditions. A novel algebraic tool system (formal series and operators on them) handles the delicate corner matching. A Galerkin-type method enriched by complements at infinity computes the behavior at infinity of corner profiles. Effective models of order 3 (Robin) and 5 (Ventcel) with a DtN map based on modal decomposition are proposed.

Result: The asymptotic expansion is justified with error estimates and validated numerically up to order 5. Well-posedness of the effective models is proven, along with error estimates and numerical results.

Conclusion: The proposed matched asymptotic framework and effective Robin/Ventcel models, combined with DtN conditions accounting for corner singularities, provide accurate and well-posed approximations for thin-layer-covered angular sectors.

Abstract: We consider the Helmholtz equation in a Dirichlet angular sector (which may be locally perturbed) covered on one side by a thin homogeneous layer. First, we propose an asymptotic expansion of the solution at any order and for any angle when the thickness of the layer tends to 0, using a matched asymptotic expansion. This consists in introducing different asymptotic expansions in three subdomains -- the vicinity of the corner, the layer and the rest of the domain -- that are linked through matching conditions. The presence of the corner makes these matching conditions delicate to derive because the fields have complex singular behaviors. We overcome this difficulty using a novel system of algebraic tools, including formal series and operators on these series. Moreover, we justify the asymptotic expansion with error estimates and provide details on the form of the expansion depending on the angle.
  We numerically validate this expansion up to order 5. This requires computing the behavior at infinity of some corner profiles. To do this, we introduce a Galerkin-type method enriched by complements at infinity. These complements are known functions that appear in the behavior at infinity of the corner profiles with coefficients that become unknowns of the system.
  Finally, we propose effective models of order 3, resp. 5 where, far from the corner, the layer is replaced with an effective Robin, resp. Ventcel, boundary condition and, near the corner, an artificial boundary is introduced and a Dirichlet-to-Neumann (DtN) type condition is imposed. The DtN map relies on a modal decomposition of the truncated asymptotic expansion at order 3, resp. 5. The modes take into account the singular behavior of the expansion terms, the corner profiles and some algebraic matching coefficients. We prove the well-posedness of our effective models, as well as error estimates, and provide numerical results.

</details>


### [98] [Blocking of bistable front propagation: Application to Wolbachia population replacement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13497)
*Saoussen Latrach,Nicolas Vauchelet,Hatem Zaag*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Studies how spatial heterogeneity in environmental carrying capacity can block the spread of Wolbachia-driven mosquito population replacement, using a reduced single reaction-diffusion equation and numerical validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Mosquito-borne diseases like dengue lack vaccines, so targeting the mosquito population via Wolbachia introduction is attractive. Prior models prove spatial propagation of replacement in homogeneous environments, but real environments are heterogeneous, and blocking of this propagation is not understood.

Method: Reduces the original 2x2 reaction-diffusion system to a single closed equation to simplify analysis, then analyzes blocking of the propagation during population replacement under spatial variations in environmental carrying capacity. Numerical simulations validate the theoretical findings.

Result: Shows that spatial variations (significant changes) in environmental carrying capacity can block the propagation of the Wolbachia replacement; theoretical results confirmed by numerical simulations.

Conclusion: Spatial heterogeneity in carrying capacity can halt Wolbachia propagation, providing insight for designing control strategies in realistic non-homogeneous environments.

Abstract: In order to control epidemics of mosquito-borne diseases such as dengue, for which there is still no vaccine available, several strategies aim to directly target the mosquito population. One technique involves introducing a symbiotic bacterium called Wolbachia into the population, which blocks the transmission of pathogens. A mathematical model of the introduction of this bacterium and the replacement of the mosquito population with a population carrying this bacterium has been developed, proving that in a homogeneous environment, there is spatial propagation of the replacement. The objective of this paper is to study the blocking of this propagation in cases where the environment is not homogeneous. In particular, we are considering scenarios where the environmental carrying capacity varies significantly across different regions of the spatial domain. To achieve this objective, we first reduce the original 2x2 reaction-diffusion system to a single closed equation, providing a simplified framework for studying the dynamics of Wolbachia propagation. We then investigate the blocking of the propagation in the population replacement process, focusing on the effects of spatial variations in the environmental carrying capacity. Numerical simulations have been performed to validate the theoretical results.

</details>


### [99] [Short scale asymptotics in nonlocal spectral problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13513)
*Volodymyr Rybalko,Antonina Rybalko*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Asymptotic expansion of the first eigenvalues of nonlocal convolution-type operators as ε→0, distinguishing interior minima (harmonic-oscillator scale ε^{1/2}) from boundary minima (Airy-type scale ε^{2/3}).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the spectral asymptotics of nonlocal convolution-type operators L_ε when the potential a(x) attains a non-degenerate minimum, and to characterize how the location of that minimum (interior vs. boundary) affects eigenvalue expansions and eigenfunction localization.

Method: Blow-up (rescaling) analysis of the operator L_ε as ε→0, treating the nonlocal convolution kernel scaled by ε with continuous, fast-decaying J≥0. Separate treatments for minimum at interior point x0∈Ω versus boundary point x0∈∂Ω, using harmonic-oscillator and Airy-function approximations.

Result: If x0∈Ω interior: λ_ε^{(k)}=a(x0)−1+εμ^{(k)}+ō(ε), with μ^{(k)} harmonic-oscillator eigenvalues; eigenfunctions concentrate near x0 on scale ε^{1/2}. If x0∈∂Ω: λ_ε^{(k)}=a(x0)−1+ε^{2/3}Λ_0+ε(μ^{(k)}+αp_0)+ō(ε); eigenfunctions factor into a distance-dependent part (Airy, scale ε^{2/3}) and tangentially localized oscillator modes (scale ε^{1/2}); Λ_0, α, p_0 depend on J(|x|^2) and a(x).

Conclusion: The spectral asymptotics and eigenfunction localization of L_ε depend crucially on whether the minimum of a(x) lies in the interior or on the boundary: interior minima yield ε^{1/2}-localized oscillator modes, while boundary minima yield a two-scale structure with an Airy-type boundary layer of width ε^{2/3} combined with tangential ε^{1/2} oscillator modes.

Abstract: We study the asymptotic behavior of eigenvalues of nonlocal convolution-type operators $$L_\varepsilonρ=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\int_ΩJ\left({\frac{|x-y|^2}{\varepsilon^2}}\right)ρ(y)dy+a(x)ρ(x),$$ (with a continuous sufficiently fast decaying at infinity function $J\geq 0$) when the scaling parameter $\varepsilon>0$ tends to zero. Assuming that the minimum of the function $a(x)$ is attained at a point $x_0\in \overlineΩ\subset \mathbb{R}^d$ and is non-degenerate, we establish several terms in the asymptotic expansion of the first eigenvalues $λ_\varepsilon^{(k)}$ of $L_\varepsilon$. If $x_0$ is an interior point of the domain $Ω$, we show that $λ_\varepsilon^{(k)}=a(x_0)-1+\varepsilonμ^{(k)}+\overline{o}(\varepsilon)$, where $μ^{(k)}$ are eigenvalues of a harmonic oscillator (obtained by the blow up analysis), the corresponding eigenfunctions of $\mathcal{L}_\varepsilon$ are concentrated near $x_0$ on the scale $\varepsilon^{1/2}$. If $x_0\in\partial Ω$ then the asymptotic expansion of the eigenvalues of $L_\varepsilon$ has the form $λ_\varepsilon^{(k)}=a(x_0)-1+\varepsilon^{2/3}Λ_0+\varepsilon(μ^{(k)}+αp_0)+\overline{o}(\varepsilon)$. The corresponding eigenfunctions are represented as the product of a function depending on the distance to $\partialΩ$ and concentrated on the scale $\varepsilon^{2/3}$, and functions localized on the scale $\varepsilon^{1/2}$ and asymptotically independent of the distance to $\partialΩ$. The latter functions converge (after rescaling) to eigenfunctions of a harmonic oscillator (in tangential variables), $μ^{(k)}$ being their eigenvalues. The former function is given, to the leading term, by the scaled Airy function, and $Λ_0$, $α$ and $p_0$ depend on $J(|x|^2)$ and $a(x)$.

</details>


### [100] [Hölder regularity of solutions to parabolic equations associated with fractional $p$-Laplacian type operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13539)
*Takashi Kumagai,Jian Wang,Meng-ge Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes Hölder regularity for local weak super-solutions of parabolic equations driven by fractional p-Laplacian type operators with general (time-dependent) kernels, extending prior work from the linear case p=2 to the nonlinear case p∈(1,∞).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The fundamental work of Kassmann and Weidner treated Hölder regularity for the linear fractional case (p=2). This paper aims to extend that theory to the nonlinear regime p≠2, which requires a new nonlocal parabolic tail condition adapted to general p.

Method: The proof relies on the method of expansion of positivity, which serves as the key ingredient in a reduction-of-oscillation argument. An explicit nonlocal parabolic tail condition is introduced, expressed via an L^{1+ε}-norm in the time variable for a suitable ε>0, valid for all p∈(1,∞).

Result: Under the proposed nonlocal parabolic tail condition, Hölder regularity holds for local weak super-solutions of the fractional p-Laplacian type parabolic equation, for all p∈(1,∞) and kernels comparable to |x-y|^{-d-sp} with s∈(0,1).

Conclusion: The paper successfully extends the linear Hölder regularity theory of Kassmann and Weidner to nonlinear fractional p-Laplacian parabolic operators via a nonlocal tail condition and the expansion-of-positivity method.

Abstract: In this paper, we study Hölder regularity of local weak super-solutions to the parabolic equation associated with fractional $p$-Laplacian type operators $$ \partial_t u(t,x)-\text{p.v.}\int_{\R^d}|u(t,y)-u(t,x)|^{p-2}(u(t,y)-u(t,x))J(t;x,y)\,dy=0,\quad (t,x)\in \R\times \R^d, $$ where $\text{p.v.}$ denotes the principal value, $p\in(1,\infty)$ and the kernel $J(t;x,y)$ is comparable (uniformly with respect to
  $(t;x,y)\in\R\times\R^d\times\R^d$) to the fractional $p$-Laplacian kernel $|x-y|^{-d-sp}$ with $s\in(0,1)$. Our contribution extends the fundamental work of Kassmann and Weidner to the nonlinear case $p\ne 2$. In particular, we provide an explicit nonlocal parabolic tail condition in terms of an $L^{1+\varepsilon}$-norm in the time variable (for a suitable $\varepsilon>0$) for all $p\in(1,\infty)$, under which Hölder regularity holds. Our proof is based on the method of expansion of positivity, which is the key ingredient in the reduction-of-oscillation argument.

</details>


### [101] [Identifying secondary critical mass levels in two- and higher-dimensional Keller-Segel systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13559)
*Yulan Wang,Michael Winkler*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper identifies a secondary critical mass m*(n,R) for the parabolic-elliptic Keller-Segel system in a ball, separating local asymptotic stability of the constant equilibrium (m<m*) from finite-time blow-up (m>m*), with m* expressed via zeros of Bessel functions of the first kind.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Known Keller-Segel theory gives mass-dependent blow-up dichotomies, but a genuinely secondary critical mass phenomenon—especially in the 2D version—had no precedent. The authors aim to characterize such a critical mass for the parabolic-elliptic system in a ball.

Method: Analysis of the parabolic-elliptic Keller-Segel system under homogeneous Neumann boundary conditions on a ball B_R in R^n. The critical mass is derived by relating it to the principal zeros of appropriate Bessel functions of the first kind; stability is studied in L^∞, and blow-up is shown for radial nonconstant initial data satisfying a monotonicity condition.

Result: A critical mass m*(n,R)>0 is identified: for 0<m<m*, the constant equilibrium u≡v≡m/|B_R| is locally asymptotically stable in L^∞; for m>m*, any solution from suitable nonnegative nonconstant radial continuous initial data blows up in finite time. m* is essentially explicit via Bessel zeros; e.g., m*(2,R)≈14.68197·π for all R>0.

Conclusion: The findings reveal a genuinely secondary critical mass phenomenon for the Keller-Segel system, complementing known mass-dependent blow-up dichotomies, and notably provide a new result in the two-dimensional case.

Abstract: The parabolic-elliptic Keller-Segel system \[
  \left\{ \begin{array}{l}
  u_t = Δu - \nabla \cdot (u\nabla v), \\[1mm]
  0 = Δv - μ+ u,
  \qquad μ:=\frac{1}{|Ω|} \int_Ωu,
  \end{array} \right.
  \qquad \qquad (\star) \] is considered under homogeneous Neumann boundary conditions in the ball $Ω=B_R(0)\subset {\mathbb R}^n$, where $n\ge 2$ and $R>0$.
  The main results identify a mass level $m_\star(n,R)>0$ as critical in the sense that
  $\bullet$ whenever $0<m<m_\star(n,R)$, the constant equilibrium of ($\star$) given by $u\equiv v\equiv \frac{m}{|B_R|}$ is locally asymptotically stable with respect to the norm in $L^\infty(B_R)$,
  but that
  $\bullet$ if $m>m_\star(n,R)$, then any solution of ($\star$) emanating from nonnegative and nonconstant continuous radial initial data $u_0$ with $\int_{B_R} u_0=m$ and $\frac{1}{|B_r|} \int_{B_r} u_0 \ge \frac{m}{|B_R|}$ for all $r\in (0,R)$ blows up in finite time.
  The value of $m_\star(n,R)$ can be determined in an essentially explicit manner in dependence on principal zeroes of appropriate Bessel functions of the first kind; in particular, $m_\star(2,R)\approx 14.68197 \cdot π$ for all $R>0$. Accordingly, as a supplement to known mass-dependent blow-up dichotomies these findings reveal a genuinely secondary critical mass phenomenon for ($\star$) especially also in its two-dimensional version in which no precedent of this form seems known.

</details>


### [102] [Boundary stabilization of multi-dimensional first-order quasilinear hyperbolic systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13577)
*Haitian Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Global existence and exponential H^s decay are proved for multi-dimensional quasilinear hyperbolic systems with nonlinear feedback boundary conditions, given Lyapunov inequalities and small data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding long-time behavior of quasilinear hyperbolic systems with nonlinear boundary feedback is important for stability analysis, but few results cover the multi-dimensional case with both interior and boundary dissipation.

Method: Assumes strict interior and boundary Lyapunov inequalities for the linearized system and non-characteristic boundaries. Proves global existence for small data and exponential H^s decay (s>d/2+1 integer), on bounded smooth domains and flat periodic cylinders.

Result: Global existence for small initial data and exponential H^s decay established. Results illustrated with physical examples and numerical simulations.

Conclusion: Provides a general framework for exponential stability of multi-dimensional quasilinear hyperbolic systems with nonlinear feedback boundary conditions.

Abstract: We study the long-time behaviour of multi-dimensional first-order symmetrizable quasilinear hyperbolic systems with nonlinear feedback boundary conditions. Assuming strict interior and boundary Lyapunov inequalities for the linearized system and non-characteristic boundaries, we prove global existence for small initial data and exponential H^s decay, with s>d/2+1 an integer. We consider both bounded smooth domains and flat periodic cylinders, and illustrate the results with physical examples and numerical simulations.

</details>


### [103] [The conformal Poisson embedding method for the anisotropic Calderón problem with a potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13592)
*Cătălin I. Cârstea,Tony Liimatainen,Yi-Hsuan Lin,William Trad*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The local Dirichlet-to-Neumann map of Δ_g+q determines a compact connected real-analytic Riemannian manifold (dim ≥ 3) and a real-analytic potential up to a boundary-fixing diffeomorphism and conformal gauge, even for the constant-potential Helmholtz case, using measurements on any nonempty open boundary set.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establishes boundary determination (up to conformal gauge) for the Schrödinger/Helmholtz equation from partial boundary data, resolving a case new even for the fixed-frequency Helmholtz equation with constant potential.

Method: Develops a conformal Poisson embedding method and employs coordinates defined by quotients of solutions.

Result: The local Dirichlet-to-Neumann map recovers the real-analytic Riemannian manifold and real-analytic potential up to a diffeomorphism fixing the observed boundary and the conformal gauge of the equation.

Conclusion: The local Dirichlet-to-Neumann map uniquely determines (up to gauge) real-analytic manifolds and potentials of dimension ≥ 3, with measurements restricted to any nonempty open boundary set.

Abstract: We prove that the local Dirichlet-to-Neumann map of $Δ_g+q$ determines a compact connected real-analytic Riemannian manifold of dimension at least three and a real-analytic real-valued potential, up to a diffeomorphism fixing the observed boundary and the conformal gauge of the equation. The result is new even when \(q\) is constant, i.e. for the fixed-frequency (Helmholtz) equation \(Δ_g u+λu=0\). The measurements are restricted to an arbitrary nonempty open boundary set $Γ$. The proof develops conformal Poisson embeddings method and uses coordinates given by quotients of solutions.

</details>


### [104] [Global Existence and Partial Regularity for the Three-Dimensional Chemotaxis--Navier--Stokes System in a Half-Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13613)
*Yubo Chen,Jianbo Yu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Establishes endpoint partial regularity and global well-posedness for 3D chemotaxis--Navier--Stokes in a half-space, showing the singular set has vanishing 1D parabolic Hausdorff measure and proving decay with Gaussian asymptotics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the regularity and long-time behavior of suitable weak solutions to the 3D chemotaxis--Navier--Stokes system with constant sensitivity and linear consumption under no-slip/no-flux boundary conditions in a half-space, extending partial regularity to the natural endpoint scaling.

Method: A Caffarelli--Kohn--Nirenberg type criterion at the natural r^{-1} dissipation scaling, valid at interior and boundary points without pressure smallness. Key estimate combines weighted mass control with the localized entropy--energy inequality, avoiding a separate n log n quantity and handling the rescaling mass term. Boundary-compatible pressure and caloric decompositions yield local Hölder regularity. Global suitable weak solutions are constructed via a lower-order criterion requiring only smallness of velocity, signal gradient, and pressure oscillation.

Result: The full space--time singular set (including boundary part) has vanishing one-dimensional parabolic Hausdorff measure. Global suitable weak solutions exist under natural entropy/energy assumptions with no smallness on initial data. With time-independent potential of bounded gradient (and Hölder-continuous gradient), a solution becomes bounded and classical after finite waiting time. Decay and Neumann Gaussian asymptotics are obtained for both scalar components without initial spatial moment assumptions; profile coefficients are the conserved bacterial mass and limiting signal mass (positive if initial signal is nonzero).

Conclusion: The paper provides endpoint partial regularity and global existence results for the chemotaxis--Navier--Stokes system in a half-space, with sharp characterization of the singular set and long-time Gaussian asymptotics.

Abstract: We establish endpoint partial regularity for suitable weak solutions to the three-dimensional chemotaxis--Navier--Stokes system with constant sensitivity and linear consumption in a half-space under no-slip and no-flux boundary conditions. The full space--time singular set, including its boundary part, has vanishing one-dimensional parabolic Hausdorff measure. This follows from a Caffarelli--Kohn--Nirenberg type criterion at the natural $r^{-1}$ dissipation scaling, valid at interior and boundary points without additional pressure smallness.
  The key estimate combines weighted mass control with the localized entropy--energy inequality to close the iteration without a separate $n\log n$ quantity and accounts for the mass term generated by rescaling. Boundary-compatible pressure and caloric decompositions yield local Hölder regularity. We also prove a lower-order criterion requiring smallness only of the velocity, signal gradient, and pressure oscillation, with a threshold depending on an arbitrary finite density mass bound.
  We construct global suitable weak solutions under natural entropy and energy assumptions without smallness restrictions on the initial data. The potential is time-independent with bounded gradient; boundedness of the potential itself is assumed only for the large-time results. If its gradient is also Hölder continuous, a solution can be selected to become bounded and classical throughout the closed half-space after a finite waiting time. For the same solution, we obtain decay and Neumann Gaussian asymptotics for both scalar components without initial spatial moment assumptions. The profile coefficients are the conserved bacterial mass and the limiting signal mass, respectively, and the latter is strictly positive whenever the initial signal is nonzero.

</details>


### [105] [Global Existence of Strong and Weak Solutions to the 2D Compressible Navier-Stokes-Poisson System in Bounded Domains with Large Data and Vacuum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13618)
*Yingzhi Du,Huijiang Zhao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves global existence of a unique strong solution and a global weak solution for the 2D compressible Navier-Stokes-Poisson system on a general simply connected bounded domain with Navier-slip boundary conditions and a possibly non-flat doping profile, for large data allowing vacuum, under shear viscosity constant and bulk viscosity ~ ρ^γ with γ > 4/3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The core difficulty is the interaction between the Poisson force and general boundary geometry, which complicates the derivation of uniform-in-time density bounds and higher-order a priori estimates.

Method: It introduces a modified material derivative, derives quantitative L^p density estimates via a Neumann duality argument with a harmonic correction from the Navier-slip condition, and obtains a pointwise representation of the effective viscous flux by pulling back the Neumann Green function on the unit disk through a conformal mapping, combined with elliptic estimates for the Poisson equation and a charge-preserving approximation for the weak solution.

Result: Global existence of a unique strong solution and a global weak solution is established for arbitrarily large initial data allowing vacuum.

Conclusion: The commutator and elliptic estimates yield a uniform-in-time upper density bound, closing the higher-order a priori estimates and enabling construction of the global weak solution.

Abstract: We study the two-dimensional compressible Navier-Stokes-Poisson system in a general simply connected bounded domain with Navier-slip boundary conditions and a positive, possibly non-flat, doping profile. We establish the global existence of a unique strong solution and of a global weak solution for arbitrarily large initial data allowing vacuum when the shear viscosity is a positive constant and the bulk one proportional to a power of the density with the power bigger than 4/3.
  The main difficulties arise from the interaction between the Poisson force and the general boundary geometry. To overcome them, we introduce a modified material derivative and derive quantitative $L^p$ estimates for the density by means of a Neumann duality argument together with a harmonic correction generated by the Navier-slip boundary condition. We further obtain a pointwise representation of the effective viscous flux by pulling back the Neumann Green function on the unit disk through a conformal mapping. The resulting commutator estimates, combined with elliptic estimates for the Poisson equation, yield a uniform-in-time upper bound for the density and allow us to close the higher-order a priori estimates. Finally, the global weak solution is constructed through a charge-preserving approximation and compactness argument.

</details>


### [106] [Travel time tomography for elliptically anisotropic elastic planar media](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13747)
*Joonas Ilmavirta,Teemu Saksala,Hjørdis Schlüter*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies travel time tomography for elliptically anisotropic planar media, showing unique determination (up to boundary fixing diffeomorphisms) when at least one isotropic point exists, and exactly two solutions otherwise.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the gauge/inverse problem for travel time tomography in elliptically anisotropic planar media, characterizing when the anisotropic stiffness tensor is uniquely determined by lens data.

Method: Uses an interplay of algebra and geometry, with a characterization of elliptic anisotropy via several polynomial conditions, leveraging two Riemannian metrics (ellipse for shear waves, conformally Euclidean).

Result: If both metrics are lens rigid, each elliptically anisotropic stiffness tensor is uniquely determined by lens data (up to boundary fixing diffeomorphisms) when at least one isotropic point exists; if none exists, there are exactly two such tensors with the same lens data. The set of well-behaved diffeomorphisms is never a subgroup of all boundary fixing diffeomorphisms.

Conclusion: The paper establishes uniqueness/non-uniqueness results for travel time tomography in elliptically anisotropic media and shows the well-behaved diffeomorphisms do not form a subgroup.

Abstract: We study the gauge of the travel time tomography problem for elliptically anisotropic planar media. Under this assumption the respective slowness surfaces consist of an inner ellipse and an outer circle. Hence, the singularities of seismic waves propagate on geodesics given by two Riemannian metrics of which the one related to shear waves is conformally Euclidean. We show that if both of these metrics are lens rigid then, up to boundary fixing diffeomorphisms, each elliptically anisotropic stiffness tensor is uniquely determined by the lens data whenever it has at least one isotropic point. If none of those points exists, then there are exactly two elliptically anisotropic stiffness tensors with the same lens data. Clearly, not all boundary fixing diffeomorphisms preserve the imposed structure of the slowness surface. We show that the set of well-behaved diffeomorphisms is never a subgroup of all boundary fixing diffeomorphisms. Our proofs are based on an interplay of algebra and geometry, and the key of the proofs is a characterization of the elliptic anisotropy via several polynomial conditions.

</details>


### [107] [Asymmetric minimal mass blow-up for the quintic NLS on a star graph](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13752)
*Mattia Buratti,François Genoud,Vladimir Georgiev*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper constructs a non-radial minimal mass blow-up solution for the focusing quintic NLS on a two-edge star graph with a delta interaction, with the singularity formed at a prescribed interior point of one edge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The focusing quintic NLS on a star graph with delta interaction is studied to understand finite-time blow-up behavior, addressing the lack of non-radial minimal mass blow-up constructions and profile classification in this setting.

Method: The approach uses a suitable cut-off of the standard pseudoconformal blow-up solution of the focusing quintic NLS on the line, inspired by Ogawa-Tsutsumi and Burq-Gérard-Tzvetkov, with persistence of the Hamiltonian domain via Kato's persistence of regularity method.

Result: A non-radial minimal mass finite-time blow-up solution is constructed with singularity at an arbitrary prescribed interior point of one edge, and a two-parameter family of distinct blow-up profiles is identified.

Conclusion: The construction is generic, extends to star graphs with any number of edges, adapts to other metric graphs, and the identified profiles should play a central role in classifying non-radial minimal mass blow-up solutions on star graphs.

Abstract: This work is concerned with the focusing quintic nonlinear Schrödinger equation on a star graph with two edges, with a delta interaction of arbitrary strength at the vertex. We construct a non-radial, minimal mass, finite time blow-up solution, whose singularity forms at an arbitrarily prescribed interior point of one edge. Our approach is based on a suitable cut-off of the standard pseudoconformal blow-up solution of the focusing quintic NLS on the line, inspired by Ogawa-Tsutsumi and Burq-Gérard-Tzvetkov. A noteworthy feature of our construction is the persistence of the domain of the Hamiltonian under the flow, obtained by the persistence of regularity method of Kato. We also identify a two-parameter family of distinct blow-up profiles, which should play a central role in the classification of non-radial minimal mass blow-up solutions on the star graph. Our construction is generic and can be extended to a star graph with an arbitrary number of edges. It can certainly also be adapted to other types of metric graphs.

</details>


### [108] [Scattering for the KP-II equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13753)
*Xi Chen*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves $L^2(\mathbb{R}^2)$ scattering for real-valued KP-II equation solutions with localized initial data, covering all Schwartz functions and various weighted/tensor Besov spaces, without smallness or zero-mean assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establish scattering for KP-II under general (non-perturbative, non-small) real-valued initial data, a longstanding open problem, extending beyond prior results that required smallness or zero horizontal mean conditions.

Method: Uses the scalar $\bar\partial$ equation associated with the heat operator; employs a uniform inverse estimate and an exact $L^2$ identity for inverse construction at large amplitudes; estimates the nonlinear remainder on propagation cones of free solutions; identifies limiting spectral mass via the heat equation.

Result: Proves scattering in $L^2(\mathbb{R}^2)$ for real-valued KP-II solutions with localized data, covering all Schwartz functions, data with a half spatial moment in $B^2_{1,1}$, and certain weighted tensor Besov spaces allowing low regularity in either coordinate.

Conclusion: This is the first result establishing scattering for the KP-II equation for every real-valued Schwartz initial datum, requiring no smallness or zero horizontal mean condition.

Abstract: We prove scattering in $L^2(\mathbb{R}^2)$ for real-valued solutions of the KP-II equation with localized initial data. The hypotheses cover all Schwartz functions, data with a half spatial moment in $B^2_{1,1}$, and a class of weighted tensor Besov spaces allowing low regularity in either coordinate. The proof uses the scalar $\bar\partial$ equation associated with the heat operator. A uniform inverse estimate and an exact $L^2$ identity give an inverse construction for large amplitudes. We then estimate the nonlinear remainder on the propagation cones of free solutions and identify the limiting spectral mass through the heat equation. No smallness or zero horizontal mean condition is required. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first result establishing scattering for the KP-II equation for every real-valued Schwartz initial datum.

</details>


### [109] [Existence and uniqueness of bound states with prescribed number of nodes for nonlinear dispersive equations on spherically symmetric manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13813)
*Valentino Badalucco*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves existence of radial bound states with a prescribed number of nodal surfaces for the elliptic ODE Δu + f(u) = 0 on spherically symmetric manifolds under mild assumptions, and uniqueness for the model nonlinearity f(u) = |u|^{p-1}u − u. Results also cover manifolds with a singularity at the origin.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standing waves of nonlinear dispersive equations (nonlinear Schrödinger, Klein–Gordon) reduce to nonlinear elliptic ODEs Δu + f(u) = 0. Understanding existence and uniqueness of their radial bound states, particularly with prescribed nodal structure, is a fundamental problem in nonlinear analysis and PDE theory.

Method: Existence of radial bound states with a prescribed number of nodal surfaces is established for a large class of spherically symmetric manifolds and nonlinearities f, using ODE/PDE analysis. Uniqueness is shown for the model nonlinearity f(u) = |u|^{p-1}u − u under mild assumptions. The approach extends to manifolds with a singularity at the origin.

Result: Radial bound states with a prescribed number of nodal surfaces are shown to exist on a large class of spherically symmetric manifolds and for general nonlinearities f. For the model nonlinearity f(u) = |u|^{p-1}u − u, uniqueness of such bound states is proven. Both results apply to some manifolds with a singularity at the origin.

Conclusion: The paper provides a general framework for existence and uniqueness of nodal radial bound states of Δu + f(u) = 0, covering broad classes of symmetric manifolds and nonlinearities, including singular manifolds, with implications for standing waves of nonlinear dispersive equations.

Abstract: Standing waves $e^{iωt}u(x)$ of nonlinear dispersive equations, such as the nonlinear Schrödinger and Klein--Gordon equations, have a profile $u$ that solves a nonlinear elliptic ODE of the form $Δu+f(u)=0$. We prove the existence of radial bound states with a prescribed number of nodal surfaces for this equation, on a large class of spherically symmetric manifolds and nonlinearities $f$. Under mild assumptions, we prove uniqueness of such bound states for the model nonlinearity $f(u)=|u|^{p-1}u-u$. Both existence and uniqueness results apply to some manifolds with a singularity at the origin.

</details>


### [110] [Existence and large-field asymptotics of Hopfions in a continuum model of frustrated magnets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13883)
*Glal Bacho,Christof Melcher*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves existence of energy minimizers for a continuum frustrated magnetism model in infinitely many nontrivial Hopf homotopy classes, establishes a unit-charge Hopfion under strong external fields, and analyzes the large-field concentration limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Hopfions are particle-like field configurations with nontrivial Hopf index arising from frustrated magnetism, but a rigorous variational understanding of such configurations (existence of minimizers and their behavior) is lacking.

Method: A variational approach: analyze an energy functional combining competing second- and first-order exchange interactions with a stabilizing external magnetic field, for maps from three-dimensional space into the two-sphere.

Result: Energy minimizers exist in infinitely many nontrivial homotopy classes; a unit-charge Hopfion exists in the strong-field regime; in the large-field limit the natural concentration scale is identified and suitably rescaled minimizers converge strongly to a minimizer of a limiting variational problem.

Conclusion: The model rigorously supports Hopfion solutions and admits a well-defined large-field limiting variational problem with strong convergence of rescaled minimizers.

Abstract: We study a continuum model of frustrated magnetism that supports particle-like field configurations with nontrivial Hopf index. The energy functional combines competing second and first order exchange interactions with a stabilizing external magnetic field and gives rise to a variational problem for maps from three-dimensional space into the two-sphere. We prove the existence of energy minimizers in infinitely many nontrivial homotopy classes. We further establish the existence of a unit-charge Hopfion in the regime of strong external fields. Finally, we analyze the corresponding large-field limit, identify the natural concentration scale, and prove strong convergence of suitably rescaled minimizers to a minimizer of a limiting variational problem.

</details>


### [111] [Ground states and sharp logarithmic Sobolev inequalities for logarithmic Schrödinger equations with partial confinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13887)
*Baihong Li,Jarosław Mederski,Xiangjian Zeng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies logarithmic Schrödinger equations with a semidefinite quadratic potential and periodic potential, establishing an abstract variational principle for convex lower semicontinuous energies and deriving sharp results on ground states and logarithmic Sobolev inequalities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The energy is extended-valued and not C^1 on the natural Hilbert space, so turning a constrained minimizer into a critical point is variationally difficult.

Method: An abstract principle for convex lower semicontinuous energies with a smooth perturbation, permitting a scaling law for the full energy without homogeneity of the convex part; combined with profile decomposition under integer translations.

Result: A positive ground state exists for every rank of the quadratic form. When the periodic potential vanishes, a sharp anisotropic logarithmic Sobolev inequality is proved with optimal constant and all equality cases, and positive ground states are classified as explicit anisotropic Gaussons unique up to translations along the kernel. When the periodic component is constant, existence and classification of all normalized ground states at every prescribed positive mass are established, with explicit formulas for the minimum energy and Lagrange multiplier.

Conclusion: The abstract principle resolves the nonsmooth variational setting and yields sharp existence, classification, and equality results for logarithmic Schrödinger ground states under semidefinite quadratic and periodic potentials.

Abstract: We study the logarithmic Schrödinger equation with an arbitrary positive semidefinite quadratic potential and a periodic potential in the unconfined directions. The main variational difficulty is to turn a constrained minimizer into a critical point when the energy is extended-valued and not of class $C^1$ on the natural Hilbert space. We establish an abstract principle for convex lower semicontinuous energies with a smooth perturbation, allowing a scaling law for the full energy without requiring homogeneity of its convex part. Combined with a profile decomposition under integer translations, this principle yields a positive ground state for every rank of the quadratic form. When the periodic potential vanishes, we prove a sharp anisotropic logarithmic Sobolev inequality, determine its optimal constant and all equality cases, and classify positive ground states as explicit anisotropic Gaussons, unique up to translations along the kernel of the quadratic form. When the periodic component is constant, we also establish existence and classify all normalized ground states at every prescribed positive mass, with explicit formulas for the minimum energy and the associated Lagrange multiplier.

</details>


### [112] [The Inviscid Limit in Uniformly Local Yudovich Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13898)
*Zachary Radke*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves vanishing viscosity limit: 2D Navier-Stokes solutions converge to Euler solutions in $L^\infty([0,T];L^2_{ul})$ as $\nu\to0^+$, for slowly growing uniformly local Yudovich vorticity and $L^\infty$ velocity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the inviscid limit theory beyond classical Yudovich/Besov spaces, addressing settings with uniformly local vorticity and unbounded velocity, which are relevant for non-decaying data on $\mathbb{R}^2$.

Method: Work with velocity in $L^\infty(\mathbb{R}^2)$ and vorticity in the uniformly local Yudovich space $Y^\theta_{ul}(\mathbb{R}^2)$; establish convergence in $L^\infty([0,T];L^2_{ul})$ under a slow-growth condition on $\theta$, for common initial data.

Result: For every $T>0$, as $\nu\to0^+$, Navier-Stokes solutions converge to Euler solutions in $L^\infty([0,T];L^2_{ul}(\mathbb{R}^2))$, provided $\theta$ grows sufficiently slowly.

Conclusion: The inviscid limit holds in this uniformly local setting, provided the Yudovich exponent grows slowly enough.

Abstract: We consider solutions to the two-dimensional Navier-Stokes and Euler equations with the velocity in $L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^2)$ and the vorticity in the uniformly local Yudovich space $Y^θ_{ul}(\mathbb{R}^2)$. We show that if $θ$ grows sufficiently slowly, then for every $T>0$, solutions of the Navier-Stokes equation converge to solutions of the Euler equation in $L^{\infty}([0,T];L^2_{ul}(\mathbb{R}^2))$ as $ν\to 0^+$, provided they are generated from the same initial data.

</details>


### [113] [An Aleksandrov estimate for kinetic equations with rough coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13921)
*Amĺie Loher,Connor Mooney,Clément Mouhot,Lukas Niebel,Luis Silvestre,Yuzhe Zhu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: Exposition of an Astra-discovered proof of an Aleksandrov estimate for kinetic equations with rough coefficients, explained via simpler elliptic and parabolic analogues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Aleksandrov estimates are known for elliptic and parabolic PDEs, but extending them to kinetic equations with rough (non-smooth) coefficients is technically challenging. There is a need to make accessible the proof developed by Astra.

Method: Expository presentation of Astra's proof, illustrating the underlying technique first in the simpler elliptic and parabolic settings, then extending it to kinetic equations with rough coefficients.

Result: Provides a clear exposition of a proof establishing an Aleksandrov-type estimate for kinetic equations with rough coefficients, with pedagogical treatment of the elliptic and parabolic cases.

Conclusion: Astra's method yields an Aleksandrov estimate for kinetic equations with rough coefficients, and the approach is clarified by analogy with elliptic and parabolic cases.

Abstract: We give an exposition of a proof, discovered by Astra, of an Aleksandrov estimate for kinetic equations with rough coefficients. We explain this method in the simpler elliptic and parabolic settings as well.

</details>


### [114] [A Riesz-Map Preconditioned Extragradient Method for Price Formation in Mean Field Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13934)
*Yeva Gevorgyan,Diogo Gomes*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper develops and analyzes a Riesz-map-preconditioned projected extragradient method for a first-order price-formation mean field game in 1D, proving mesh-independent Lipschitz bounds and convergence on fixed grids, with first-order price/value error decay under refinement (density slower).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Solving a first-order price-formation MFG coupling HJB and transport equations via a global market-clearing constraint is difficult; the authors seek a stable algorithm whose step size is robust to mesh refinement and that avoids adding higher-order regularization to the MFG equations.

Method: A monotone weak formulation with mixed initial-terminal data; Riesz-map preconditioning using L2 norms for density/price and space-time H1 for value-function directions; finite propagation justifies truncating transport to a bounded interval. A density-only argument rules out cocoercivity, motivating extragradient updates. Discretization uses monotone upwind with adjoint-consistent transport and state-constraint boundary closure; exact metric projection onto bounded convex admissible sets.

Result: Mesh-independent Lipschitz bound on bounded convex admissible sets with uniform density, slope, and price bounds. For H=p^2/2, the iteration converges to a discrete variational inequality solution on each fixed grid with a step-size threshold independent of mesh sizes. On a quadratic benchmark, all runs reach tolerance with one fixed step size across a factor-of-16 reduction in both mesh sizes; price and value-function errors (away from low-density regions) decay at first order, density errors slower. Convergence under mesh refinement is not proved.

Conclusion: The projected extragradient method with Riesz-map preconditioning is a practical, mesh-robust solver for the localized first-order price-formation MFG, converging per fixed grid with mesh-independent step sizes and exhibiting near-first-order price/value accuracy under refinement.

Abstract: We develop and analyze a projected extragradient method, preconditioned by a Riesz map, for a first-order price-formation mean field game in one space dimension. The model couples Hamilton--Jacobi--Bellman and transport equations through a global market-clearing constraint. Finite propagation justifies truncating the first-order transport equation to a bounded interval. A monotone weak formulation incorporates the mixed initial--terminal data. Its Riesz map uses $L^2$ norms for density and price directions and a space-time $H^1$ norm for value-function directions, preconditioning the residual without adding higher-order regularization terms to the MFG equations. A density-only argument rules out cocoercivity on the localized admissible set in every equivalent Hilbert metric, motivating extragradient updates. For $H(x,p)=p^2/2$, we use a monotone upwind discretization with adjoint-consistent transport and a state-constraint boundary closure. We establish a mesh-independent Lipschitz bound on bounded convex admissible sets with uniform density, slope, and price bounds. With exact metric projection onto these sets, the iteration converges to a solution of the discrete variational inequality on each fixed grid, with an admissible step-size threshold independent of both mesh sizes. Convergence under mesh refinement is not proved. On a quadratic benchmark, all reported runs reach the stopping tolerance with one fixed step size across a factor-of-16 reduction in both mesh sizes. Price errors and value-function errors measured away from low-density regions decay approximately at first order under refinement, while density errors decrease more slowly.

</details>


<div id='physics.comp-ph'></div>
