### [139] [Constrained Probabilistic Numerics: Gaussian Process Solvers for Linear PDEs under Linear Inequality Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13804)
*Boyuan Deng,Kshitiz Upadhyay,Michael Shields*

Main category: stat.ML

TL;DR: This paper shows that Gaussian process PDE solvers can jointly condition on equality data (PDE residuals, boundaries, measurements) and inequality constraints (nonnegativity, monotonicity, no-arbitrage) via a sequential conditioning equivalence, yielding a computable non-Gaussian posterior through truncated-Gaussian moments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Gaussian process solvers for linear PDEs only condition on equality information, so they cannot enforce inequality constraints such as nonnegativity, monotonicity, or no-arbitrage that many physically or financially meaningful solutions must satisfy.

Method: The authors prove that conditioning a GP prior jointly on linear equality observations and a finite-dimensional inequality event equals sequential conditioning on Borel measures over the solution space. The physics-informed posterior thus serves as a valid prior for an inequality update at virtual points, with the combined non-Gaussian law computed through truncated-Gaussian moments without modifying either the PDE conditioning or the inequality update.

Result: On a Poisson benchmark, monotonicity cut 2D reconstruction error by 63% under incomplete boundary data. On a real S&P 500 option chain thinned to 20 quotes, no-arbitrage constraints reduced held-out extrapolation error by 22% and cut calendar-arbitrage rate from 10% to 1%. On four real air-quality snapshots, nonnegativity reduced a sub-zero-tail proxy by 74% at the sparsest monitor count; accuracy and diagnostic changes varied across data regimes.

Conclusion: The construction makes admissibility a conditioning input for probabilistic PDE solvers rather than a post-processing fix, and the probability of the noisy virtual constraint event under the equality posterior measures agreement with the bounds before updating.

Abstract: Gaussian process solvers for linear partial differential equations (PDEs) return posteriors rather than point estimates, but they condition only on equality information: the PDE residual, boundary data, and measurements. Many solutions of interest also obey inequality constraints, including nonnegativity, monotonicity, and absence of arbitrage, which equality conditioning cannot enforce. We prove that conditioning a Gaussian process prior jointly on linear equality observations and a finite-dimensional inequality event is equivalent to conditioning sequentially in terms of Borel measures on the solution space. The physics-informed posterior is therefore a valid prior for the inequality update at virtual points. The combined law is non-Gaussian yet computable through truncated-Gaussian moments, and neither the PDE conditioning nor the inequality update requires modification. We evaluated the construction on a Poisson benchmark, option-price surfaces, and air-quality fields. Under incomplete boundary data, a monotonicity constraint lowered the error of a two-dimensional Poisson reconstruction by $63\%$. On a real S&P 500 option chain thinned to $20$ retained quotes, no-arbitrage constraints reduced held-out extrapolation error by $22\%$ and cut the calendar-arbitrage rate from $10\%$ to $1\%$. On four real air-quality snapshots, nonnegativity reduced a Gaussian moment-based sub-zero-tail proxy by $74\%$ at the sparsest monitor count. Changes in mean accuracy and constraint diagnostics differed across data regimes. The probability of the noisy virtual constraint event under the equality posterior measures agreement with the specified bounds before the update. The construction turns admissibility from a post-processing step into conditioning information for probabilistic PDE solvers.

</details>


### [140] [Constrained Probabilistic Numerics: Gaussian Process Solvers for Linear PDEs under Linear Inequality Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13804)
*Boyuan Deng,Kshitiz Upadhyay,Michael Shields*

Main category: stat.ML

TL;DR: This paper shows that Gaussian process PDE solvers can incorporate inequality constraints (nonnegativity, monotonicity, no-arbitrage) by sequentially conditioning the physics-informed posterior at virtual points, yielding computable non-Gaussian posteriors that substantially improve accuracy and admissibility.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: GP solvers for linear PDEs return posteriors but condition only on equality information (PDE residual, boundary data, measurements). Many solutions must also obey inequality constraints like nonnegativity, monotonicity, and absence of arbitrage, which equality conditioning cannot enforce, making admissibility a mere post-processing concern.

Method: The authors prove that jointly conditioning a GP prior on linear equality observations and a finite-dimensional inequality event is equivalent to sequential conditioning (in Borel measures on the solution space). Thus the physics-informed posterior serves as a valid prior for an inequality update at virtual points. The combined law is non-Gaussian but computable via truncated-Gaussian moments, requiring no modification to either the PDE conditioning or the inequality update.

Result: Evaluated on a Poisson benchmark, option-price surfaces, and air-quality fields: under incomplete boundary data, monotonicity cut 2D Poisson reconstruction error by 63%; on a real S&P 500 option chain thinned to 20 quotes, no-arbitrage constraints reduced held-out extrapolation error by 22% and cut calendar-arbitrage rate from 10% to 1%; on four real air-quality snapshots, nonnegativity reduced a sub-zero-tail proxy by 74% at the sparsest monitor count. Accuracy and constraint diagnostics varied across data regimes.

Conclusion: The construction turns admissibility from a post-processing step into conditioning information for probabilistic PDE solvers. The probability of the noisy virtual constraint event under the equality posterior measures agreement with specified bounds before the update.

Abstract: Gaussian process solvers for linear partial differential equations (PDEs) return posteriors rather than point estimates, but they condition only on equality information: the PDE residual, boundary data, and measurements. Many solutions of interest also obey inequality constraints, including nonnegativity, monotonicity, and absence of arbitrage, which equality conditioning cannot enforce. We prove that conditioning a Gaussian process prior jointly on linear equality observations and a finite-dimensional inequality event is equivalent to conditioning sequentially in terms of Borel measures on the solution space. The physics-informed posterior is therefore a valid prior for the inequality update at virtual points. The combined law is non-Gaussian yet computable through truncated-Gaussian moments, and neither the PDE conditioning nor the inequality update requires modification. We evaluated the construction on a Poisson benchmark, option-price surfaces, and air-quality fields. Under incomplete boundary data, a monotonicity constraint lowered the error of a two-dimensional Poisson reconstruction by $63\%$. On a real S&P 500 option chain thinned to $20$ retained quotes, no-arbitrage constraints reduced held-out extrapolation error by $22\%$ and cut the calendar-arbitrage rate from $10\%$ to $1\%$. On four real air-quality snapshots, nonnegativity reduced a Gaussian moment-based sub-zero-tail proxy by $74\%$ at the sparsest monitor count. Changes in mean accuracy and constraint diagnostics differed across data regimes. The probability of the noisy virtual constraint event under the equality posterior measures agreement with the specified bounds before the update. The construction turns admissibility from a post-processing step into conditioning information for probabilistic PDE solvers.

</details>

### [141] [Non-resonant angles in a Robin eigenvalue problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13601)
*Konstantin Pankrashkin*

Main category: math.SP

TL;DR: This work determines the full range of non-resonant opening angles for Laplacian eigenvalues with strongly attractive Robin boundary conditions in polygons, confirming a conjecture by Dauge and Lafranche.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: An earlier study showed that in polygons, if every corner is concave or non-resonant, the asymptotic behavior of individual Laplacian eigenvalues (with strongly attractive Robin boundary conditions) is governed by an effective 1D boundary operator with Dirichlet conditions at corners. However, the non-resonance condition was only verified for right and obtuse angles, leaving the full angular range unknown.

Method: The authors analyze the non-resonance condition (an eigenvalue bound on truncated sectors) to determine exactly which opening angles satisfy it.

Result: The non-resonance condition fails only for opening angles π/(2n+1) with n=1,2,3,… These critical values are equivalently characterized by jumps in the eigenvalue counting function and by the existence of bounded generalized eigenfunctions.

Conclusion: The full range of non-resonant angles is determined, resolving the numerically supported conjecture of Dauge and Lafranche.

Abstract: In an earlier study of the Laplacian eigenvalues with strongly attractive Robin boundary conditions in polygons it was found that if each corner is either concave or non-resonant, then the asymptotic behavior of the individual eigenvalues is determined by an effective one-dimensional operator on the boundary with Dirichlet boundary conditions at the corners. The non-resonance condition was formulated in terms of an eigenvalue bound on truncated sectors, and it was only shown to be satisfied by the right and obtuse opening angles. In the present work, we determine the full range of non-resonant angles: it turns out that the non-resonance condition fails only for the opening angles $\fracπ{2n+1}$ with $n=1,2,3,\dots$, and these critical values are equivalently characterized in terms of the jumps of the eigenvalue counting function and the existence of bounded generalized eigenfunctions. This resolves the numerically supported conjecture stated by Dauge and Lafranche.

</details>


### [142] [Sharp upper bounds for Steklov eigenvalues on warped products](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13738)
*Jade Brisson,Bruno Colbois,Alexandre Girouard,Katie Gittins,Jean Lagacé-Taylor*

Main category: math.SP

TL;DR: Sharp isoperimetric-type upper bounds are derived for Steklov eigenvalues on warped products with compact manifold bases and closed manifold fibres, using separation of variables and comparison with auxiliary Helmholtz-Steklov problems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Steklov eigenvalues encode boundary spectral geometry, and understanding their behavior on warped products connects the geometry of the base, fibre, and warping function to boundary spectra.

Method: After separation of variables, bounds are obtained by comparison with auxiliary Helmholtz-Steklov problems on the base involving weighted Laplace operators induced by the warping function. Spectral approximation methods are used, including homogenisation on manifolds for one-dimensional fibres.

Result: Sharp upper bounds involving the volume of the warped product and its boundary, fibre Laplace eigenvalues, and base Steklov eigenvalues. The effect of geometric constraints (e.g., warping function equal to one on the boundary) is also studied.

Conclusion: The work provides sharp isoperimetric-type Steklov eigenvalue bounds on warped products and clarifies how warping and geometric constraints influence these bounds.

Abstract: We obtain sharp isoperimetric-type upper bounds for Steklov eigenvalues on warped products whose bases are compact Riemannian manifolds with boundary and whose fibres are closed Riemannian manifolds. These bounds involve the volume of the warped product and of its boundary, as well as the eigenvalues of the Laplace operator on the fibre and the Steklov eigenvalues of the base. After separation of variables, these bounds are obtained by comparison with auxiliary Helmholtz-Steklov problems on the base involving weighted Laplace operators naturally induced by the warping function. We also study the effect of various natural geometric constraints, such as the warping function being identically equal to one on the boundary. The proofs make use of various spectral approximation methods, including homogenisation on manifolds when the fibres are one-dimensional.

</details>


### [143] [Non-resonant angles in a Robin eigenvalue problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13601)
*Konstantin Pankrashkin*

Main category: math.SP

TL;DR: This paper fully characterizes the range of non-resonant opening angles for Laplacian eigenvalues with strongly attractive Robin boundary conditions in polygons, resolving the Dauge–Lafranche conjecture.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: An earlier study showed that for polygons with strongly attractive Robin boundary conditions, the asymptotic behavior of individual Laplacian eigenvalues is governed by an effective 1D boundary operator with Dirichlet conditions at corners—provided each corner is concave or non-resonant. However, the non-resonance condition was only verified for right and obtuse angles, leaving the full angle range unknown.

Method: The authors analyze the non-resonance condition, originally formulated via an eigenvalue bound on truncated sectors, and determine exactly which opening angles satisfy it, also characterizing the critical angles through eigenvalue counting function jumps and bounded generalized eigenfunctions.

Result: The non-resonance condition fails only for opening angles of the form π/(2n+1) with n=1,2,3,…; these critical values admit equivalent characterizations via jumps of the eigenvalue counting function and existence of bounded generalized eigenfunctions.

Conclusion: This establishes the complete range of non-resonant angles and resolves the numerically supported conjecture of Dauge and Lafranche.

Abstract: In an earlier study of the Laplacian eigenvalues with strongly attractive Robin boundary conditions in polygons it was found that if each corner is either concave or non-resonant, then the asymptotic behavior of the individual eigenvalues is determined by an effective one-dimensional operator on the boundary with Dirichlet boundary conditions at the corners. The non-resonance condition was formulated in terms of an eigenvalue bound on truncated sectors, and it was only shown to be satisfied by the right and obtuse opening angles. In the present work, we determine the full range of non-resonant angles: it turns out that the non-resonance condition fails only for the opening angles $\fracπ{2n+1}$ with $n=1,2,3,\dots$, and these critical values are equivalently characterized in terms of the jumps of the eigenvalue counting function and the existence of bounded generalized eigenfunctions. This resolves the numerically supported conjecture stated by Dauge and Lafranche.

</details>


### [144] [Sharp upper bounds for Steklov eigenvalues on warped products](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13738)
*Jade Brisson,Bruno Colbois,Alexandre Girouard,Katie Gittins,Jean Lagacé-Taylor*

Main category: math.SP

TL;DR: Sharp isoperimetric-type upper bounds for Steklov eigenvalues on warped products, with bases being compact Riemannian manifolds with boundary and closed fibre manifolds, derived via separation of variables and comparison with Helmholtz-Steklov problems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Steklov eigenvalues on warped products are of geometric interest, but sharp isoperimetric-type upper bounds in this general setting were lacking. The paper aims to extend such bounds to warped products with boundary base and closed fibres.

Method: After separation of variables, bounds are obtained by comparison with auxiliary Helmholtz-Steklov problems on the base involving weighted Laplace operators induced by the warping function. Spectral approximation methods are used, including homogenisation on manifolds when fibres are one-dimensional.

Result: Sharp isoperimetric-type upper bounds involving the volume of the warped product and its boundary, eigenvalues of the Laplace operator on the fibre, and Steklov eigenvalues of the base. The effect of geometric constraints (e.g. warping function equal to one on the boundary) is also studied.

Conclusion: The paper provides sharp Steklov eigenvalue upper bounds for warped products via Helmholtz-Steklov comparison, clarifying the role of volume, fibre, base spectra, and geometric constraints on the warping function.

Abstract: We obtain sharp isoperimetric-type upper bounds for Steklov eigenvalues on warped products whose bases are compact Riemannian manifolds with boundary and whose fibres are closed Riemannian manifolds. These bounds involve the volume of the warped product and of its boundary, as well as the eigenvalues of the Laplace operator on the fibre and the Steklov eigenvalues of the base. After separation of variables, these bounds are obtained by comparison with auxiliary Helmholtz-Steklov problems on the base involving weighted Laplace operators naturally induced by the warping function. We also study the effect of various natural geometric constraints, such as the warping function being identically equal to one on the boundary. The proofs make use of various spectral approximation methods, including homogenisation on manifolds when the fibres are one-dimensional.

</details>

# eess.SY [[Back]](#toc)

### [145] [Symbolic-Permutation-Based Direct Sparse Jacobian Assembly for Accelerated Power-Flow Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12654)
*Tianshi Cheng,Venkata Dinavahi*

Main category: eess.SY

TL;DR: A symbolic-permutation method for direct sparse Jacobian assembly in Newton-Raphson power flow eliminates bus-type projection overhead, achieving 24-30x faster assembly than MATPOWER and 11.7-154.3x end-to-end speedups, implemented in RustPower.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Repeated assembly of the sparse Jacobian matrix is a primary computational bottleneck in Newton-Raphson power-flow analysis. Existing methods incur runtime bus-type projection overhead and non-fused differentiation/assembly steps.

Method: Algebraic equivariance analysis of the Jacobian, leading to a direct sparse Jacobian assembly method based on symbolic permutation. The permutation is pre-applied to the admittance matrix in a one-time symbolic phase, eliminating runtime bus-type projection. Differentiation and global assembly fuse into a single-pass, branchless algorithm streaming values into contiguous memory, realized in the open-source RustPower solver.

Result: Assembly is 24.4-30.1x faster than MATPOWER-style baseline and 6.0-7.8x faster than Schafer-Braun optimized assembly. End-to-end grid solve is 11.7x-154.3x faster than pandapower 3.5.4 with Numba, with unchanged numerical results and convergence. Comparable to C++ LightSim2Grid 1.0.0 on IEEE 39, 1.3-1.8x faster on IEEE 118 and PEGASE systems.

Conclusion: The method preserves numerical results and convergence while delivering major speedups, and extends directly to general problems where a sparse Jacobian inherits structure from a static network operator, including transient stability analysis, EMT simulation, and OPF Hessian assembly.

Abstract: The repeated assembly of the sparse Jacobian matrix constitutes a primary computational bottleneck in the Newton-Raphson power-flow analysis. Through an algebraic equivariance analysis of the Jacobian matrix, this paper proposes a direct sparse Jacobian assembly method based on symbolic permutation. By pre-applying the permutation to the admittance matrix during a one-time symbolic phase, the runtime bus-type projection overhead is entirely eliminated. Consequently, complex Jacobian differentiation and global matrix assembly fuse into a single operation, yielding a single-pass, branchless algorithm that streams values directly into contiguous memory. The framework is realized in the open-source RustPower solver. Benchmarks on IEEE test systems and PEGASE 9241 demonstrate that the proposed assembly is 24.4-30.1x faster than the MATPOWER- style baseline and 6.0-7.8x faster than the Schafer-Braun optimized assembly. End-to-end, the grid-level solve time is 11.7x-154.3x faster than pandapower 3.5.4 with Numba, while leaving numerical results and convergence behavior unchanged. Compared with the native C++ LightSim2Grid 1.0.0, grid-level performance is comparable on IEEE 39 and 1.3x-1.8x faster on IEEE 118 and the PEGASE systems. The method extends directly to general problems where a sparse Jacobian inherits its structure from a static network operator, including transient stability analysis, EMT simulation, and OPF Hessian assembly.

</details>


### [146] [Symbolic-Permutation-Based Direct Sparse Jacobian Assembly for Accelerated Power-Flow Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12654)
*Tianshi Cheng,Venkata Dinavahi*

Main category: eess.SY

TL;DR: This paper proposes a symbolic-permutation-based direct sparse Jacobian assembly method for Newton-Raphson power flow, eliminating runtime bus-type projection and fusing differentiation and assembly into a single branchless pass, implemented in RustPower.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Repeated assembly of the sparse Jacobian matrix is a primary computational bottleneck in Newton-Raphson power-flow analysis, and existing methods (MATPOWER-style, Schafer-Braun optimized) incur overhead from runtime bus-type projection and non-fused differentiation/assembly.

Method: An algebraic equivariance analysis of the Jacobian matrix leads to a direct sparse assembly via symbolic permutation: the permutation is pre-applied to the admittance matrix in a one-time symbolic phase, removing runtime bus-type projection overhead and fusing complex Jacobian differentiation with global matrix assembly into a single-pass, branchless algorithm streaming values into contiguous memory.

Result: On IEEE test systems and PEGASE 9241, assembly is 24.4-30.1x faster than the MATPOWER-style baseline and 6.0-7.8x faster than the Schafer-Braun optimized assembly. End-to-end grid-level solve is 11.7x-154.3x faster than pandapower 3.5.4 with Numba, with unchanged numerical results and convergence. Versus native C++ LightSim2Grid 1.0.0, performance is comparable on IEEE 39 and 1.3x-1.8x faster on IEEE 118 and PEGASE systems.

Conclusion: The method eliminates assembly bottlenecks while preserving numerical behavior, and extends to general problems where a sparse Jacobian inherits its structure from a static network operator, including transient stability analysis, EMT simulation, and OPF Hessian assembly.

Abstract: The repeated assembly of the sparse Jacobian matrix constitutes a primary computational bottleneck in the Newton-Raphson power-flow analysis. Through an algebraic equivariance analysis of the Jacobian matrix, this paper proposes a direct sparse Jacobian assembly method based on symbolic permutation. By pre-applying the permutation to the admittance matrix during a one-time symbolic phase, the runtime bus-type projection overhead is entirely eliminated. Consequently, complex Jacobian differentiation and global matrix assembly fuse into a single operation, yielding a single-pass, branchless algorithm that streams values directly into contiguous memory. The framework is realized in the open-source RustPower solver. Benchmarks on IEEE test systems and PEGASE 9241 demonstrate that the proposed assembly is 24.4-30.1x faster than the MATPOWER- style baseline and 6.0-7.8x faster than the Schafer-Braun optimized assembly. End-to-end, the grid-level solve time is 11.7x-154.3x faster than pandapower 3.5.4 with Numba, while leaving numerical results and convergence behavior unchanged. Compared with the native C++ LightSim2Grid 1.0.0, grid-level performance is comparable on IEEE 39 and 1.3x-1.8x faster on IEEE 118 and the PEGASE systems. The method extends directly to general problems where a sparse Jacobian inherits its structure from a static network operator, including transient stability analysis, EMT simulation, and OPF Hessian assembly.

</details>

### [147] [Resistive alignment of a black-hole magnetosphere](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13288)
*Ruben Lier,Oliver Porth*

Main category: astro-ph.HE

TL;DR: A resistive relativistic MHD theory explains why black-hole magnetospheres align their magnetic axis with the spin axis: alignment arises from second-order spin effects plus resistive dissipation, confirmed by simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Numerical simulations show black-hole magnetospheres rapidly align the magnetic axis with the spin axis, but the physical origin of this alignment was previously unclear.

Method: Develops an analytic theory within resistive relativistic magnetohydrodynamics. Using the slow-spin Blandford-Znajek monopole solution as the exterior field, the induction equation is reduced to a relaxation equation for the current-sheet inclination angle. Prediction tested with resistive-GRMHD simulations across several spins and resistivities.

Result: Alignment is shown to result from second-order spin effects in the Blandford-Znajek solution combined with explicit resistive dissipation. Simulations reveal a regime where the measured late-time secular decay matches the analytic prediction quantitatively.

Conclusion: The alignment process is analytically explained and quantitatively validated, providing a clear physical mechanism for magnetospheric axis alignment in black holes.

Abstract: Recent numerical simulations have shown that black-hole magnetospheres evolve rapidly toward alignment between the magnetic axis and spin axis but the physical origin of this process has remained unclear. We develop an analytic theory of this alignment within resistive relativistic magnetohydrodynamics. Taking the slow-spin Blandford-Znajek monopole solution as the exterior magnetic field, we reduce the induction equation to a relaxation equation for the current-sheet inclination angle. Our analysis shows that alignment results from second-order spin effects in the Blandford-Znajek solution combined with explicit resistive dissipation. We test this prediction using resistive-GRMHD simulations at several spins and resistivities and find a regime in which the measured late-time secular decay agrees quantitatively with the analytic prediction.

</details>


### [148] [Resistive alignment of a black-hole magnetosphere](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13288)
*Ruben Lier,Oliver Porth*

Main category: astro-ph.HE

TL;DR: The paper develops an analytic theory explaining why black-hole magnetospheres align their magnetic and spin axes, attributing it to second-order spin effects in the Blandford-Znajek solution plus resistive dissipation, and confirms it with resistive-GRMHD simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Numerical simulations show black-hole magnetospheres rapidly evolve toward alignment of the magnetic and spin axes, but the physical origin of this process was unclear.

Method: Within resistive relativistic MHD, using the slow-spin Blandford-Znajek monopole solution as the exterior field, the induction equation is reduced to a relaxation equation for the current-sheet inclination angle. Predictions are tested via resistive-GRMHD simulations across several spins and resistivities.

Result: Alignment arises from second-order spin effects in the Blandford-Znajek solution combined with explicit resistive dissipation. A regime is found where the measured late-time secular decay matches the analytic prediction quantitatively.

Conclusion: The magnetic-axis/spin-axis alignment in black-hole magnetospheres has a well-defined physical origin, confirmed analytically and numerically.

Abstract: Recent numerical simulations have shown that black-hole magnetospheres evolve rapidly toward alignment between the magnetic axis and spin axis but the physical origin of this process has remained unclear. We develop an analytic theory of this alignment within resistive relativistic magnetohydrodynamics. Taking the slow-spin Blandford-Znajek monopole solution as the exterior magnetic field, we reduce the induction equation to a relaxation equation for the current-sheet inclination angle. Our analysis shows that alignment results from second-order spin effects in the Blandford-Znajek solution combined with explicit resistive dissipation. We test this prediction using resistive-GRMHD simulations at several spins and resistivities and find a regime in which the measured late-time secular decay agrees quantitatively with the analytic prediction.

</details>

### [149] [The binormal flow: a toy model for turbulence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13457)
*Elena Danesi,Pablo Merino,Luis Vega*

Main category: physics.flu-dyn

TL;DR: Lecture notes summarizing Luis Vega's one-week course on the binormal flow model for 3D vortex filament dynamics in incompressible fluids under Euler equations, connecting binormal flow, the Schrödinger map, and the 1D cubic nonlinear Schrödinger equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To document and disseminate the content of a course linking the binormal flow to related geometric and dispersive PDE models, revealing rich physical and numerical phenomenology.

Method: Lecture-note format compiling the course material, written and completed by two attendees (Elena Danesi and Pablo Merino), covering connections among binormal flow, Schrödinger map, and 1D cubic NLS.

Result: A set of course notes presented at the 2nd Advanced Summer School at IESC, Cargèse, Corsica (June 20–30, 2023).

Conclusion: The notes provide a coherent account of the binormal flow model and its deep links to the Schrödinger map and 1D cubic NLS, offering a useful reference for the field.

Abstract: These notes summarize the content of a one-week course given by Luis Vega on the binormal flow model applied to describe the dynamics of three-dimensional vortex filaments in incompressible fluids following the Euler equations. This topic touches connections between the binormal flow, the Schrödinger map and the 1d cubic nonlinear Schrödinger equation, providing a rich casuistry linked to the physical and numerical phenomenology. This course was part of the $2^{\text{nd}}$ Advanced Summer School held at the Institut d'Etudes Scientifiques de Cargèse (IESC), at Cargèse (Corsica), from $20^{\text{th}}$ to $30^{\text{th}}$ June 2023. These notes have been written and completed by two of its attendees, Elena Danesi and Pablo Merino.

</details>


### [150] [The binormal flow: a toy model for turbulence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13457)
*Elena Danesi,Pablo Merino,Luis Vega*

Main category: physics.flu-dyn

TL;DR: Lecture notes from Luis Vega's one-week course on the binormal flow model for 3D vortex filament dynamics in incompressible Euler fluids, connecting binormal flow, Schrödinger maps, and the 1D cubic NLS equation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To document and disseminate a course that bridges the binormal flow, Schrödinger maps, and the 1D cubic nonlinear Schrödinger equation, offering a rich casuistry tied to physical and numerical phenomenology of 3D vortex filaments.

Method: Lecture-note format summarizing the course content and its connections between the binormal flow model and related PDE/dynamical systems frameworks (Schrödinger map, 1D cubic NLS).

Result: A set of written and completed notes produced by two attendees, Elena Danesi and Pablo Merino, covering the course delivered at the 2nd Advanced Summer School at IESC, Cargèse, 20–30 June 2023.

Conclusion: The notes provide a unified account of the binormal flow model and its links to Schrödinger maps and the 1D cubic NLS, serving as a reference for the physical and numerical phenomenology of 3D vortex filament dynamics.

Abstract: These notes summarize the content of a one-week course given by Luis Vega on the binormal flow model applied to describe the dynamics of three-dimensional vortex filaments in incompressible fluids following the Euler equations. This topic touches connections between the binormal flow, the Schrödinger map and the 1d cubic nonlinear Schrödinger equation, providing a rich casuistry linked to the physical and numerical phenomenology. This course was part of the $2^{\text{nd}}$ Advanced Summer School held at the Institut d'Etudes Scientifiques de Cargèse (IESC), at Cargèse (Corsica), from $20^{\text{th}}$ to $30^{\text{th}}$ June 2023. These notes have been written and completed by two of its attendees, Elena Danesi and Pablo Merino.

</details>

### [151] [Existence of ergodic invariant measures for 3D Navier-Stokes eqs with superlinear noise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13266)
*Zdzislaw Brzezniak,Benedetta Ferrario,Margherita Zanella*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: Extends a prior global well-posedness result for 3D Navier-Stokes equations with superlinear noise to include additive noise and a deterministic forcing term, and proves existence of invariant and ergodic measures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work (arXiv:2609.08284) established a unique global-in-time solution for 3D Navier-Stokes with superlinear noise for initial data in V. This paper aims to broaden that result by adding additive noise and deterministic forcing, and to study long-time statistical behavior.

Method: Adds additive noise and deterministic forcing to the superlinear-noise system; for time-independent forcing, applies the Krylov-Bogoliubov method relying on tightness and the Feller property in the space V to construct invariant measures.

Result: The unique global-in-time solution result holds with additive noise and deterministic forcing. For time-independent forcing, at least one invariant measure exists, and the set of invariant measures contains an ergodic invariant measure.

Conclusion: The theory of globally well-posed 3D Navier-Stokes with superlinear noise extends to additive noise and forcing, and supports the existence of invariant and ergodic measures, describing long-term statistics of the stochastic system.

Abstract: We consider the Navier-Stokes equations in a smooth bounded domain $D\subset \mathbb R^3$. It has been proved in arXiv:2609.08284 that these equations have a unique solution - globally defined in time - when a superlinear noise is introduced and the initial velocity belongs to the space $V$ of divergence-free $H^1_0(D;\mathbb R^3)$-vector fields. Here we show that the same result holds when there is also an additive noise and a deterministic forcing term. Moreover, when the deterministic forcing term is time-independent we prove the existence of at least one invariant measure for this stochastic equation. This is obtained by the Krylov-Bogoliubov method, based on tightness and Feller property in $V$. Finally, we show that the set of invariant measures contains one ergodic invariant measure.

</details>


### [152] [Statistical inference for rough Burgers-type stochastic partial differential equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13673)
*Simon Chony Acosta Christian Olivera Ciprian Tudor*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: The paper studies a Burgers-type stochastic PDE driven by space-time white noise on the 1D torus, working with rough-path mild solutions. It establishes a regularity separation between the stationary Gaussian part and the remainder, computes exact quadratic/quartic variations of the Gaussian part, and derives strongly consistent pathwise estimators of the diffusivity θ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The transport term g(u)∂_x u in the SPDE has no classical interpretation, so solutions must be defined via rough paths rather than Itô calculus. This motivates the need for an inference procedure for an SPDE whose solution is only a pathwise, measurable function of a rough path, which the authors claim does not previously exist.

Method: Solutions are decomposed as u = ψ + R, where ψ is a stationary centred Gaussian process solving a damped stochastic heat equation and R is a remainder. The authors show R is spatially Lipschitz and temporally Hölder of order > 1/2 away from the initial time, hence smoother than ψ. They compute the spatial quadratic variation and temporal quartic variation of ψ almost surely (not merely in L²), show u's variations are unaffected by R, and combine this with an exact time-scaling identity to build pathwise estimators of θ.

Result: The remainder R is strictly smoother than ψ in both space and time away from the initial time. The spatial quadratic and temporal quartic variations of ψ are computed exactly, almost surely, and coincide with those of u. Together with an exact time-scaling identity, this yields strongly consistent pathwise estimators of the diffusivity θ.

Conclusion: The paper provides what is, to the authors' knowledge, the first inference procedure for an SPDE whose solution is defined only pathwise, as a measurable function of a rough path, rather than as an Itô process. The key mechanism is a separation of regularity scales between the Gaussian part and the remainder.

Abstract: We consider the Burgers-type stochastic PDE \[
  du = θ\,\partial_x^2 u\,dt + f(u)\,dt + g(u)\,\partial_x u\,dt + σ\,dW(t), \] driven by space--time white noise on the one-dimensional torus, where \(θ>0\) is the diffusivity parameter and \(σ>0\) the noise intensity. The transport term \(g(u)\,\partial_x u\) admits no classical interpretation, and we work with the mild solutions defined via rough paths following~\cite{Hairer2011}, for which the solution decomposes as \(u=ψ+R\) with \(ψ\) a stationary centred Gaussian process solving a damped stochastic heat equation and \(R\) a remainder.
  We first show that, away from the initial time, \(R\) is Lipschitz in space and Hölder in time of order strictly greater than \(1/2\), hence strictly smoother than \(ψ\) in both variables. We then compute exactly the spatial quadratic variation and the temporal quartic variation of \(ψ\), almost surely and not merely in \(L^{2}(Ω)\), and prove that the corresponding variations of \(u\) are unaffected by the remainder. Combined with an exact time-scaling identity, this separation of regularity scales yields strongly consistent pathwise estimators of \(θ\). To our knowledge this is the first inference procedure for an SPDE whose solution is defined only pathwise, as a measurable function of a rough path, rather than as an Itô process.

</details>


### [153] [Existence of ergodic invariant measures for 3D Navier-Stokes eqs with superlinear noise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13266)
*Zdzislaw Brzezniak,Benedetta Ferrario,Margherita Zanella*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: Extends a prior global well-posedness result for 3D Navier-Stokes with superlinear noise to include additive noise and deterministic forcing, and proves existence of an invariant measure (including an ergodic one) via Krylov-Bogoliubov.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Build on arXiv:2609.08284, which proved global-in-time unique solvability of 3D Navier-Stokes with superlinear noise for initial data in V, by asking whether the result survives additional additive noise and a deterministic forcing term, and whether an invariant measure exists.

Method: Analysis of the stochastic Navier-Stokes equations in a smooth bounded domain in R^3; proving global well-posedness in V with multiplicative superlinear noise plus additive noise and deterministic forcing; then, for time-independent forcing, applying the Krylov-Bogoliubov method based on tightness and the Feller property in V.

Result: Global-in-time unique solvability holds under the combined superlinear + additive noise and deterministic forcing. For time-independent forcing, at least one invariant measure exists, and the set of invariant measures contains an ergodic one.

Conclusion: The earlier well-posedness result is robust to additive noise and deterministic forcing, and the resulting stochastic system admits invariant and ergodic invariant measures.

Abstract: We consider the Navier-Stokes equations in a smooth bounded domain $D\subset \mathbb R^3$. It has been proved in arXiv:2609.08284 that these equations have a unique solution - globally defined in time - when a superlinear noise is introduced and the initial velocity belongs to the space $V$ of divergence-free $H^1_0(D;\mathbb R^3)$-vector fields. Here we show that the same result holds when there is also an additive noise and a deterministic forcing term. Moreover, when the deterministic forcing term is time-independent we prove the existence of at least one invariant measure for this stochastic equation. This is obtained by the Krylov-Bogoliubov method, based on tightness and Feller property in $V$. Finally, we show that the set of invariant measures contains one ergodic invariant measure.

</details>


### [154] [Statistical inference for rough Burgers-type stochastic partial differential equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13673)
*Simon Chony Acosta Christian Olivera Ciprian Tudor*

Main category: math.PR

TL;DR: This paper builds pathwise estimators of the diffusivity parameter θ for a Burgers-type SPDE driven by space-time white noise, using rough-path mild solutions and regularity-separation between the Gaussian component and the remainder.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The transport term g(u)∂_x u has no classical (Itô) interpretation, so the solution is only defined pathwise via rough paths. No inference procedure previously existed for an SPDE defined as a measurable function of a rough path rather than an Itô process.

Method: Uses rough-path mild solutions (Hairer 2011), decomposing u = ψ + R. Shows R is spatially Lipschitz and temporally Hölder of order >1/2 (smoother than ψ). Computes exact spatial quadratic variation and temporal quartic variation of ψ almost surely, proves u's variations are unaffected by R, and combines with an exact time-scaling identity to construct strongly consistent pathwise estimators of θ.

Result: R is strictly smoother than ψ in both space and time. Spatial quadratic and temporal quartic variations of ψ are computed exactly, almost surely (not just in L²(Ω)). The corresponding variations of u match those of ψ, and together with the time-scaling identity this yields strongly consistent pathwise estimators of θ.

Conclusion: Provides the first inference procedure for an SPDE whose solution is defined only pathwise, as a measurable function of a rough path, rather than as an Itô process.

Abstract: We consider the Burgers-type stochastic PDE \[
  du = θ\,\partial_x^2 u\,dt + f(u)\,dt + g(u)\,\partial_x u\,dt + σ\,dW(t), \] driven by space--time white noise on the one-dimensional torus, where \(θ>0\) is the diffusivity parameter and \(σ>0\) the noise intensity. The transport term \(g(u)\,\partial_x u\) admits no classical interpretation, and we work with the mild solutions defined via rough paths following~\cite{Hairer2011}, for which the solution decomposes as \(u=ψ+R\) with \(ψ\) a stationary centred Gaussian process solving a damped stochastic heat equation and \(R\) a remainder.
  We first show that, away from the initial time, \(R\) is Lipschitz in space and Hölder in time of order strictly greater than \(1/2\), hence strictly smoother than \(ψ\) in both variables. We then compute exactly the spatial quadratic variation and the temporal quartic variation of \(ψ\), almost surely and not merely in \(L^{2}(Ω)\), and prove that the corresponding variations of \(u\) are unaffected by the remainder. Combined with an exact time-scaling identity, this separation of regularity scales yields strongly consistent pathwise estimators of \(θ\). To our knowledge this is the first inference procedure for an SPDE whose solution is defined only pathwise, as a measurable function of a rough path, rather than as an Itô process.

</details>

### [155] [Enhanced Coulomb pseudopotentials in high-pressure hydrides from first principles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12563)
*Kieran Bozier,Chris J. Pickard*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: This paper computes the Coulomb parameter μ and pseudopotential μ*_ME from first principles for superconducting hydrides under pressure, showing that μ*_ME can be far larger than the conventional 0.1-0.15 range in systems with large density of states at the Fermi level.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Most first-principles predictions treat Coulomb repulsion with an empirical Coulomb pseudopotential μ*_ME (0.1-0.15), which may be inaccurate. The authors aim to compute μ and μ*_ME from first principles to check this assumption in superconducting hydrides.

Method: They performed first-principles calculations of μ and μ*_ME across pressures in superconducting hydrides, evaluating the screened Coulomb interaction at the random phase approximation level using the Mantel code.

Result: Hydrides such as H3S, YSbH6, and Mg2IrH6 show strong Coulomb repulsion driven by large N_F. For Mg2IrH6, μ=0.62 and μ*_ME≈0.39, causing a 40 K suppression of Tc versus μ*_ME=0.16. Including the energy dependence of the density of states resolves a factor-of-two discrepancy in previous Tc estimates. Across all systems, μ tracks N_F closely.

Conclusion: Commonly used μ*_ME values overestimate Tc in materials with large density of states at the Fermi energy, and μ*_ME can lie far above the conventional range.

Abstract: In the Eliashberg theory of superconductivity, Coulomb interactions oppose the phonon-mediated attraction and suppress the superconducting critical temperature $T_{\mathrm{c}}$. However, most first-principles predictions continue to treat this repulsion with an empirical Coulomb pseudopotential $μ^*_{\text{ME}}$ in the range 0.1-0.15. Here we present first-principles calculations of the Coulomb parameter $μ$ and pseudopotential $μ^*_{\text{ME}}$ in superconducting hydrides across a range of pressures. The screened Coulomb interaction is evaluated at the random phase approximation level with the Mantel code. We demonstrate that several hydride systems, including H$_3$S, YSbH$_6$ and Mg$_2$IrH$_6$, have strong Coulomb repulsions driven by a large density of states at the Fermi energy, $N_{\mathrm{F}}$. For Mg$_2$IrH$_6$ we obtain $μ=0.62$ and $μ^*_{\text{ME}}\approx0.39$, leading to a 40 K suppression in $T_{\mathrm{c}}$ relative to a standard $μ^*_{\text{ME}}=0.16$. Our obtained $μ^*_{\text{ME}}$ is far above the conventional range and, accounting for the energy dependence of the density of states, resolves the factor-of-two discrepancy between $T_{\mathrm{c}}$ estimates of previous studies. Across all systems and pressures studied, we find that $μ$ closely tracks $N_{\mathrm{F}}$, suggesting that commonly used $μ^*_{\text{ME}}$ values lead to overestimated $T_{\mathrm{c}}$ in materials where the density of states at the Fermi energy is large.

</details>


### [156] [Enhanced Coulomb pseudopotentials in high-pressure hydrides from first principles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12563)
*Kieran Bozier,Chris J. Pickard*

Main category: cond-mat.supr-con

TL;DR: First-principles calculations show that superconducting hydrides can have Coulomb pseudopotentials far above the conventional 0.1-0.15 range, leading to overestimated T_c when standard empirical values are used.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Most first-principles predictions treat the Coulomb repulsion with an empirical pseudopotential μ*_ME in the range 0.1-0.15, which may be inaccurate for certain materials and lead to incorrect T_c estimates.

Method: The authors computed the Coulomb parameter μ and pseudopotential μ*_ME from first principles for superconducting hydrides across pressures, evaluating the screened Coulomb interaction at the random phase approximation level using the Mantel code.

Result: Several hydrides (H3S, YSbH6, Mg2IrH6) show strong Coulomb repulsion driven by large density of states at the Fermi energy. For Mg2IrH6, μ=0.62 and μ*_ME≈0.39, causing a 40 K suppression in T_c versus μ*_ME=0.16, resolving a factor-of-two discrepancy between earlier studies.

Conclusion: μ closely tracks N_F across all studied systems and pressures, implying that commonly used μ*_ME values overestimate T_c in materials with large density of states at the Fermi energy.

Abstract: In the Eliashberg theory of superconductivity, Coulomb interactions oppose the phonon-mediated attraction and suppress the superconducting critical temperature $T_{\mathrm{c}}$. However, most first-principles predictions continue to treat this repulsion with an empirical Coulomb pseudopotential $μ^*_{\text{ME}}$ in the range 0.1-0.15. Here we present first-principles calculations of the Coulomb parameter $μ$ and pseudopotential $μ^*_{\text{ME}}$ in superconducting hydrides across a range of pressures. The screened Coulomb interaction is evaluated at the random phase approximation level with the Mantel code. We demonstrate that several hydride systems, including H$_3$S, YSbH$_6$ and Mg$_2$IrH$_6$, have strong Coulomb repulsions driven by a large density of states at the Fermi energy, $N_{\mathrm{F}}$. For Mg$_2$IrH$_6$ we obtain $μ=0.62$ and $μ^*_{\text{ME}}\approx0.39$, leading to a 40 K suppression in $T_{\mathrm{c}}$ relative to a standard $μ^*_{\text{ME}}=0.16$. Our obtained $μ^*_{\text{ME}}$ is far above the conventional range and, accounting for the energy dependence of the density of states, resolves the factor-of-two discrepancy between $T_{\mathrm{c}}$ estimates of previous studies. Across all systems and pressures studied, we find that $μ$ closely tracks $N_{\mathrm{F}}$, suggesting that commonly used $μ^*_{\text{ME}}$ values lead to overestimated $T_{\mathrm{c}}$ in materials where the density of states at the Fermi energy is large.

</details>

### [157] [Uniform Exponential Attractors for Non-Autonomous Dynamical Systems and an Application to the damped hyperbolic equation with sub-quintic growth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13068)
*Chang Qingquan,Li Dandan*

Main category: math.DS

TL;DR: The paper builds an abstract framework for uniform exponential attractors in non-autonomous dynamical systems and applies it to a weakly damped hyperbolic equation with sub-quintic nonlinearity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Non-autonomous dynamical systems need a rigorous theory of uniform exponential attractors that remain stable under perturbations, and concrete applications to damped hyperbolic equations require such a framework.

Method: An abstract framework using time-dependent absorbing sets while ensuring exponential attraction among constructed sets. For the application, exponential attractors are constructed for the process generated by the Shatah-Struwe solution satisfying energy and Strichartz estimates.

Result: The resulting exponential attractors are uniform, i.e., stable under perturbations. For the weakly damped hyperbolic equation with sub-quintic nonlinearity, exponential attractors are constructed, and their Holder continuity with respect to the external forcing term is established.

Conclusion: A general abstract theory of uniform exponential attractors is developed and successfully applied to a weakly damped hyperbolic equation, yielding uniform exponential attractors with Holder continuity in the forcing term.

Abstract: In this paper, we develop an abstract framework for the theory of uniform exponential attractors in non-autonomous dynamical systems. Our construction employs time-dependent absorbing sets while ensuring exponential attraction among the constructed sets. Moreover, we show that the resulting exponential attractors are uniform-that is, they remain stable under perturbations. As an application of this abstract theory, we study the weakly damped hyperbolic equation with sub-quintic nonlinearity. Using the proposed approach, we construct exponential attractors for the process generated by the Shatah-Struwe solution, which satisfies both energy and Strichartz estimates. Furthermore, we establish the Holder continuity of these exponential attractors with respect to the external forcing term.

</details>


### [158] [Uniform Exponential Attractors for Non-Autonomous Dynamical Systems and an Application to the damped hyperbolic equation with sub-quintic growth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13068)
*Chang Qingquan,Li Dandan*

Main category: math.DS

TL;DR: The paper builds an abstract framework for uniform exponential attractors in non-autonomous dynamical systems via time-dependent absorbing sets, and applies it to the weakly damped hyperbolic equation with sub-quintic nonlinearity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to extend the theory of exponential attractors to non-autonomous dynamical systems, ensuring uniformity and stability under perturbations, which is needed for analyzing time-dependent dissipative PDEs.

Method: They construct an abstract framework using time-dependent absorbing sets while ensuring exponential attraction among the constructed sets. They apply this to the weakly damped hyperbolic equation using the Shatah-Struwe solution satisfying energy and Strichartz estimates.

Result: The construction yields exponential attractors that are uniform (stable under perturbations). For the hyperbolic equation, exponential attractors are constructed for the process generated by the Shatah-Struwe solution, and Holder continuity with respect to the external forcing term is established.

Conclusion: The abstract theory provides a general method for uniform exponential attractors in non-autonomous systems, demonstrated successfully on the weakly damped hyperbolic equation, with regularity (Holder continuity) results.

Abstract: In this paper, we develop an abstract framework for the theory of uniform exponential attractors in non-autonomous dynamical systems. Our construction employs time-dependent absorbing sets while ensuring exponential attraction among the constructed sets. Moreover, we show that the resulting exponential attractors are uniform-that is, they remain stable under perturbations. As an application of this abstract theory, we study the weakly damped hyperbolic equation with sub-quintic nonlinearity. Using the proposed approach, we construct exponential attractors for the process generated by the Shatah-Struwe solution, which satisfies both energy and Strichartz estimates. Furthermore, we establish the Holder continuity of these exponential attractors with respect to the external forcing term.

</details>

# cond-mat.mtrl-sci [[Back]](#toc)

### [159] [VN-GraphGG: von Neumann--Mullins--based graph grain growth simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13418)
*Yuntong Huang,Zhihao Li,Shuyang Dai,Chang Ma,Yang Xiang*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: VN-GraphGG is a 2D grain growth simulation coupling von Neumann-Mullins kinetics on a graph representation with a GNN-based microstructure reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Efficient grain growth simulation is needed to study process-structure-property relationships in polycrystalline materials, but conventional methods like phase-field are slow.

Method: Represent microstructure as a graph with grain and junction nodes; evolve grain areas via the VN law on the grain graph; train a GNN to reconstruct microstructure from the graph. Kinetic coefficients can vary without retraining reconstruction; topological changes (grain disappearance, neighbor changes) handled via reconstruction and postprocessing.

Result: Accurate predictions of VN kinetics and coarsening statistics, with significant speedup over phase-field simulations. Application to abnormal grain growth shows kinetic heterogeneity strongly affects microstructure evolution.

Conclusion: Provides a foundation for efficient grain-scale-kinetics-based grain growth simulation, enabling incorporation of broader physical mechanisms.

Abstract: Efficient simulations of grain growth are essential for investigating the process-structure-property relationships in polycrystalline materials. We introduce a two-dimensional von Neumann-Mullins-based graph grain growth simulation (VN-GraphGG), which couples the grain-scale kinetics with microstructure reconstruction. The polycrystalline microstructure is represented as a graph with grain and junction nodes. The grain-area evolution on the grain graph is governed by the von Neumann--Mullins (VN) law, while a graph neural network is trained to reconstruct the microstructure from the graph. This separation allows kinetic coefficients to be varied without retraining the neural reconstruction model. Topological changes during grain growth such as disappearance of grains and changes in neighbor relations are handled straightforwardly through reconstruction and postprocessing. Simulations demonstrate accurate predictions of VN kinetics and coarsening statistics, and significant speedup over the phase-field simulations. The proposed method provides a foundation for efficient grain growth simulation based on grain-scale kinetics, opening the way to incorporate a broader range of physical mechanisms. An application example is presented for abnormal grain growth, in which kinetic heterogeneity significantly influences the grain microstructure evolution.

</details>


### [160] [Predicting Nanofabrication Variance: A Conditional Diffusion Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13420)
*Wenhan Zhang,Ben Mills,Oleksandr Buchnev,Kevin F. MacDonald*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: A conditional diffusion model learns the statistical distribution of FIB milling outcomes conditioned on input designs, enabling realistic prediction of fabrication variability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: FIB-fabricated micro/nanostructures deviate from designs due to systematic and stochastic effects (ablation-rate fluctuations, redeposition), so nominally identical processes yield distributions of non-identical outcomes that vary physical properties. Existing models predict only a single deterministic result, neglecting variability.

Method: A conditional diffusion model trained to learn the statistical distribution of process outcomes conditioned on the input design, producing a realistic distribution of possible output sample structures rather than one deterministic prediction.

Result: Predictions accurately reproduce edge morphology, linewidth variations, and defect probabilities observed in fabricated samples, enabling statistical analysis of milling performance and identification of dominant structural and process-parameter-dependent deformation modes.

Conclusion: The work establishes a probabilistic modelling framework for fabrication variability analysis and fabrication-aware design optimization.

Abstract: Micro- and nanostructures fabricated by focused ion beam (FIB) milling invariably deviate from input designs due to systematic and stochastic effects such as ablation-rate fluctuations, and redeposition. As such, nominally identical iterations of a given process yield a distribution of non-identical fabrication outcomes, leading to variations in the physical (optical, mechanical) properties of fabricated structures. Existing models of the milling process typically predict a single deterministic fabrication result, neglecting this variability. Here we present a conditional diffusion model that learns the statistical distribution of process outcomes conditioned on input design, enabling it to predict a realistic distribution of possible output sample structures. These predictions accurately reproduce edge morphology, linewidth variations, and defect probabilities observed in fabricated samples, enabling statistical analysis of milling performance and the identification of dominant structural and process parameter-dependent deformation modes. The work establishes a probabilistic modelling framework for fabrication variability analysis and fabrication-aware design optimization.

</details>


### [161] [VN-GraphGG: von Neumann--Mullins--based graph grain growth simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13418)
*Yuntong Huang,Zhihao Li,Shuyang Dai,Chang Ma,Yang Xiang*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: VN-GraphGG couples von Neumann-Mullins grain kinetics on a graph with a GNN-based microstructure reconstruction, achieving fast and accurate 2D grain growth simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Efficient grain growth simulation is essential for studying process-structure-property relationships in polycrystalline materials. Existing methods like phase-field are computationally expensive, and kinetic coefficients cannot be easily varied without retraining.

Method: Represents the polycrystalline microstructure as a graph with grain and junction nodes. Grain-area evolution follows the von Neumann-Mullins law on the grain graph, while a graph neural network reconstructs the microstructure from the graph. This separation decouples kinetics from reconstruction, so kinetic coefficients can be varied without retraining. Topological changes (grain disappearance, neighbor changes) are handled via reconstruction and postprocessing.

Result: Simulations accurately predict VN kinetics and coarsening statistics, with significant speedup over phase-field simulations. An abnormal grain growth example shows kinetic heterogeneity strongly influences microstructure evolution.

Conclusion: Provides a foundation for efficient grain growth simulation based on grain-scale kinetics, opening the way to incorporate broader physical mechanisms.

Abstract: Efficient simulations of grain growth are essential for investigating the process-structure-property relationships in polycrystalline materials. We introduce a two-dimensional von Neumann-Mullins-based graph grain growth simulation (VN-GraphGG), which couples the grain-scale kinetics with microstructure reconstruction. The polycrystalline microstructure is represented as a graph with grain and junction nodes. The grain-area evolution on the grain graph is governed by the von Neumann--Mullins (VN) law, while a graph neural network is trained to reconstruct the microstructure from the graph. This separation allows kinetic coefficients to be varied without retraining the neural reconstruction model. Topological changes during grain growth such as disappearance of grains and changes in neighbor relations are handled straightforwardly through reconstruction and postprocessing. Simulations demonstrate accurate predictions of VN kinetics and coarsening statistics, and significant speedup over the phase-field simulations. The proposed method provides a foundation for efficient grain growth simulation based on grain-scale kinetics, opening the way to incorporate a broader range of physical mechanisms. An application example is presented for abnormal grain growth, in which kinetic heterogeneity significantly influences the grain microstructure evolution.

</details>


### [162] [Predicting Nanofabrication Variance: A Conditional Diffusion Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13420)
*Wenhan Zhang,Ben Mills,Oleksandr Buchnev,Kevin F. MacDonald*

Main category: cond-mat.mtrl-sci

TL;DR: A conditional diffusion model predicts the statistical distribution of FIB milling outcomes conditioned on input design, capturing edge morphology, linewidth variations, and defect probabilities that deterministic models miss.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: FIB-fabricated micro/nanostructures deviate from input designs due to systematic and stochastic effects (ablation-rate fluctuations, redeposition). Nominally identical process iterations yield a distribution of non-identical outcomes, causing variations in optical/mechanical properties. Existing models only predict a single deterministic result, neglecting this variability.

Method: A conditional diffusion model that learns the statistical distribution of process outcomes conditioned on the input design, enabling prediction of a realistic distribution of possible output sample structures.

Result: Predictions accurately reproduce edge morphology, linewidth variations, and defect probabilities observed in fabricated samples, enabling statistical analysis of milling performance and identification of dominant structural and process parameter-dependent deformation modes.

Conclusion: The work establishes a probabilistic modeling framework for fabrication variability analysis and fabrication-aware design optimization.

Abstract: Micro- and nanostructures fabricated by focused ion beam (FIB) milling invariably deviate from input designs due to systematic and stochastic effects such as ablation-rate fluctuations, and redeposition. As such, nominally identical iterations of a given process yield a distribution of non-identical fabrication outcomes, leading to variations in the physical (optical, mechanical) properties of fabricated structures. Existing models of the milling process typically predict a single deterministic fabrication result, neglecting this variability. Here we present a conditional diffusion model that learns the statistical distribution of process outcomes conditioned on input design, enabling it to predict a realistic distribution of possible output sample structures. These predictions accurately reproduce edge morphology, linewidth variations, and defect probabilities observed in fabricated samples, enabling statistical analysis of milling performance and the identification of dominant structural and process parameter-dependent deformation modes. The work establishes a probabilistic modelling framework for fabrication variability analysis and fabrication-aware design optimization.

</details>
