# cs.LG [[Back]](#toc)

### [163] [Low-Rank Kolmogorov-Arnold Generator Flows for Interpretable Operator Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13028)
*Minjong Cheon*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: KAFlow learns physical operators as low-rank symbols built from univariate Kolmogorov-Arnold functions, with gauge fixing, exact exponential integrators, and automatic closed-form distillation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Neural operators should generalize beyond training and expose learned laws in closed form. The Kolmogorov-Arnold neural operator achieves this but uses a dense pseudo-differential symbol whose cost grows with resolution squared, and its closed forms must be extracted manually. This density is unnecessary since finite-order variable-coefficient differential operators have symbols whose separation rank is bounded by the number of coefficient functions.

Method: KAFlow learns the generator of a physical operator as a low-rank symbol composed of univariate Kolmogorov-Arnold functions. It fixes the gauge of these functions so each term has a unique place, integrates dynamics with exact exponential integrators, and automatically distills the closed form.

Result: On synthetic benchmarks, KAFlow lowers out-of-distribution loss by 3.4 to 11 times and recovers every coefficient to within 10^-4 without symbolic templates, reaching the data precision floor. On long-horizon quantum dynamics it lowers state infidelity by up to 6 orders of magnitude, and 30 times when trained only on position and momentum measurements. It learns a variable-order fractional operator 15 times more accurately than existing operator networks and discovers nonlocal, fractional, and non-polynomial dispersion relations where sparse regression fails.

Conclusion: Low-rank symbol learning with gauge fixing enables efficient, generalizable, and interpretable neural operators that recover closed-form physical laws and outperform dense-symbol and sparse-regression alternatives.

Abstract: Neural operators that learn physical laws from data should generalize beyond their training distribution and expose the learned law in closed form. The Kolmogorov-Arnold neural operator achieves both for position-dependent dynamics by learning a dense pseudo-differential symbol over all positions and frequencies, at a cost that grows with the square of the resolution and with closed forms that must be read out by hand. This dense symbol is unnecessary: a finite-order variable-coefficient differential operator has a symbol whose separation rank is bounded by the number of its coefficient functions, independently of the resolution, and the symbols that this network learns on its own benchmarks have rank two. We introduce the Kolmogorov-Arnold flow (KAFlow), which learns the generator of a physical operator as a low-rank symbol built from univariate Kolmogorov-Arnold functions, fixes the gauge of these functions so that every term has a unique place, integrates the dynamics with exact exponential integrators, and distills its closed form automatically. On the synthetic benchmarks of the Kolmogorov-Arnold neural operator, KAFlow lowers the out-of-distribution loss by 3.4 to 11 times and recovers every coefficient to within 10^-4 without symbolic templates, reaching the precision floor of the data. On long-horizon quantum dynamics, it lowers the state infidelity by up to 6 orders of magnitude, and by 30 times when trained only on position and momentum measurements. KAFlow also learns a variable-order fractional operator 15 times more accurately than existing operator networks and discovers nonlocal, fractional and non-polynomial dispersion relations on which sparse regression fails.

</details>


### [164] [Recovering Irreducible Three-Body Interactions with Lagrangian Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13292)
*Sena Kalabalik,Sukru Caglar,Abdullah Umut Hamzaogullari,Arkadas Ozakin*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Lagrangian Neural Networks can recover an irreducible three-body interaction (Axilrod-Teller) from trajectory data alone, including its closed-form law via symbolic regression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior LNN work only studied pairwise-additive interactions, leaving open whether LNNs can recover irreducible many-body interactions from motion alone.

Method: Prescribe kinetic energy exactly and learn only the potential as a function of interparticle distances and angles, combined with a signal-aware sampling strategy that concentrates data where the three-body signal is strongest while preserving broad configuration coverage.

Result: The learned potential reproduces the radial scaling, angular dependence, and sign reversal of the Axilrod-Teller interaction, and generates stable trajectories. Embedded in a Lennard-Jones potential, the three-body term is recovered when its dynamical signature exceeds residual modeling error (cleanly at amplified coupling, not at real noble-gas coupling). Symbolic regression recovers the exact Axilrod-Teller expression.

Conclusion: An LNN with domain-specific structure can recover an irreducible three-body interaction from trajectories as a closed-form law, pointing toward learning interaction laws whose structure is not known a priori.

Abstract: Lagrangian Neural Networks (LNNs) learn mechanical systems from trajectory data by parameterizing a scalar Lagrangian and deriving the dynamics through the Euler-Lagrange equations, biasing the model toward motion that respects the variational structure of mechanics. The interactions studied so far have been pairwise-additive, leaving open whether LNNs can recover an irreducible many-body interaction from motion alone. We address this using the Axilrod-Teller triple-dipole interaction, the leading three-body dispersion term, whose dependence on the collective geometry of three particles cannot be represented by any sum of pairwise terms. We prescribe the kinetic energy exactly and learn only the potential, as a function of interparticle distances and angles. Combined with a signal-aware sampling strategy that concentrates training data where the three-body signal is strongest while preserving broad coverage of configuration space, this recovers the interaction from accelerations alone. The learned potential reproduces the radial scaling, angular dependence, and characteristic sign reversal of the Axilrod-Teller interaction, and generates stable trajectories. Embedding it within a pairwise Lennard-Jones potential lets us examine its observability: the three-body term is recovered once its dynamical signature exceeds the residual modeling error, which it does cleanly at amplified coupling but not at the coupling of a real noble gas, bracketing the transition between the two. Finally, symbolic regression on the trained potential, with no assumed functional form, returns the Axilrod-Teller expression with angular numerator, radial law, and coupling constant intact. An LNN with domain-specific structure can thus recover an irreducible three-body interaction from trajectories as a closed-form law, pointing toward learning interaction laws whose structure is not known a priori.

</details>


### [165] [Sustainable Cloud-Fog-Edge Systems for Smart Buildings: Algorithmic Adaptation under Memory Constraints and Dynamic Orchestration Strategies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13307)
*Mamadou Sow,Christophe Cerin*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: The paper proposes a holistic edge-computing architecture for smart buildings, combining resilient IoT data acquisition, a SLAs orchestration framework (KOptim), and an adapted GHA/PCA algorithm validated on low-power microcontrollers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The ICT sector's growing environmental footprint (data centers could reach 3% of global electricity by 2030) and the need for real-time responsiveness in smart buildings require moving away from centralized computing toward edge computing, treating the building itself as the data center.

Method: Three pillars: 1) resilient data acquisition chains using affordable Arduino (Uno R4 WiFi/BME280) with secure persistence (Node-RED/MySQL, Python/rsync/SSH); 2) a lightweight KOptim orchestration framework interoperable with FIWARE for dynamic SLA management; 3) adaptation and experimental validation of the Generalized Hebbian Algorithm (GHA) for PCA on low-power microcontrollers, with an iterative buffered learning strategy that sorts and preserves extreme samples for diversity.

Result: Experiments on high-dimensional Tour Perret sensor data show the novel buffered method, despite O(W log W) temporal complexity from sorting, achieves convergence and final result quality visually equivalent to full in-memory dataset processing, reliably identifying two distinct operational states.

Conclusion: The proposed edge-based, privacy-preserving learning system enables autonomous, resilient smart buildings, demonstrating that lightweight on-device PCA is feasible while matching full-dataset quality.

Abstract: The escalating environmental footprint of the Information and Communication Technol- ogy (ICT) sectorwith global Data Center electric- ity usage potentially reaching 3% by 2030 and the imperative for real-time responsiveness in smart buildings demand a foundational shift towards Edge Computing. This article proposes a holis- tic architectural and algorithmic approach for cre- ating autonomous, resilient, and privacy-preserving learning systems within smart buildings, advancing the core concept of the building itself as the data center. Our contributions, demonstrating originality compared to conventional centralized systems, are structured around three main pillars: 1) Validation of resilient data acquisition chains using the af- fordable Arduino ecosystem (Uno R4 WiFi/BME280) and secure persistence solutions including centralized archiving (Node-RED/MySQL) and secure remote transfer (Python/rsync/SSH). 2). The proposal of a lightweight, KOptim orchestration framework, inter- operable with the FIWARE standard, for the dynamic management of Service Level Agreements (SLA). 3) The adaptation and experimental valida- tion of the Generalized Hebbian Algorithm (GHA) for Principal Component Analysis (PCA) on low- power microcontrollers,We introduce and compare an iterative learning strategy using data buffers, featuring sorting and explicit preservation of extreme samples to maintain diversity. Experimental results on the high-dimensional Tour Perret sensor data confirm that this novel method, despite introducing a necessary temporal complexity of O(W log W ) due to sorting, achieves convergence and final result quality that is visually equivalent to processing the entire dataset in full memory, reliably identifying two distinct operational states.

</details>


### [166] [Low-Rank Kolmogorov-Arnold Generator Flows for Interpretable Operator Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13028)
*Minjong Cheon*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: KAFlow learns a physical operator's generator as a low-rank symbol from univariate Kolmogorov-Arnold functions, enabling generalization and automatic closed-form extraction, outperforming the Kolmogorov-Arnold neural operator across benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Neural operators should generalize beyond training data and expose learned laws in closed form. The Kolmogorov-Arnold neural operator achieves this for position-dependent dynamics but uses a dense pseudo-differential symbol whose cost grows with resolution squared, and its closed forms must be read out manually. Dense symbols are unnecessary because finite-order variable-coefficient differential operators have symbols with separation rank bounded by coefficient count, independent of resolution—and the learned symbols have rank two.

Method: KAFlow learns the generator of a physical operator as a low-rank symbol built from univariate Kolmogorov-Arnold functions. It fixes the gauge of these functions so each term has a unique place, integrates dynamics with exact exponential integrators, and automatically distills the closed form.

Result: On synthetic benchmarks, KAFlow lowers out-of-distribution loss by 3.4–11× and recovers every coefficient to within 1e-4 without symbolic templates (data precision floor). On long-horizon quantum dynamics, it lowers state infidelity by up to 6 orders of magnitude, and 30× when trained only on position and momentum measurements. It learns a variable-order fractional operator 15× more accurately than existing operator networks and discovers nonlocal, fractional, and non-polynomial dispersion relations where sparse regression fails.

Conclusion: KAFlow demonstrates that low-rank symbolic generator learning with gauge fixing and exact integration yields strong out-of-distribution generalization, precise closed-form recovery, and improved accuracy on challenging fractional and nonlocal operator discovery tasks.

Abstract: Neural operators that learn physical laws from data should generalize beyond their training distribution and expose the learned law in closed form. The Kolmogorov-Arnold neural operator achieves both for position-dependent dynamics by learning a dense pseudo-differential symbol over all positions and frequencies, at a cost that grows with the square of the resolution and with closed forms that must be read out by hand. This dense symbol is unnecessary: a finite-order variable-coefficient differential operator has a symbol whose separation rank is bounded by the number of its coefficient functions, independently of the resolution, and the symbols that this network learns on its own benchmarks have rank two. We introduce the Kolmogorov-Arnold flow (KAFlow), which learns the generator of a physical operator as a low-rank symbol built from univariate Kolmogorov-Arnold functions, fixes the gauge of these functions so that every term has a unique place, integrates the dynamics with exact exponential integrators, and distills its closed form automatically. On the synthetic benchmarks of the Kolmogorov-Arnold neural operator, KAFlow lowers the out-of-distribution loss by 3.4 to 11 times and recovers every coefficient to within 10^-4 without symbolic templates, reaching the precision floor of the data. On long-horizon quantum dynamics, it lowers the state infidelity by up to 6 orders of magnitude, and by 30 times when trained only on position and momentum measurements. KAFlow also learns a variable-order fractional operator 15 times more accurately than existing operator networks and discovers nonlocal, fractional and non-polynomial dispersion relations on which sparse regression fails.

</details>


### [167] [Recovering Irreducible Three-Body Interactions with Lagrangian Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13292)
*Sena Kalabalik,Sukru Caglar,Abdullah Umut Hamzaogullari,Arkadas Ozakin*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Lagrangian Neural Networks can recover an irreducible three-body Axilrod-Teller interaction from trajectory data alone, including its closed-form symbolic expression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior LNN studies only handled pairwise-additive interactions; it was unknown whether they could recover an irreducible many-body interaction that cannot be decomposed into pairwise sums.

Method: Prescribe kinetic energy exactly and learn only the potential as a function of interparticle distances and angles, using signal-aware sampling that concentrates data where the three-body signal is strongest while preserving configuration-space coverage.

Result: The learned potential reproduces the Axilrod-Teller radial scaling, angular dependence, and sign reversal, and yields stable trajectories. Observability emerges once the dynamical signature exceeds residual error (clean at amplified coupling, not at real noble-gas coupling). Symbolic regression recovers the exact Axilrod-Teller expression with angular numerator, radial law, and coupling constant.

Conclusion: An LNN with domain-specific structure can recover an irreducible three-body interaction from trajectories as a closed-form law, pointing toward learning interaction laws whose structure is not known a priori.

Abstract: Lagrangian Neural Networks (LNNs) learn mechanical systems from trajectory data by parameterizing a scalar Lagrangian and deriving the dynamics through the Euler-Lagrange equations, biasing the model toward motion that respects the variational structure of mechanics. The interactions studied so far have been pairwise-additive, leaving open whether LNNs can recover an irreducible many-body interaction from motion alone. We address this using the Axilrod-Teller triple-dipole interaction, the leading three-body dispersion term, whose dependence on the collective geometry of three particles cannot be represented by any sum of pairwise terms. We prescribe the kinetic energy exactly and learn only the potential, as a function of interparticle distances and angles. Combined with a signal-aware sampling strategy that concentrates training data where the three-body signal is strongest while preserving broad coverage of configuration space, this recovers the interaction from accelerations alone. The learned potential reproduces the radial scaling, angular dependence, and characteristic sign reversal of the Axilrod-Teller interaction, and generates stable trajectories. Embedding it within a pairwise Lennard-Jones potential lets us examine its observability: the three-body term is recovered once its dynamical signature exceeds the residual modeling error, which it does cleanly at amplified coupling but not at the coupling of a real noble gas, bracketing the transition between the two. Finally, symbolic regression on the trained potential, with no assumed functional form, returns the Axilrod-Teller expression with angular numerator, radial law, and coupling constant intact. An LNN with domain-specific structure can thus recover an irreducible three-body interaction from trajectories as a closed-form law, pointing toward learning interaction laws whose structure is not known a priori.

</details>


### [168] [Sustainable Cloud-Fog-Edge Systems for Smart Buildings: Algorithmic Adaptation under Memory Constraints and Dynamic Orchestration Strategies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13307)
*Mamadou Sow,Christophe Cerin*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Proposes a holistic edge-computing architecture for smart buildings, treating the building itself as a data center, with three pillars: resilient Arduino-based data acquisition, a lightweight KOptim SLA orchestration framework (FIWARE-interoperable), and an adapted Generalized Hebbian Algorithm for PCA on low-power microcontrollers using buffered iterative learning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: ICT's growing environmental footprint (data center electricity potentially reaching 3% by 2030) plus the need for real-time responsiveness in smart buildings require a shift from centralized to edge computing, motivating autonomous, resilient, and privacy-preserving learning systems inside buildings.

Method: Three-pillar approach: (1) resilient data acquisition using Arduino Uno R4 WiFi/BME280 with secure persistence (Node-RED/MySQL central archiving, Python/rsync/SSH remote transfer); (2) a lightweight KOptim orchestration framework for dynamic SLA management, interoperable with FIWARE; (3) adaptation of the Generalized Hebbian Algorithm (GHA) for PCA on low-power microcontrollers, with an iterative learning strategy using data buffers that sort and explicitly preserve extreme samples for diversity.

Result: Experiments on high-dimensional Tour Perret sensor data show the buffered method converges with final quality visually equivalent to full in-memory processing, reliably identifying two distinct operational states—despite adding O(W log W) temporal complexity from sorting.

Conclusion: A holistic edge-computing design can enable autonomous, resilient, privacy-preserving learning within smart buildings, with the building acting as its own data center; the buffered GHA-PCA approach is viable on constrained microcontrollers for real-time state identification.

Abstract: The escalating environmental footprint of the Information and Communication Technol- ogy (ICT) sectorwith global Data Center electric- ity usage potentially reaching 3% by 2030 and the imperative for real-time responsiveness in smart buildings demand a foundational shift towards Edge Computing. This article proposes a holis- tic architectural and algorithmic approach for cre- ating autonomous, resilient, and privacy-preserving learning systems within smart buildings, advancing the core concept of the building itself as the data center. Our contributions, demonstrating originality compared to conventional centralized systems, are structured around three main pillars: 1) Validation of resilient data acquisition chains using the af- fordable Arduino ecosystem (Uno R4 WiFi/BME280) and secure persistence solutions including centralized archiving (Node-RED/MySQL) and secure remote transfer (Python/rsync/SSH). 2). The proposal of a lightweight, KOptim orchestration framework, inter- operable with the FIWARE standard, for the dynamic management of Service Level Agreements (SLA). 3) The adaptation and experimental valida- tion of the Generalized Hebbian Algorithm (GHA) for Principal Component Analysis (PCA) on low- power microcontrollers,We introduce and compare an iterative learning strategy using data buffers, featuring sorting and explicit preservation of extreme samples to maintain diversity. Experimental results on the high-dimensional Tour Perret sensor data confirm that this novel method, despite introducing a necessary temporal complexity of O(W log W ) due to sorting, achieves convergence and final result quality that is visually equivalent to processing the entire dataset in full memory, reliably identifying two distinct operational states.

</details>

### [169] [Homogeneous to multi-scale inhomogeneous complex magnetism in Ba3LnRu2O9 (Ln = Gd, Ho), Revealed by muSR and Machine-Learned Muon-Site Calculations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12726)
*S. Ghosh,K. Sharma,E. Kushwaha,M. Kumar,G. Roy,A. Pandey,F. L. Pratt,S. Cottrell,D. T. Adroja,T. Basu*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: A muSR study of 6H-perovskites Ba3LnRu2O9 (Ln=Gd, Ho) shows rare-earth anisotropy controls whether the 4d-4f magnetic ground state is homogeneous (Gd, S-state) or complex and multi-scale inhomogeneous (Ho).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how rare-earth anisotropy governs the magnetic ground state in 4d-4f coupled systems, exploiting the contrast between Ho3+ (strong anisotropy) and Gd3+ (L=0, isotropic S-state).

Method: Comprehensive muon spin rotation/relaxation (ZF and LF muSR) on hexagonal 6H-perovskites Ba3HoRu2O9 and Ba3GdRu2O9, combined with Redfield analysis and machine-learning-assisted muon-site and dipolar-field calculations.

Result: Ba3HoRu2O9 shows two orderings at TN1=46 K and TN2=10 K, with two-component exponential relaxation, broad local-field distributions, incomplete LF decoupling, and persistent spin dynamics; Ba3GdRu2O9 shows single ordering at TN=14.5 K with Gaussian relaxation and a narrow static field distribution. ML-assisted calculations attribute the broad field distribution to muons sensing distinct coexisting magnetic domains at different structural sites.

Conclusion: The rare-earth ion is a powerful tuning parameter, driving the ground state from homogeneous antiferromagnetic (Gd) to complex, multi-scale inhomogeneous behavior (Ho) via competing 4d-4f magnetic interactions.

Abstract: We report a comprehensive muon spin rotation and relaxation (muSR) study of the hexagonal 6H-perovskites Ba3LnRu2O9 (Ln = Gd, Ho) to investigate the role of rare-earth anisotropy in the magnetic ground state of 4d-4f coupled systems. Ba3HoRu2O9 exhibits two successive magnetic orderings at TN1 = 46 K and TN2 = 10 K, leading to a complex magnetic ground state, whereas Ba3GdRu2O9, with Gd3+ (L = 0) S-state ions, shows primarily single magnetic ordering at TN = 14.5 K. Zero-field (ZF) and longitudinal-field (LF) muSR measurements reveal markedly different magnetic behavior despite their structural similarity. Ba3HoRu2O9 displays a multi-scale inhomogeneous magnetic state characterized by two-component exponential relaxation, broad local-field distributions, incomplete LF decoupling, and persistent spin dynamics. The muSR results identify two characteristic magnetic energy scales near 46 and 10 K, associated with correlated Ru-Ho interactions and quasistatic magnetic ordering, respectively. Redfield analysis further demonstrates strong local magnetic fields and distributed spin fluctuation timescales, confirming magnetic inhomogeneity. Machine-learning-assisted muon-site and dipolar-field calculations show that the broad field distribution naturally arises from muons sensing distinct coexisting magnetic domains at different structural sites. In contrast, Ba3GdRu2O9 exhibits a comparatively homogeneous antiferromagnetic ground state below 14.5 K, with Gaussian relaxation consistent with a narrow static field distribution. These results establish that the rare-earth ion in Ba3LnRu2O9 is a powerful tuning parameter, driving the ground state from homogeneous to complex, multi-scale inhomogeneous behavior through competing 4d-4f magnetic interactions.

</details>


### [170] [Homogeneous to multi-scale inhomogeneous complex magnetism in Ba3LnRu2O9 (Ln = Gd, Ho), Revealed by muSR and Machine-Learned Muon-Site Calculations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12726)
*S. Ghosh,K. Sharma,E. Kushwaha,M. Kumar,G. Roy,A. Pandey,F. L. Pratt,S. Cottrell,D. T. Adroja,T. Basu*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: muSR study of hexagonal 6H-perovskites Ba3LnRu2O9 (Ln = Gd, Ho) shows rare-earth anisotropy controls the magnetic ground state: Ho compounds exhibit complex, multi-scale inhomogeneous magnetism, while Gd compounds show homogeneous antiferromagnetic order.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the role of rare-earth anisotropy in the magnetic ground state of 4d-4f coupled systems, using the structurally similar Ba3LnRu2O9 family as a platform.

Method: Zero-field and longitudinal-field muon spin rotation/relaxation (muSR) measurements, Redfield analysis, and machine-learning-assisted muon-site and dipolar-field calculations.

Result: Ba3HoRu2O9 shows two magnetic orderings (TN1 = 46 K, TN2 = 10 K) with two-component exponential relaxation, broad local-field distributions, incomplete LF decoupling, and persistent spin dynamics. Ba3GdRu2O9 orders at TN = 14.5 K with Gaussian relaxation and a narrow static field distribution. Calculations show broad field distribution arises from muons sensing coexisting magnetic domains at distinct structural sites.

Conclusion: The rare-earth ion is a powerful tuning parameter that drives the ground state from homogeneous (Gd) to complex, multi-scale inhomogeneous behavior (Ho) via competing 4d-4f magnetic interactions.

Abstract: We report a comprehensive muon spin rotation and relaxation (muSR) study of the hexagonal 6H-perovskites Ba3LnRu2O9 (Ln = Gd, Ho) to investigate the role of rare-earth anisotropy in the magnetic ground state of 4d-4f coupled systems. Ba3HoRu2O9 exhibits two successive magnetic orderings at TN1 = 46 K and TN2 = 10 K, leading to a complex magnetic ground state, whereas Ba3GdRu2O9, with Gd3+ (L = 0) S-state ions, shows primarily single magnetic ordering at TN = 14.5 K. Zero-field (ZF) and longitudinal-field (LF) muSR measurements reveal markedly different magnetic behavior despite their structural similarity. Ba3HoRu2O9 displays a multi-scale inhomogeneous magnetic state characterized by two-component exponential relaxation, broad local-field distributions, incomplete LF decoupling, and persistent spin dynamics. The muSR results identify two characteristic magnetic energy scales near 46 and 10 K, associated with correlated Ru-Ho interactions and quasistatic magnetic ordering, respectively. Redfield analysis further demonstrates strong local magnetic fields and distributed spin fluctuation timescales, confirming magnetic inhomogeneity. Machine-learning-assisted muon-site and dipolar-field calculations show that the broad field distribution naturally arises from muons sensing distinct coexisting magnetic domains at different structural sites. In contrast, Ba3GdRu2O9 exhibits a comparatively homogeneous antiferromagnetic ground state below 14.5 K, with Gaussian relaxation consistent with a narrow static field distribution. These results establish that the rare-earth ion in Ba3LnRu2O9 is a powerful tuning parameter, driving the ground state from homogeneous to complex, multi-scale inhomogeneous behavior through competing 4d-4f magnetic interactions.

</details>

### [171] [Evaluating LLMs for Translating Legacy Fortran Atmospheric Physics to GPU-Accelerated JAX](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12662)
*Iris Linck,John Dennis,John Schreck,Karen Stengel,David John Gagne*

Main category: physics.ao-ph

TL;DR: LAFT is an agentic LLM framework that translates legacy Fortran atmospheric physics into GPU-accelerated JAX while preserving numerical agreement, validated on Kessler microphysics across four LLMs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Legacy Fortran physics packages in models like CESM, WRF, and E3SM hinder GPU modernization, and manual porting demands scarce accelerator-programming expertise.

Method: LAFT coordinates source-code analysis, translation, repair, runtime validation, and performance profiling. It uses a deterministic front end for metadata extraction and bridge generation, structured translation prompts, numerical validation, and profiling-guided feedback. A bridge separates array-layout conversion from scientific computation.

Result: Tested on Kessler warm-rain microphysics with Claude, GPT, Gemini, and Qwen. All four validated translations matched Fortran outputs to near floating-point precision, but GPU performance varied widely. Gemini's implementation reached up to 357x speedup over serial Fortran at 1,000,000 columns (cached execution, excluding compilation and host-device transfers).

Conclusion: Numerical agreement alone does not ensure efficient GPU execution, motivating combining numerical validation with hardware-aware performance analysis. LAFT offers a practical workflow for modernizing legacy atmospheric physics code via LLM-assisted translation, deterministic interface generation, automated validation/repair, and reproducible profiling.

Abstract: Legacy Fortran physics packages in atmospheric models such as CESM, WRF, and E3SM pose a barrier to GPU modernization, while manual porting requires accelerator-programming expertise. We present LAFT (LLM-Assisted Fortran Translation), an agentic framework for converting Fortran atmospheric physics parameterizations into GPU-accelerated JAX while preserving numerical agreement with the Fortran reference. LAFT coordinates source-code analysis, translation, repair, runtime validation, and performance profiling. It combines a deterministic front end for metadata extraction and bridge generation, structured translation prompts, numerical validation, and profiling-guided feedback to identify inefficient execution patterns. The bridge handles array-layout conversion separately from scientific computation. We evaluate LAFT using the Kessler warm-rain microphysics scheme and four LLMs: Claude, GPT, Gemini, and Qwen. All four validated translations reproduce the Fortran reference outputs to near floating-point precision for the evaluated case, but their GPU performance differs substantially with execution design. The Gemini-generated implementation achieves up to a 357-fold speedup over serial Fortran in cached execution at 1,000,000 atmospheric columns, excluding compilation and host-device transfers. The results demonstrate that numerical agreement alone does not ensure efficient GPU execution and motivate combining numerical validation with hardware-aware performance analysis. LAFT provides a practical workflow for modernizing legacy atmospheric physics code through LLM-assisted translation, deterministic interface generation, automated validation and repair, and reproducible profiling.

</details>


### [172] [Evaluating LLMs for Translating Legacy Fortran Atmospheric Physics to GPU-Accelerated JAX](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12662)
*Iris Linck,John Dennis,John Schreck,Karen Stengel,David John Gagne*

Main category: physics.ao-ph

TL;DR: LAFT is an LLM-assisted agentic framework that translates legacy Fortran atmospheric physics code into GPU-accelerated JAX while preserving numerical agreement, validated on the Kessler microphysics scheme across four LLMs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Legacy Fortran physics packages in atmospheric models (CESM, WRF, E3SM) hinder GPU modernization, and manual porting requires accelerator-programming expertise.

Method: LAFT coordinates source-code analysis, translation, repair, runtime validation, and performance profiling. It uses a deterministic front end for metadata extraction and bridge generation, structured translation prompts, numerical validation, and profiling-guided feedback. A bridge separates array-layout conversion from scientific computation.

Result: Evaluated on the Kessler warm-rain microphysics scheme with Claude, GPT, Gemini, and Qwen: all four translations matched Fortran outputs to near floating-point precision, but GPU performance varied. Gemini's implementation achieved up to a 357-fold speedup over serial Fortran at 1,000,000 columns (cached execution, excluding compilation and host-device transfers).

Conclusion: Numerical agreement alone does not ensure efficient GPU execution; combining numerical validation with hardware-aware performance analysis is essential. LAFT offers a practical workflow for modernizing legacy atmospheric physics code.

Abstract: Legacy Fortran physics packages in atmospheric models such as CESM, WRF, and E3SM pose a barrier to GPU modernization, while manual porting requires accelerator-programming expertise. We present LAFT (LLM-Assisted Fortran Translation), an agentic framework for converting Fortran atmospheric physics parameterizations into GPU-accelerated JAX while preserving numerical agreement with the Fortran reference. LAFT coordinates source-code analysis, translation, repair, runtime validation, and performance profiling. It combines a deterministic front end for metadata extraction and bridge generation, structured translation prompts, numerical validation, and profiling-guided feedback to identify inefficient execution patterns. The bridge handles array-layout conversion separately from scientific computation. We evaluate LAFT using the Kessler warm-rain microphysics scheme and four LLMs: Claude, GPT, Gemini, and Qwen. All four validated translations reproduce the Fortran reference outputs to near floating-point precision for the evaluated case, but their GPU performance differs substantially with execution design. The Gemini-generated implementation achieves up to a 357-fold speedup over serial Fortran in cached execution at 1,000,000 atmospheric columns, excluding compilation and host-device transfers. The results demonstrate that numerical agreement alone does not ensure efficient GPU execution and motivate combining numerical validation with hardware-aware performance analysis. LAFT provides a practical workflow for modernizing legacy atmospheric physics code through LLM-assisted translation, deterministic interface generation, automated validation and repair, and reproducible profiling.

</details>

### [173] [Sharp lower bound of the first eigenvalue of the $p$-Laplacian under integral Ricci curvature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12852)
*Shoo Seto*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: A sharp Zhong-Yang type lower bound is proved for the first nonzero eigenvalue of the p-Laplacian on closed manifolds with small integral Ricci curvature for p ≥ 2, combining a global elliptic estimate via Sobolev truncation with strict perturbations of one-dimensional comparison profiles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Zhong-Yang type lower bounds for the first nonzero eigenvalue are classical for the Laplacian under curvature conditions; extending such sharp bounds to the p-Laplacian (p ≥ 2) under small integral Ricci curvature is the goal, as this weaker curvature condition and nonlinear operator were previously less understood.

Method: The proof combines a global elliptic estimate obtained through Sobolev truncation with strict perturbations of one-dimensional comparison profiles.

Result: A sharp Zhong-Yang type lower bound is established for the first nonzero eigenvalue of the p-Laplacian on closed manifolds with small integral Ricci curvature, for p ≥ 2.

Conclusion: The p-Laplacian eigenvalue admits a sharp Zhong-Yang type lower bound under small integral Ricci curvature, confirming the expected optimal behavior via the Sobolev truncation and comparison-profile perturbation technique.

Abstract: We prove a sharp Zhong-Yang type lower bound for the first nonzero eigenvalue of the $p$-Laplacian on closed manifolds with small integral Ricci curvature for $p \geq 2$. The proof combines a global elliptic estimate obtained by Sobolev truncation with strict perturbations of one-dimensional comparison profiles.

</details>


### [174] [Recovering a Riemannian manifold from minimal surface areas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13405)
*Catalin Carstea,Matti Lassas,Tony Liimatainen,Leo Tzou*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: Areas of minimal surfaces determine the metric on manifolds foliated by minimal surfaces up to boundary-fixing isometry, resolving a boundary rigidity conjecture for least-area data, and global rigidity for Einstein metrics on compact 3-manifolds with boundary holds under π₁(M,∂M)=0 and a non-trapping condition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper addresses a conjecture on boundary rigidity for least-area data in an exterior formulation, aiming to recover a 3D Riemannian manifold from minimal surface areas, including without topological restrictions on the minimal surfaces.

Method: Uses the areas of minimal surfaces to reconstruct the metric on manifolds foliated by minimal surfaces, showing determination up to boundary-fixing isometry; also applies these techniques to prove global rigidity for Einstein metrics under π₁(M,∂M)=0 and a non-trapping condition.

Result: Areas of minimal surfaces determine the metric up to boundary-fixing isometry for foliated manifolds, resolving the exterior boundary rigidity conjecture for least-area data without topological restrictions; global rigidity holds for Einstein metrics on compact 3-manifolds with boundary under the stated conditions.

Conclusion: Minimal surface areas suffice to determine geometry in the studied settings, settling the conjecture and extending rigidity to Einstein metrics on compact 3-manifolds with boundary, though the abstract is truncated before fully describing the Einstein result.

Abstract: We study the recovery of a three-dimensional Riemannian manifold from the areas of minimal surfaces. We show that areas of minimal surfaces determine the metric on manifolds foliated by minimal surfaces, up to boundary-fixing isometry. This resolves the conjecture on boundary rigidity for least-area data in an exterior formulation, even without topological restrictions on the minimal surfaces. We also prove global rigidity for Einstein metrics on compact three-manifolds with boundary, assuming $\(π_1(M,\partial M)=0\)$ and a non-trapping condition. The Einstein result includes the

</details>


### [175] [Topping's conjecture for solutions of harmonic map flow with isolated bubbles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13746)
*Melanie Rupflin,Alex Waldron,Sebastian Woodward*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: By proving a weighted Lojasiewicz-Simon inequality near a harmonic map modulo scaling, the paper resolves Topping's conjecture on body-map continuity for finite-time singularities with only isolated bubbles.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Topping's conjecture concerns the continuity of the body map in harmonic map heat flow at finite-time singularities. Understanding body-map behavior is key to analyzing singularity formation, especially when singularities involve only isolated bubbles.

Method: The authors establish a weighted Lojasiewicz-Simon inequality for maps close to a fixed harmonic map modulo scaling, then apply it to control the body map near the singular time.

Result: Topping's conjecture on continuity of the body map is proven in the case of finite-time singularities where only isolated bubbles form.

Conclusion: The weighted Lojasiewicz-Simon inequality provides an effective tool for resolving body-map continuity, confirming Topping's conjecture under the isolated-bubble assumption.

Abstract: By establishing a weighted Lojasiewicz-Simon inequality for maps which are close to a fixed harmonic map modulo scaling, we prove Topping's conjecture on continuity of the body map in the case of finite time singularities at which only isolated bubbles form.

</details>


### [176] [The Bernstein problem in higher dimensional Heisenberg groups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13913)
*Gianmarco Giovannardi,Simone Verzellesi*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: In any sub-Riemannian Heisenberg group H^n (n≥2), there exists a global perimeter minimizer whose boundary is a smooth, non-characteristic, entire intrinsic graph, yet it is not a vertical hyperplane.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding perimeter minimizers and minimal surfaces in sub-Riemannian Heisenberg groups is a central problem. It was natural to expect that global minimizers with such regularity might necessarily be vertical hyperplanes, motivating a search for counterexamples.

Method: Construct, within the sub-Riemannian geometry of H^n, a global perimeter minimizer whose boundary is a smooth non-characteristic entire intrinsic graph, and verify it is distinct from any vertical hyperplane.

Result: A global perimeter minimizer is proven to exist for every n≥2 with the stated properties: smooth, non-characteristic, entire intrinsic graph, and not a vertical hyperplane.

Conclusion: There exist global perimeter minimizers in Heisenberg groups beyond the trivial vertical hyperplane case, showing richer structure for minimal surfaces in sub-Riemannian settings.

Abstract: In every sub-Riemannian Heisenberg group $\mathbb H^n$, with $n\geq 2$, we prove the existence of a global perimeter minimizer whose boundary is a smooth, non-characteristic, entire intrinsic graph but it is not a vertical hyperplane.

</details>


### [177] [Sharp lower bound of the first eigenvalue of the $p$-Laplacian under integral Ricci curvature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12852)
*Shoo Seto*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper proves a sharp Zhong-Yang type lower bound for the first nonzero eigenvalue of the p-Laplacian on closed manifolds with small integral Ricci curvature, for p ≥ 2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extend Zhong-Yang type eigenvalue lower bounds to the p-Laplacian (p ≥ 2) under weak integral Ricci curvature conditions, generalizing classical pointwise curvature assumptions.

Method: Combines a global elliptic estimate obtained via Sobolev truncation with strict perturbations of one-dimensional comparison profiles.

Result: A sharp Zhong-Yang type lower bound is established for the first nonzero eigenvalue of the p-Laplacian on closed manifolds with small integral Ricci curvature, p ≥ 2.

Conclusion: Sharp eigenvalue lower bounds hold for the p-Laplacian under weak (integral) Ricci curvature conditions, extending classical Zhong-Yang results.

Abstract: We prove a sharp Zhong-Yang type lower bound for the first nonzero eigenvalue of the $p$-Laplacian on closed manifolds with small integral Ricci curvature for $p \geq 2$. The proof combines a global elliptic estimate obtained by Sobolev truncation with strict perturbations of one-dimensional comparison profiles.

</details>


### [178] [Recovering a Riemannian manifold from minimal surface areas](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13405)
*Catalin Carstea,Matti Lassas,Tony Liimatainen,Leo Tzou*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: Areas of minimal surfaces determine a Riemannian metric on 3-manifolds foliated by minimal surfaces, up to boundary-fixing isometry, resolving the least-area boundary rigidity conjecture; global rigidity also holds for Einstein metrics under topological and non-trapping conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper addresses the boundary rigidity problem for least-area data: whether the areas of minimal surfaces encode the metric of a 3D Riemannian manifold, a conjecture studied via an exterior formulation.

Method: Uses the areas of minimal surfaces as measurement data; derives metric recovery on manifolds foliated by minimal surfaces without topological restrictions on the minimal surfaces.

Result: Shows areas of minimal surfaces determine the metric up to boundary-fixing isometry; proves global rigidity for Einstein metrics on compact 3-manifolds with boundary assuming pi_1(M,∂M)=0 and a non-trapping condition.

Conclusion: Resolves the least-area boundary rigidity conjecture in an exterior formulation and establishes global rigidity for Einstein 3-manifolds with boundary under stated hypotheses.

Abstract: We study the recovery of a three-dimensional Riemannian manifold from the areas of minimal surfaces. We show that areas of minimal surfaces determine the metric on manifolds foliated by minimal surfaces, up to boundary-fixing isometry. This resolves the conjecture on boundary rigidity for least-area data in an exterior formulation, even without topological restrictions on the minimal surfaces. We also prove global rigidity for Einstein metrics on compact three-manifolds with boundary, assuming $\(π_1(M,\partial M)=0\)$ and a non-trapping condition. The Einstein result includes the

</details>


### [179] [Topping's conjecture for solutions of harmonic map flow with isolated bubbles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13746)
*Melanie Rupflin,Alex Waldron,Sebastian Woodward*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper proves Topping's conjecture on the continuity of the body map for finite-time singularities of harmonic map heat flow where only isolated bubbles form, using a weighted Lojasiewicz-Simon inequality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the structure of finite-time singularities in harmonic map heat flow is important; Topping's conjecture concerns whether the body (non-bubbling) map remains continuous at singular times when only isolated bubbles develop.

Method: Establish a weighted Lojasiewicz-Simon inequality for maps close to a fixed harmonic map modulo scaling, then apply it to control the behavior of the flow near the singularity.

Result: Topping's conjecture is confirmed for finite-time singularities with only isolated bubbles.

Conclusion: The weighted Lojasiewicz-Simon inequality yields continuity of the body map, resolving Topping's conjecture in the isolated-bubble case.

Abstract: By establishing a weighted Lojasiewicz-Simon inequality for maps which are close to a fixed harmonic map modulo scaling, we prove Topping's conjecture on continuity of the body map in the case of finite time singularities at which only isolated bubbles form.

</details>


### [180] [The Bernstein problem in higher dimensional Heisenberg groups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13913)
*Gianmarco Giovannardi,Simone Verzellesi*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: In every sub-Riemannian Heisenberg group H^n (n≥2), the paper proves existence of a global perimeter minimizer whose boundary is a smooth, non-characteristic, entire intrinsic graph that is not a vertical hyperplane.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding global perimeter minimizers in sub-Riemannian Heisenberg groups is central to geometric measure theory in this setting, where classical Euclidean intuition often fails. The paper addresses the open question of whether non-trivial examples of such minimizers exist.

Method: Construction and proof of existence of a global minimizer of the sub-Riemannian perimeter whose boundary is an entire intrinsic graph, smooth and non-characteristic, using the intrinsic geometry of H^n.

Result: For every H^n with n≥2, a global perimeter minimizer is shown to exist. Its boundary is a smooth, non-characteristic, entire intrinsic graph, and it is not a vertical hyperplane.

Conclusion: The results establish the existence of novel global perimeter minimizers in Heisenberg groups, showing that minimizers need not be vertical hyperplanes, thus enriching the landscape of sub-Riemannian minimal surfaces.

Abstract: In every sub-Riemannian Heisenberg group $\mathbb H^n$, with $n\geq 2$, we prove the existence of a global perimeter minimizer whose boundary is a smooth, non-characteristic, entire intrinsic graph but it is not a vertical hyperplane.

</details>

### [181] [Mesh-Native Tokenisation: Retrofitting Foundation Models to Unstructured Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12618)
*Georg Maierhofer,Miles Cranmer,Payel Mukhopadhyay*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: The paper introduces Mesh-Native Convolution (MNC), a locality-aware tokenisation layer enabling physics foundation models to process unstructured meshes like CNNs on grids. MNC allows models trained on grid data to generalize to arbitrary unstructured meshes with minimal retraining, providing an efficient bridge between the two data regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Physics foundation models rely on tokenisation layers (typically CNNs) that work well on grid/field data but cannot handle unstructured meshes (e.g., curved boundaries), which require expensive GNNs that encode information differently. This limits emulator portability between grid and mesh regimes.

Method: MNC is a locality-aware tokenisation layer that encodes unstructured mesh data analogously to CNNs. It enables models to be trained once on mesh data and deployed to arbitrary, even irregular, unstructured meshes with minimal retraining. The approach can retrofit grid-pretrained foundation models onto unstructured meshes.

Result: The paper provides theoretical results confirming consistency of MNC with classical CNN encoders. Empirically, the retrofitting approach reaches acceptable accuracy in many cases even in zero-shot deployment and outperforms existing baselines after only a few epochs of fine-tuning.

Conclusion: MNC offers an efficient bridge between grid and unstructured mesh data regimes, enabling grid-pretrained physics foundation models to be retrofitted onto unstructured meshes both cheaply and effectively.

Abstract: Physics foundation models are an increasingly popular approach for building versatile emulators for spatiotemporal systems. They require tokenisation layers that lift physical field data into a latent representation suitable for dynamic processing. A typical choice for this layer is a convolutional encoder, which lifts field information in a locality-aware way and can be trained very efficiently. However, this choice cannot be applied to a large class of scientific computing problems where data are represented on unstructured meshes, for example simulations on domains with curved boundaries. Such data typically require graph neural network architectures, which are more expensive to train and, in the form currently used, encode information differently from their CNN counterparts, thus limiting the portability of physics emulators between these data regimes. In this work we introduce Mesh-Native Convolution (MNC), a locality-aware tokenisation layer that encodes unstructured mesh-based data in a way that is analogous to CNNs. This approach leads to models that can be trained once on mesh data and then deployed to arbitrary unstructured meshes, even if these are irregular, with minimal retraining. We show that this tokenisation approach can be used to retrofit grid-pretrained foundation models efficiently on unstructured meshes and provide theoretical results to confirm the consistency of this approach with classical CNN encoders. We demonstrate the favourable performance of this retrofitting approach, which in many cases reaches acceptable accuracy even in zero-shot deployment, and outperforms existing baselines after only a few epochs of fine-tuning, thus providing an efficient bridge between the two data regimes.

</details>


### [182] [Mesh-Native Tokenisation: Retrofitting Foundation Models to Unstructured Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12618)
*Georg Maierhofer,Miles Cranmer,Payel Mukhopadhyay*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: The paper introduces Mesh-Native Convolution (MNC), a locality-aware tokenisation layer that encodes unstructured mesh data analogously to CNNs, enabling physics foundation models to be retrofitted from grid-pretrained models and deployed to arbitrary unstructured meshes with minimal retraining.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Physics foundation models typically use convolutional encoders, but these cannot handle unstructured meshes (e.g., curved boundaries) which require expensive graph neural networks. This limits the portability of physics emulators between grid and mesh data regimes.

Method: MNC is a locality-aware tokenisation layer that encodes unstructured mesh-based data in a CNN-analogous way. The authors provide theoretical results confirming consistency with classical CNN encoders and use it to retrofit grid-pretrained foundation models onto unstructured meshes.

Result: Models trained once on mesh data can be deployed to arbitrary unstructured (even irregular) meshes with minimal retraining. Retrofitting reaches acceptable accuracy in many cases even under zero-shot deployment and outperforms existing baselines after only a few epochs of fine-tuning.

Conclusion: MNC provides an efficient bridge between grid and mesh data regimes, enabling grid-pretrained physics foundation models to be adapted to unstructured meshes cheaply and effectively.

Abstract: Physics foundation models are an increasingly popular approach for building versatile emulators for spatiotemporal systems. They require tokenisation layers that lift physical field data into a latent representation suitable for dynamic processing. A typical choice for this layer is a convolutional encoder, which lifts field information in a locality-aware way and can be trained very efficiently. However, this choice cannot be applied to a large class of scientific computing problems where data are represented on unstructured meshes, for example simulations on domains with curved boundaries. Such data typically require graph neural network architectures, which are more expensive to train and, in the form currently used, encode information differently from their CNN counterparts, thus limiting the portability of physics emulators between these data regimes. In this work we introduce Mesh-Native Convolution (MNC), a locality-aware tokenisation layer that encodes unstructured mesh-based data in a way that is analogous to CNNs. This approach leads to models that can be trained once on mesh data and then deployed to arbitrary unstructured meshes, even if these are irregular, with minimal retraining. We show that this tokenisation approach can be used to retrofit grid-pretrained foundation models efficiently on unstructured meshes and provide theoretical results to confirm the consistency of this approach with classical CNN encoders. We demonstrate the favourable performance of this retrofitting approach, which in many cases reaches acceptable accuracy even in zero-shot deployment, and outperforms existing baselines after only a few epochs of fine-tuning, thus providing an efficient bridge between the two data regimes.

</details>

# cond-mat.other [[Back]](#toc)

### [183] [Ultrafast laser-stimulated field emission from a metal: a non-equilibrium Green's functions approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13251)
*Luciano Jacopo D'Onofrio,Adolfo Avella,Martin Eckstein*

Main category: cond-mat.other

TL;DR: This paper develops a non-equilibrium Green's function framework to model ultrafast laser-assisted field emission from metallic nanotips, incorporating electron-electron correlations and dynamic back-action effects that reshape the far-field spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing theoretical models of ultrafast laser-assisted field emission from metallic nanotips often neglect many-body strong correlation effects in the emitter's electronic structure and its response to the optical field.

Method: A theoretical framework based on the non-equilibrium Green's function formalism for out-of-equilibrium electron dynamics across a strongly driven matter-vacuum interface, using a one-dimensional model. The tip-vacuum coupling is treated perturbatively. Electron-electron interactions are included via the second-Born approximation, and the Dyson equation is solved self-consistently at the interface.

Result: The interplay between strong-field and multi-photon emission regimes is described as a filtering of the emitter's local density of occupied states through field-dressed vacuum propagators. Correlation-induced modifications in the electronic structure are directly transferred to the far-field spectrum, and a dynamic back-action regime with periodic polarization of the emitter reshapes the far-field spectrum.

Conclusion: The non-equilibrium Green's functions approach provides a versatile framework for exploring and analyzing field emission of correlated electrons.

Abstract: Ultrafast laser-assisted field emission from metallic nanotips is a promising route for generating coherent electron pulses. However, existing theoretical models often neglect many-body strong correlation effects in the emitter's electronic structure and its response to the optical field. In this work, we propose a theoretical framework based on the non-equilibrium Green's function formalism to simulate the out-of-equilibrium electron dynamics across a strongly driven matter-vacuum interface, in a one-dimensional model. Treating the tip-vacuum coupling perturbatively, we find that the interplay between strong-field emission and multi-photon emission regimes can be described in terms of a filtering of the emitter's local density of occupied states through field-dressed vacuum propagators. By including electron-electron interactions via the second-Born approximation, we show that correlation-induced modifications in the electronic structure are directly transferred to the far-field spectrum. Finally, by self-consistently solving the Dyson equation at the interface, we uncover a dynamic back-action regime driven by the optical field, characterized by a periodic polarization of the emitter, which reshapes the far-field spectrum. This shows that the non-equilibrium Green's functions approach provides a versatile framework for exploring and analyzing field emission of correlated electrons.

</details>

*Luciano Jacopo D'Onofrio,Adolfo Avella,Martin Eckstein*

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>

Correlation-induced modifications in the electronic structure transfer directly to the far-field spectrum. A dynamic back-action regime driven by the optical field emerges, featuring periodic polarization of the emitter that reshapes the far-field spectrum.

Abstract: Ultrafast laser-assisted field emission from metallic nanotips is a promising route for generating coherent electron pulses. However, existing theoretical models often neglect many-body strong correlation effects in the emitter's electronic structure and its response to the optical field. In this work, we propose a theoretical framework based on the non-equilibrium Green's function formalism to simulate the out-of-equilibrium electron dynamics across a strongly driven matter-vacuum interface, in a one-dimensional model. Treating the tip-vacuum coupling perturbatively, we find that the interplay between strong-field emission and multi-photon emission regimes can be described in terms of a filtering of the emitter's local density of occupied states through field-dressed vacuum propagators. By including electron-electron interactions via the second-Born approximation, we show that correlation-induced modifications in the electronic structure are directly transferred to the far-field spectrum. Finally, by self-consistently solving the Dyson equation at the interface, we uncover a dynamic back-action regime driven by the optical field, characterized by a periodic polarization of the emitter, which reshapes the far-field spectrum. This shows that the non-equilibrium Green's functions approach provides a versatile framework for exploring and analyzing field emission of correlated electrons.

</details>
